## 2.3.1.1 The re-baseline's tables

Made by the one-time re-baseline of 2026-10-08 (Rule 5.10; the decision: 2.1.1.35). Old numbers in git history before it resolve here. The proposal (2.3.1.2) shows the plan as approved; the notes were then placed by part (owner, 2026-10-08), so where the two differ, these tables are what was done.

**Subchapters.** A subchapter that moved whole keeps its topics: old `V.C.S.T` is new `<new>.T`. A subchapter that became a topic of a notes subchapter: old `V.C.S.T` is new `<new>.T`, one level deeper.

## 2.3.1.2 Proposal: the one-time re-baseline of the manual

*Written before the re-baseline of 2026-10-08: the manual numbers, volume numbers and decision IDs in this topic are the old ones, kept as written; 2.3.1.1 maps them.*

### 2.3.1.2.1 Why

The global rules changed on 2026-10-07 (engineering-rules `d276cda`, `0f4a453`, `0adc51a`): the manual has **four
volumes** in reading order â 1 description and architecture (old Volumes 1 and 2 together: each part's design with the
technologies it uses), 2 engineering decisions (decisions 2.1, troubleshooting 2.2, notes 2.3: old Volume 5),
3 installation (old Volume 4), 4 operations (old Volume 3); Volumes 2-4 follow Volume 1's chapters, so one number leads
to a part's design, its decisions, troubleshooting and notes, its installation and its operation (Rule 5.9); a
decision's identity is its topic number (Rule 13). A manual written before may be renumbered once (Rule 5.10). The
owner agreed to the re-baseline, the subchapter and topic layout of the decisions, the spine below, and moving each
part's design into its Volume 1 chapter (2026-10-07).

### 2.3.1.2.2 The spine: Volume 1's chapters

Today Volume 1 has no chapter for most of fabric's parts: DNS, certificates, DHCP, 802.1X, the DNS filter, time, logs
and disk encryption are designed in Volume 2 or as subchapters of *Architecture*. Alignment needs one Volume 1
chapter per part. The spine (agreed 2026-10-07), each part's design and its technology detail together in its chapter:

| New | Chapter | From today's |
|---|---|---|
| 1.1 | **The product, and how it is built and released**: what fabric is, where it came from, phases, the roadmap, code conventions, what every change must keep working, releases and versions | 1.1, 1.10, parts of 4.7 and 4.9 |
| 1.2 | **Architecture and the host**: topology and request flows, repository and installed layout, containers and their limits, targets, the privileges inventory, security defaults, host changes and consent | 1.3 (less DNS and PKI), 1.9, design parts of 2.7 |
| 1.3 | **fabricctl, setup and fabric-agent** | 1.4 |
| 1.4 | **DNS: BIND** (zones, records, TSIG, ACLs, reverse DNS, the landing page's links) | 1.3.5 |
| 1.5 | **Certificates: Step-CA** (the PKI chain, issuing, ACME, site CAs) | 1.3.6 |
| 1.6 | **Identity: the directory and sign-in** (Samba AD, Keycloak, sign-in layers, people, joining machines) | 1.6, 2.10 and 2.11 design parts |
| 1.7 | **Secrets: OpenBao** | 1.7 |
| 1.8 | **The web UI** | 1.5 |
| 1.9 | **Federation** | 1.8 |
| 1.10 | **DHCP: Kea** | design parts of 2.2 |
| 1.11 | **802.1X: FreeRADIUS** | design parts of 2.3 |
| 1.12 | **The DNS filter: AdGuard Home** | design parts of 2.4 |
| 1.13 | **Time: chrony** | design parts of 2.5 |
| 1.14 | **Container images** (channels, publishing, updates) | design parts of 2.6 |
| 1.15 | **Log forwarding: Fluent Bit** | design parts of 2.8 |
| 1.16 | **Disk encryption** | design parts of 2.9 |
| 1.17 | **The function reference** (generated from the code) | 1.11 |

Today's 1.2 (the decision register) moves to 2.1 and is deleted from Volume 1.

### 2.3.1.2.3 How the other volumes follow it

- **Volume 1:** today's Volume 1 and Volume 2 folded into the spine â a part's settings (today's 2.1 settings
  reference) go to its chapter; third-party licences (2.12) to 1.1; host changes and consent (2.7) to 1.2.
- **Volume 2 (engineering decisions):** 2.1 decisions (2.1.C per chapter, one decision per topic); 2.2
  troubleshooting (today's 5.6 â 2.2.6 and 2.2.8); 2.3 notes â 2.3.1 the re-baseline's tables, and every other note
  under the chapter it concerns (alignment history, the stale-code register, review findings, blockers, reasoning,
  environments, the design proposals: Samba replacing 389-DS â 2.3.6 and 2.3.9, sign-in â 2.3.6).
- **Volume 3 (installation, upgrades, builds):** today's Volume 4 â installation, reinstall and restore â 3.2 (the
  host) and 3.3 (setup); joining an existing fabric â 3.9; the subordinate CA â 3.5; disk encryption â 3.16; images,
  packaging, testing and development â 3.1 (how fabric is built and released) and 3.14; stubs elsewhere.
- **Volume 4 (operations):** today's Volume 3 â fabricctl â 4.3, lifecycle and ports â 4.2, DNS â 4.4, certificates
  â 4.5, people, the directory and joins â 4.6, OpenBao â 4.7, the web UI â 4.8, federation â 4.9, DHCP â 4.10,
  802.1X â 4.11, the DNS filter â 4.12, time â 4.13, images â 4.14, logs â 4.15; stubs at 4.1, 4.16, 4.17.

The full file-by-file table (164 files today) is 5.8.3.8; the decisions by chapter, 5.8.3.9.

### 2.3.1.2.4 The decisions

About 120 decisions today, in seven files and three numbering schemes (D1âD121, F1âF10, J1âJ3, S1âS6). Each becomes a
topic `2.1.C.T` under the chapter it shapes most, written out with: the decision, the chapters it shapes, its date and
who made it, its reasoning (from today's register rows and Volume 5's reasoning), and its status (in force, revised
by, replaced by, removed â the history today's rows carry as "Revised 2026-10-05 â¦"). Then every reference across the
repository is replaced (whole IDs only: `D13`, never inside `D113`; ranges such as "D106âD111" written out), the old
register deleted, and a D/F/J/S-number-to-topic table kept in 2.3.1 for old commits.

### 2.3.1.2.5 Impact assessment

- **Architecture:** none. Documents only; no behaviour changes.
- **Breaking changes:** every manual number changes, once, and the volume folders are renamed. Things that cite them change in
  the same change: links inside the manual; `manual X.Y` references in code docstrings, comments, templates and
  messages printed to users; the tests that check the manual (the docs suite, the function reference generator and
  its number file, the review ledger); `AGENTS.md`. Old numbers stay in git history; 2.3.1's tables resolve them.
- **Scope creep risk:** large but mechanical. The risk is losing or misplacing content. Guarded by Rule 9's baseline:
  before and after, every old item is accounted for (a new place, or a removal note), every link resolves, the docs
  and lint suites pass, and no code behaviour changes (the code changes are comment and message text only; the unit
  and render suites run before and after).
- **Effort:** the largest documentation change so far â a full day of work or more, done on its own branch
  (`manual-rebaseline`) and merged after 0.6.2 so production does not wait on it.

### 2.3.1.2.6 How it is done

1. The full mapping table written for review (the spine is agreed).
2. Volume 1 built to the spine from today's Volumes 1 and 2; Volumes 2, 3 and 4 filled from today's 5, 4 and 3 in
   their aligned places, stubs where nothing exists.
3. Each decision written at its topic, with date, reason and status.
4. Every reference replaced (manual numbers and decision IDs), the old register deleted.
5. The baseline check: every old file and topic mapped, links resolve, the suites pass.
6. One pull request, reviewed by the owner.

### 2.3.1.2.7 For the owner to decide

Agreed 2026-10-07: the spine (5.8.3.2), and moving each part's design into its Volume 1 chapter. For approval: the
file-by-file mapping (5.8.3.8) and the decisions by chapter (5.8.3.9); nothing moves before.

### 2.3.1.2.8 The file-by-file mapping

Every one of today's 164 subchapter files, and where it goes. A number of the form `2.3.C.T` means the file
becomes a topic inside that subchapter (its own topics become `2.3.C.T.x`). Subchapters keep their topics and
titles; only the numbers change. The volume folders become `volume_1_description_and_architecture`,
`volume_2_engineering_decisions`, `volume_3_installation` and `volume_4_operations`; `assets/review-ledger.tsv`
moves to Volume 2's `assets/`.

## 2.3.1.3 Aligning fabric to the global working rules: the plan

*Written before the re-baseline of 2026-10-08: the manual numbers, volume numbers and decision IDs in this topic are the old ones, kept as written; 2.3.1.1 maps them.*

### 2.3.1.3.1 Why

The owner's global working rules (Rules 1â13, from `github.com/archdukejim/engineering-rules`) make a 5-volume
manual under `docs/` the only source of truth, settle per-project choices in a decision register (Rule 13,
chapter 1.2), and fix the repository layout (Rule 7). fabric predates them: its documentation is 51 flat files
(guides, `design/`, a generated function reference, `maintenance/`), its rules live in `AGENTS.md`, and its code
sits in `fabricctl/`, `webui/` and `installers/deb/`. Owner decision 2026-10-03: align everything in one branch,
`feature/manual-alignment`, before any new feature work (the Samba work waits).

### 2.3.1.3.2 Scope

Approved by the owner on 2026-10-03 (Rule 5.5, after the impact assessment in 5.1.1.6):

1. The manual: master index, five volumes, every existing document moved into its volume by aspect (5.8),
   numbered (5.7); the decision register (1.2) with the checklist answers (Rule 13).
2. Every reference to the old documents in code, tests, templates and tools rewritten to manual numbers; the docs
   checks in `tests/docs/` and the generated function reference (`gen_lib_doc.py`) writing into the manual; the
   in-browser manual (nginx `/manual/`) reading `docs/README.md` instead of fixed tabs.
3. `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` cut down to pointers into the manual (Rule 7.1); facts held in the agent's memory
   moved into the manual (5.2) and the memory trimmed.
4. The repository layout of Rule 7: `src/` (with `src/ux/cli` and `src/ux/web`, decision A2), `templates/`,
   `static/` (vendored libraries recorded in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES`), `config/`, `packaging/`, `scripts/`,
   `examples/`; the root README under 1000 words.
5. Container image publishing (decision C5): fabric's own images built in CI from `packaging/` with one build
   matrix, multi-arch, published and pinned by digest; hosts pull instead of building. This changes existing
   designs (D9, D10, D21), so it gets its own design chapter and impact assessment before it is built.
6. Lint (decision B3): ruff and shellcheck in CI and in the definition of done.

### 2.3.1.3.3 Order of work

Each step is its own commit (or a few), checked before the next:

1. Baseline (Rule 9): the installed tree with hashes, the rendered templates, the generated reference, every test
   suite's results â captured at commit `b64302c` before any change (5.1.1.6).
2. This skeleton: master index, volume indexes, this plan, the decision register (1.2).
3. The documents, in one generated move: most old documents span several volumes, so moving a volume at a time
   would leave broken links between commits. A one-off tool moves them by a line-exact mapping, renumbers headings,
   rewrites links and `Â§` references, and the coverage check of 5.1.1.6 proves the result; the map of old
   sections to manual numbers is 5.1.2.
4. References in code and tests, the docs tools, the in-browser manual.
5. Agent files and memory.
6. Rule 7 layout (moves only, behaviour unchanged), then lint.
7. C5 image publishing (after its design chapter is approved).
8. Baseline comparison, every suite, the sandbox. (`HANDOFF.md`, the session handoff, was removed early on
   2026-10-03 at the owner's request: it named a test machine's address in this public repository.)

### 2.3.1.3.6 How the move is checked

- **Baseline** (Rule 9), captured before any change at commit `b64302c`, kept outside the repository: the
  installed tree from `installers/deb/assemble-tree.sh` with a SHA-256 per file (784 files besides `docs/`), the
  templates rendered by `tests/render.py`, the generated function reference, and every suite's PASS/FAIL lines
  (`tests/run-all.sh`, all suites; the sandbox separately). One known baseline failure: the docs suite's "every
  file has been reviewed", caused by `HANDOFF.md` (not in the review ledger).
- **Documents:** a coverage check proves every line of every old document lands in exactly one manual file
  (headings renumbered, nothing else changed) before the old file is removed.
- **Code moves (Rule 7):** the installed tree outside `docs/` and the rendered templates must hash identically
  to the baseline; the test results must match.
- **Impact** (presented and approved 2026-10-03): no product design change in steps 1â6; breaking for the
  in-browser manual, about 187 references in 106 code and test files, the docs tools, and the package build.
  Scope creep risk: medium, held down by keeping the document move mechanical.

- **While documents move:** the master index (`docs/README.md`) carries a marked temporary list of the documents
  not yet moved, so every file stays indexed; Rule 5.6 allows only volumes and chapters there, so the list is
  removed when the last document has moved. (Removed 2026-10-03: the function reference became 1.11 and the review ledger moved to Volume 5's
  `assets/`.)

### 2.3.1.3.7 Not in scope

- Rewording, merging or correcting documents during the move (after it, as normal changes).
- New features. The Samba work (2.11) resumes after this branch.
- Text that is data on running installs keeps its old wording: the `fabricNetwork` schema's description in
  `05-schema.ldif` still names `dhcp-management.md` (now 2.2.2, 5.1.2), because a schema definition is never
  changed in place (seed.py adds it only when its identifier is missing).

## 2.3.1.6 Review findings (2026-09-30)

### 2.3.1.6.1 About this review


Found while every file was reviewed against the code (the docs suite and
`review-ledger.tsv`). Documentation problems were fixed during the review;
what is listed here needs a code change or an owner decision. Mark items
done in the same change that fixes them ([1.1.5.5](../volume_1_description_and_architecture/1.1.5-code-conventions.md#1155-stale-code) applies to the DEAD ones).

Paths are relative to `src/` unless they start with another top-level folder (`templates/`, `packaging/`,
`tests/`, â¦); they were rewritten to the repository's layout of 2026-10-03 (manual 2.3.2.1).

### 2.3.1.6.2 P1 â security, fix first

| # | Where | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| S1 | `deploy.py:425-469` | Everything is rendered into a fixed `/tmp/fabric-render` with the default umask and left there: TSIG/rndc keys, every LDAP role password, the web UI's OIDC secret, compose files with passwords, `rfc2136.ini` â world-readable. A local user can also pre-create the folder and get root (systemd units are copied from it). Only the web UI path is shielded (fabric-agent's PrivateTmp). Fix: `mkdtemp` under umask 077, removed after the deploy. |
| S2 | `templates/nginx/www/certs/*.sh.j2`, `www/ldap/install-ldap.sh.j2`, `nginx.conf.j2:36` | The CA trust anchor and root-run scripts are fetched over plain HTTP and piped into `sudo bash`, with no fingerprint check: anyone on the LAN can install their own root CA or run code as root. Fix: bake the root SHA-256 into each script and refuse on mismatch; "download, check, run" instead of a pipe. |
| S3 | `templates/nginx/nginx.conf.j2:171, 282` | `proxy_ssl_verify off` to Step-CA and Keycloak ([1.2.8](../volume_1_description_and_architecture/1.2.8-security-defaults.md#128-security-defaults)). |
| S4 | `setup/backup_install.py`, `cli.py` (reinstall) | `reinstall` leaves `/root/fabric-reinstall-<time>/` forever: plaintext secrets, CA keys, the vault key. |
| S5 | `setup/uninstall.py:311-313` | Uninstall deletes `/opt/<name>` for every TSIG key name: a key named `containerd` wipes an unrelated `/opt/containerd`. |
| S6 | `templates/dirsrv/seed/00-config.ldif.j2:18`, `30-aci.ldif.j2:9-10` | Anonymous clients can list every user, group, device and role, and who is an admin; `targetattr!=` exposes any attribute added later. |
| S7 | `templates/keycloak/docker-compose.yml.j2:31-32` | `KC_PROXY_HEADERS` without trusted addresses (any container can spoof X-Forwarded-*); an unused plaintext listener on 8080. |
| S8 | `templates/bind9/config/named.conf.options.j2`, nginx DoH | DoH queries arrive from nginx's IP, which is in the resolver ACL: anyone reaching 443 can query every zone; all DoH clients share one RRL bucket. |
| S9 | `rbac/required_permission.py` (`POST /v1/apply`) | Apply needs only `dns:write` but runs the whole deployment. |
| S10 | `src/fabriclib/deploy/keep_old_install.py` (and its callers `setup/upgrade_vars`, `deploy/apply_deployment`, `tests/render.py`) | Keeps what an install from before 0.7.0 needs at its upgrade: its DC's name (the host's, 2.1.6.45) and its uid/gid numbering (2.1.6.46). The owner says no install from before 0.7 is working (2.1.6.51), so nothing reaches it; the DC-name part now makes a setting the `member` step refuses. | An upgrade from 0.6.x, should one turn up. | Delete with the decisions it serves (2.1.6.45's and 2.1.6.46's "existing installs keep"), on the owner's word. | 2026-10-12 | done â deleted 2026-10-13 on `dev/0.8.0-ipam`, commit "Delete keep_old_install: no install from before 0.7.0 exists (S10, 2.1.6.51)": the owner's word of 2026-10-12 ("not applicable no one has a working 0.7", 2.1.6.51); 2.1.6.45 and 2.1.6.46 revised. Its callers' calls, its test in `tests/render.py` and its mentions went with it; `samba/id_range` and `setup/upgrade_vars` stay (they serve every install). (This row sits in 2.3.1.6.2 by mistake; its home is 2.3.1.5.1.) |
| (S10 above) | â | *Not a security finding: the stale-code row above was filed in this table by mistake on 2026-10-12. Its home is the stale-code register, 2.3.1.5.1, row S10 (done 2026-10-13); the security finding numbered S10 is the next row.* |
| S10 | compose files (dirsrv, keycloak, postgres) | Passwords in `environment:` (visible to `docker inspect`); Fluent Bit already uses a 0600 env file. |

### 2.3.1.6.3 P2 â bugs that break things people will hit

| # | Where | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| B1 | `vault/rotate_vault_key.py:43,60-73` | An interrupted rotation leaves `previous_key_id`; every later rotation is refused forever. |
| B2 | `setup/check_export_dir.py:182` | The export-folder check misses folders uninstall deletes (TSIG key folders, `acme_*`, data dirs outside the base): an export there is deleted with the install. |
| B3 | `setup/run_restore_command.py:37-38` | Restore forwards `--deploy-base` without its value and passes `--no-color`, which setup rejects. |
| B4 | `pki/mint_extra_cert.py`, `setup/mint_extra_certs.py` | A missing `extra_certs` file is re-minted with a new key on every setup â a subordinate CA is silently re-keyed; a missing `out_dir` fails the certs step. |
| B5 | `templates/vars.yaml.j2` + `logs/deploy_fluentbit.py:49` | Settings not emitted by the template are dropped after the first apply (`ldap_password_min_length`, lockout, `webui_session_*`, `dirsrv_errorlog_level`, `fluentbit_journal`/`extra_files`, `cname_radius`); Fluent Bit is rendered without them at all. |
| B6 | `interactive.py` `apply_mode` | Apply restarts services a second time, `fabric-web` synchronously (can drop the web UI's own request); a failed restart is a traceback. |
| B7 | `dns/remove_acl_entries.py:32` | Removing a key from an ACL comes back on the next apply when the key lists that ACL. |
| B8 | `dns/set_key_acls.py:18` | `!key "x"` is treated as membership. |
| B9 | `dhcp/normalize_dhcp.py:61` | A bad router address crashes apply with a traceback. |
| B10 | `templates/kea/kea-dhcp4.conf.j2:39` | Kea subnet ids are list positions: reordering subnets misattributes leases. |
| B11 | `templates/nginx/www/ldap/index.html.j2:53`, `install-certs.sh.j2:102`, `www/manual/index.html.j2:27` | Broken links: the LDAP install script (404), `certificates.<domain>` (does not exist), `lib-doc.md` (now `lib-doc/`). |
| B12 | `setup/run_setup.py:57` | Only `SetupError` is caught: ValidationError/CalledProcessError from several steps end in tracebacks. Same for `vault rotate`, `radius -n`, a few CLI options. |
| B13 | `templates/openbao/openbao.hcl.j2:39-54` | OpenBao's audit logs are never rotated (grow on the Pi's disk). |
| B14 | `templates/nginx/nginx.conf.j2:145-173` | Step-CA's vhost terminates TLS: mTLS renew/rekey cannot work through `ca.<domain>:443`. |
| B15 | `templates/bind9/data/zone.j2:18,27` | A domain zone without `zone_authority` gets `NS ns.<domain>` with no address for it. |
| B16 | `templates/webui-app/base.html` (Audit log link), `src/webui/routes/stepca_page.py` | The Audit log link shows to everyone (403 for most); custom bundles with `pki:sign` but no `devices:read` get 403 on sign/issue. |
| B17 | `cli.py:93` | `fabricctl --help` refuses non-root users. |
| B18 | `templates/systemd/fabric-agent.service.j2` | `IPAddressDeny=any` likely blocks adding a KMIP HSM on the LAN from the web UI (only tested against a local PyKMIP). |

### 2.3.1.6.4 P3 â AGENTS.md rules not met

| # | Where | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | compose templates | No memory limit by default (bind9, dirsrv, nginx, step-ca, keycloak, postgres, fabric-web) â only under some `host_ram_capacity` values (Â§5). |
| R2 | `system/service_status.py`, vars `security` | Relaxations (firewall off, `firewall_allow`, Docker hardening off, `message_authenticator: false`) are not shown by `fabricctl status` or the dashboard (Â§4); `vars.yaml.j2` says they are. |
| R3 | local image builds | `apt-get install` of unpinned packages (and Kea's key fetched) at build time; no offline or mirror path ([1.2.8](../volume_1_description_and_architecture/1.2.8-security-defaults.md#128-security-defaults), [1.1.6](../volume_1_description_and_architecture/1.1.6-must-keep-working.md#116-what-every-change-must-keep-working)). |
| R4 | design 2.1.3.2/2.1.2.2 | The decided privilege model (CLI â agent socket, `fabric-admins`) and userns-remap are marked decided but not built â build them or record the current model. |
| R5 | CLI audit | Every CLI change is audited as `root`, not the sudo user (`common/sudo_owner.py` exists). |

### 2.3.1.6.5 P4 â lower-risk security and robustness

- `src/webui/session/start_login.py` `next=` accepts control characters (possible open redirect after sign-in, unverified); pending logins are unbounded for 10 min (same file).
- `src/webui/oidc.py:222-228` some malformed tokens raise the wrong error type (still refused).
- `src/containers/freeradius/fabric_radius.py:85,146` unauthenticated User-Name goes into the decision log unescaped (log-line forging).
- `vault/add_usb_slot.py:64` a failed USB enrolment leaves the key on the stick; `vault/add_kmip_slot.py:21` KMIP client key briefly on disk-backed `/tmp`.
- `templates/stepca/leaf.tpl.j2:14-15` every leaf gets `clientAuth`: the web UI's mTLS gate accepts service and device certificates (the CN must still match the user).
- `pki/sign_csr.py:63` signing for a device links it without `pki:link-device` (custom bundles only).
- `templates/kea/kea-dhcp-ddns.conf.j2:9` the DDNS listener on fabric_net is unauthenticated.
- `templates/postgres/docker-compose.yml.j2` accepts non-TLS connections.
- nginx: port 80 on all interfaces, no `default_server` for unknown SNI, TLS settings not uniform; Fluent Bit's HTTP server on 2020 unused; weak healthchecks.
- Templates insert values unescaped into pages and shell scripts; `install-certs.sh` predictable temp file; the LDAP client script lets every directory user log in.
- `secrets/save_secrets.py:56` rewrites the only secrets file in place (not atomic); `secrets show radius_secrets` prints all at once.
- `dns/*` minor: `/opt` hard-coded for `rfc2136.ini`, quoted `key "x"` refused, CNAME beside A allowed; `radius/map_radius_group.py:27` unstripped name.
- Vendored `marked`/`mermaid` have no source URL or checksum; the manual publishes all of `docs/` unauthenticated.
- `templates/freeradius/config/mods/eap.j2`: TLS max 1.2, deprecated options, no CRL/OCSP (revocation = unlinking).
- `images/switch_image.py:51` rollback only for some exception types.

### 2.3.1.6.6 P5 â dead code (AGENTS Â§3: delete, or register as stale)

- Settings nothing reads: `cert_root_*`, `cert_intermediate_*` (8, never passed to Step-CA), `system_timezone`, `service_dirs`, `compose_file`/`project_containers` (menu only).
- `setup/context.py` `SetupContext.enabled` and the `secrets` setter; `setup/verify_install.py` `_curl(client_cert)`.
- `logs/deploy_fluentbit.py:7` `_write` duplicates `common/write_file_if_changed` (without the chmod).
- `keycloak/require_password_change.py`, `user_has_role.py` build their own admin client instead of `keycloak_admin()`.
- `setup/uninstall.py` old `webui` unit and `fabric/webui:local` image names.
- `templates/bind9/config/named.conf.tls.j2` unused `tls` profile and the empty `bind9/ssl` mount; nginx `conf.d` include; `modconfdir`.
- `.gitignore` Ansible-era entries.

### 2.3.1.6.7 Test gaps (tests/README.md is honest about what each suite proves)

- Never asserted: the host firewall and Docker daemon hardening, certificate renewal, the offline path, relaxations in `status`, arm64 images, `needs_rebuild` on a new base.
- Only in their own suites (not in the full install): EAP-TLS, log forwarding; the web UI's Kea/RADIUS pages against a real agent.
- Weak checks: `tests/freeradius/run.py:397` (PAP outside the tunnel uses a wrong password), `:433` and `tests/kea/run.py:194` (hardening checks inspect flags the test itself set), `tests/dirsrv/run.sh:74` (TLS < 1.2 may be refused by the client), `tests/fluentbit/run.py` logs-status counter (fails consistently: investigate).
- Intermittent service starts (2026-09-30 / 10-01, each passed on rerun, none tied to the change under test): `dirsrv` "Can't contact LDAP server" while seeding in `hardening`; `systemctl start postgres` failing during the sandbox's reinstall; `systemctl start nginx` failing during the sandbox's image rollback. **Cause found 2026-10-02** (postgres during a sandbox install): the wrapper unit's health wait counted a container that was still *missing* after 15 s as dead, but `compose up` was still pulling its image; systemd restarted the unit while the first `compose up` went on creating the container ("name already in use" loop). Fixed in `jinja/systemd/wrapper.service.j2`: missing or created means still pulling or creating, only a container that ran and stopped fails the wait (one that dies at once ends `compose up --abort-on-container-exit`, which fails the unit by itself). Watch whether the dirsrv and nginx cases recur.

- `jinja/nginx/www/ldap/install-ldap.sh.j2` (2026-10-02): the client installer trusts a root CA fetched over plain HTTP without a fingerprint check, and joins nothing useful because no person has a POSIX identity (`uidNumber`â¦). Not to be advertised until M10 step 1 (design [domain-join.md](../volume_1_description_and_architecture/1.6.6-domain-join.md#1661-status), gaps G1âG9).

- `tests/fluentbit` (2026-10-02): "both destinations with records sent" fails â the syslog output retries and sends 0 while Elasticsearch gets its record. Reproduced on a636b5e (before the M9/split work), so not caused by it; investigate the TLS syslog receiver in the test or Fluent Bit 5.1's syslog output.

### 2.3.1.6.8 Ideas from the owner (not scheduled)

- **AdGuard Home as an optional fabric service** (2026-10-01): the owner's network asks AdGuard on 53 for
  everything, and AdGuard forwards the fabric domain to BIND on 5053 (TSIG updates go to BIND directly).
  fabric already supports BIND on another port (`bind_dns_port`, and federation links carry it); a
  managed AdGuard on 53 would forward each site's domain to its BIND, and Kea would hand out AdGuard's
  address (DHCP gives addresses, never ports).

### 2.3.1.6.9 Decisions for the owner

- OpenBao first in setup (2.1.1.8?) â secrets straight into OpenBao at install; needs a temporary TLS bootstrap before Step-CA. Does not remove secrets from service configs (S1, S10 do that).
- `POST /v1/apply` (S9): its own permission, e.g. `system:apply`, or `system:admin`.
- `system:admin` has no route today; what should it grant?
- Plain-HTTP CA bootstrap (S2): keep a HTTP path for first contact, with fingerprint pinning, or HTTPS only?
- The design's privilege model (R4): build it or adopt the current one.
