## 2.3.1.8 Why the checklist answers are what they are

### 2.3.1.8.1 Product shape (2.1.1.8, 2.1.1.9, 2.1.1.10, 2.1.1.11, 2.1.1.12, 2.1.2.3)

fabric exists to set up and run a network's core services on a host, so it is a host product (2.1.1.8). The owner
framed it as two products from the start, the `fabricctl` control and the Open Fabric web control (2.1.3.2), which
Rule 7.2 calls two UX variants; they share one package because the web UI is only a client of the agent the
package installs (2.1.1.9). Ubuntu LTS is what the reference Raspberry Pi and the test machines run (2.1.1.10). On 2026-10-04 the owner made it the
only host target, dropping Debian 13 and Raspberry Pi OS from 2.1.1.1: one large, widely supported distribution with a
long support cycle, backed by one of the largest groups, rather than a test matrix spread over three. The owner
requires arm64 for the Pi and amd64 for testing (2.1.1.11). There is no commercial edition (2.1.1.12). The 4 GB Pi budget
shaped every memory limit (2.1.2.3).

### 2.3.1.8.2 Code and repository (2.1.1.13, 2.1.1.14, 2.1.1.15, 2.1.1.16, 2.1.1.17)

Python was chosen over Go so the package needs no build step and hosts can read what runs (2.1.3.1, 2.1.1.13). One package
is enough for one product (2.1.1.14). ruff and shellcheck catch real errors in the code as written; a typecheck would
need annotations the code does not carry, so it waits (2.1.1.15, owner: "whatever you recommend"). The repository is
on GitHub and already builds in Actions (2.1.1.16). The structured docstrings and the generated reference were the
owner's choice on 2026-09-28 and are built (2.1.1.17).

### 2.3.1.8.3 Release and packaging (2.1.1.18, 2.1.1.19, 2.1.1.20, 2.1.14.4, 2.1.1.21)

`config/VERSION` already follows semantic versioning (2.1.1.18). On 2026-10-04 the owner noted that everything so far has been beta: the first release meant for users is the 1.0 milestone, after thorough testing of the whole stack (2.1.1.31). Semantic versioning orders a pre-release below its release (1.5.0-beta is still above 1.0.0), and v1.0.0 was already taken, so the earlier releases were renumbered to 0.x and later betas stay 0.x until 1.0.0. Releases already publish the `.deb` (2.1.1.2, 2.1.1.19). Only
the previous release must upgrade, because the owner rebuilds older installs instead of migrating them (2.1.1.20).
Digest pinning keeps an upstream push from changing what runs (2.1.14.3, 2.1.14.4). Building images on each host costs the
Pi time and makes hosts differ; the owner chose to publish fabric's own images and manage their updates (2.1.1.21,
2026-10-03).

### 2.3.1.8.4 Installed footprint (2.1.2.4, 2.1.2.5, 2.1.2.6, 2.1.1.22, 2.1.2.7)

The `fabric-*` names and the 600â649 band avoid Ubuntu's own accounts after the 26.04 upgrade asked to remove
`bind` (2.1.2.4). Every install already uses `/opt`, which 2.1.2.1 had wanted to change (2.1.2.5). Uninstall exports on request
because it removes the CA and the vault; downloaded images are kept because they are large and shared (2.1.2.6). The
owner insisted that offline must always work (2.1.1.22). The host-change groups are the consent design (2.1.2.7, 1.2.9).

### 2.3.1.8.5 Security (2.1.7.3, 2.1.6.2, 2.1.2.8, 2.1.2.9, 2.1.2.10)

OpenBao holds secrets so nothing sensitive sits in plain files after setup (2.1.7.1, 2.1.7.3). Keycloak with TOTP and a
client certificate make the web UI's sign-in strong (2.1.6.2). Privileges are listed per container so each one can be
justified (2.1.2.8). The relaxations are the settings that already exist (2.1.2.9). Internal traffic is encrypted because
the services trust each other only through fabric's CA (2.1.2.10).

### 2.3.1.8.6 Integrations, dependencies and data (2.1.1.23, 2.1.1.24, 2.1.1.25, 2.1.2.11)

The owner dropped the Cockpit bridge once a community plugin showed fabric's stacks (2.1.1.23). Hosts refuse extra apt
sources after a release upgrade disabled Docker's own repository on the test Pi, so the host uses only the
archive; inside images the LTS rule (2.1.1.5) can need an upstream repository (2.1.1.24). The owner accepted that the
images run GPL programs unchanged (2.1.1.25). The data stores are the services fabric runs (2.1.2.11).

### 2.3.1.8.7 Testing and documentation (2.1.1.26, 2.1.1.27, 2.1.1.28, 2.1.1.29)

The test machines are what the owner has (2.1.1.26). Hardware security modules are the only thing the owner does not
have, so they are emulated (2.1.1.27). No exports are needed yet (2.1.1.28). The test layout deviation is explained in 2.1.1
(2.1.1.29).

## 2.3.1.9 Test environments

### 2.3.1.9.1 What is available

| Environment | Used for | Status |
|---|---|---|
| WSL Ubuntu 24.04 with Docker (amd64) | Every suite and the sandbox (`tests/run-all.sh`) | In use |
| A Raspberry Pi 5 with 4 GB, Ubuntu 26.04 (arm64) | Real-host installs (`tests/host/run.sh`; the interactive installer with its refusals, `tests/host/installer.py`, since 0.6.2), arm64, the 4 GB budget (2.1.2.3) on real hardware, Ubuntu 26.04 (2.1.1.1) | In use: the agent's test platform, with host-1 and host-2. Its SSD moved to a 4 GB board on 2026-10-04 (the 8 GB board went to production) |
| Two Windows lab hosts (host-1 with WSL, host-2) | Multi-site federation, joining and leaving the domain, Windows Group Policy checks | **Available now (owner, 2026-10-04): the agent's test platform, free to change** (2.3.1.9.5) |

Rules for using them: ask before changing a test machine's system configuration, and never disturb the other
environments on the same machine (global Rule 9) â the WSL machine also runs the owner's own containers.

### 2.3.1.9.2 Docker in WSL

What running the suites there has taught (2026-10-02 to 10-03):

- Call WSL with `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` from Git Bash, and put any multi-line command in a script file: inline
  `bash -c` strings lose their escapes, and a `$(â¦)` in them is expanded on the Windows side.
- The distribution shuts down a few seconds after its last session ends, stopping detached containers (the
  sandbox exits 255) and restarting the owner's: keep a session open for the whole run.
- Never start a second `tests/run-all.sh` with the same `FABRIC_TEST_OUT` while one runs: run-all clears that
  folder. The dirsrv, keycloak and hardening suites need `render` in the same run.
- Never edit a test script while it runs: bash reads scripts as it goes.
- The WSL kernel can hang: processes stuck in `sync()` (state D on `super_lock`) never exit, 389-DS reports
  "already running as process N" and Docker cannot stop containers. Only `wsl --shutdown` clears it, which stops
  every distribution: ask the owner first.
- A Windows `git worktree` cannot be used from WSL (its `.git` file holds a Windows path): clone inside WSL.
- From Git Bash, Python defaults to the cp1252 code page: set `PYTHONUTF8=1` before it edits UTF-8 files.
- (2026-11-01) On gaming-23 the default distribution is NVIDIA-Workbench (no Docker for root, no `jinja2`): name
  it, `wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04`. A Windows worktree can still be tested without a clone by pointing git at it:
  `GIT_DIR=/mnt/d/<repo>/.git/worktrees/<name> GIT_WORK_TREE=$PWD`; without it the lint and docs suites find no files
  ("0 shell scripts") and fail for that alone (2.3.8.3.30).

### 2.3.1.9.3 The Windows lab hosts

Waiting to be rebuilt (2.3.1.7.2). What is pending when they are back: a zone transfer from the Pi's root site to the
lab site on BIND's port 5053; on both hosts Windows Internet Connection Sharing holds UDP 53, and WSL's mirrored
networking needs Hyper-V firewall rules; the Pi's root site still records the old `lab` site as a child â remove
or re-parent it when the lab is rebuilt. Ask the owner before changing Windows firewall or sharing settings.

### 2.3.1.9.4 The test Raspberry Pi

Reached over SSH with a key only (never a password); it is on the owner's live LAN, so DHCP (`install_kea`) is
never turned on there. Its address and account are kept out of this public repository. Its address is fixed in its
own netplan file, with cloud-init's network configuration disabled, so the Imager's settings cannot hand it another
machine's address again (it came up on the production Pi's address after the SSD swap).

Owner, 2026-10-04: the test system's SSD moved to a **4 GB** Raspberry Pi 5 (Ubuntu 26.04; checked the same day:
3.9 GiB, the same host key, fabric's test install intact), which makes it the smallest target (2.1.2.3) on real hardware: every memory limit and the budget (1.2.6.1) are measured there, not only in
WSL. The rules above carry over to it.

### 2.3.1.9.5 The agent's test platform

Owner, 2026-10-04: host-1 (Windows, with WSL) and host-2 (Windows) and the test Raspberry Pi are the agent's to
test on as it needs â joining them to the domain and removing them included; breaking them is acceptable. They
answer the checks only Windows can (a Windows join, Group Policy such as the root-CA trust, per-site log-on rights:
spike Q13, Q16; S5) and the multi-site tests. The owner may later rebuild the lab hosts (e.g. as Proxmox servers);
that changes nothing for fabric. How the agent reaches them is asked of the owner; addresses and credentials stay out
of the repository and off command lines.

### 2.3.1.9.6 Hyper-V test machines

Owner, 2026-10-09: the test Pi is slow, so four Ubuntu machines on Hyper-V join it: 20.04 desktop, 22.04
desktop, 24.04 server and 26.04 server. The servers are fabric hosts (fresh installs, the upgrade and installer tests,
a second site); the desktops are computers outside the domain (trusting the CA, the first sign-in) and the older
releases for the spike of 2.3.1.12. As for 2.3.1.9.5, how the agent reaches them is asked of the owner and their
addresses stay out of the repository.

Owner, 2026-10-10: the Hyper-V machine is **gaming-23**, the owner's gaming PC (not host-1). Its VMs are named
`g23-vm-ub<Ubuntu major>-<n>`, for example `g23-vm-ub24-1`; `<n>` counts copies of one release, only 1 so far, more
added when a test needs them. From 2026-10-10 they also carry 0.9's 802.1X tests (an authenticator VM running
hostapd as a wired authenticator, with Windows and Ubuntu supplicant VMs) and 0.10's HA tests (2.1.1.45).

### 2.3.1.9.7 Moving off WSL to dedicated VMs: proposed, deferred to a later milestone (2026-11-01)

The owner, 2026-11-01: everything should come off WSL into dedicated VMs, but not now: "when we get to a later
milestone we will switch everything over". Until then 2.1.1.26 stands (WSL for the suites), and a VM is made only for
a specific use case that needs one. The impact assessment, kept for that milestone (5.5):

- **The change:** every suite, package build, dev preview and release check runs in dedicated Ubuntu 24.04 VMs on
  gaming-23 (64 GB, beside the lab); the test Pi, GitHub Actions and the lab's campaign VMs unchanged. It revises
  2.1.1.26.
- **What moves:** the suites and the sandbox (no change to them: they already run on GitHub's `ubuntu-24.04`); `/rc`'s
  gate; the apt key check (3.14.1); the dev previews (`.claude/launch.json`); the lab's answer media, the one real
  code change (`scripts/lab/make_seeds.sh` calls `wslpath`, `icacls.exe`, `cmd.exe`), and with it a bootstrap: the
  first build VM needs media only WSL makes today.
- **What it fixes:** the default-distribution and worktree traps and the kernel hangs (2.3.1.9.2), the path mistake
  that removed WSL's `/bin` and `/lib` (3.1.5), and sharing a machine with the owner's own containers (2.3.1.9.1).
- **Risks:** gaming-23's room (2.1.1.64: room when one campaign runs); no self-hosted GitHub runner (a public
  repository).
- **Open points, with the recommendation first:** two build VMs (`g23-vm-build-1`, `-2`: two sessions test at
  once), or one, or one per session; the tree copied over SSH (uncommitted work tested), or the pushed branch
  fetched; a clean checkpoint before each full run, or only when broken; the answer media built in PowerShell with
  Windows' IMAPI2 (no WSL even to rebuild), or in a build VM bootstrapped once from WSL; the WSL distributions left
  installed (they run the owner's containers), or Ubuntu-24.04 removed.

## 2.3.1.10 The owner's network

### 2.3.1.10.1 Two machines per house

The owner runs fabric at home, with a split by load and exposure:

- a **core** Raspberry Pi runs fabric: Keycloak with Postgres, the directory, BIND, Step-CA, OpenBao and the web
  UI. Owner, 2026-10-04: production moves to the **8 GB** Pi; the 4 GB Pi becomes the test Pi (2.3.1.9.4). The
  smallest supported target stays 4 GB (2.1.2.3): production having more memory changes nothing in the design;
- an **edge** Raspberry Pi is the only machine with ports open to the internet: a reverse proxy publishing a
  media server with public certificates, a WireGuard endpoint, and a dynamic-DNS client keeping the home address
  current at the public DNS provider (Cloudflare). The home address is dynamic.

The edge machine and its WireGuard are another stack, outside fabric (2.1.9.10). Let's Encrypt through Cloudflare's
DNS-01 is its own fabric milestone (2.1.5.3).

### 2.3.1.10.2 Several houses

Several houses, each its own routed subnet (not one stretched VLAN), linked by WireGuard (another stack, 2.1.9.10);
fabric federates over the tunnels (1.9). Planned (owner, 2026-10-04): five sites â the home LAN (the root) and the
home lab as two separate sites, and three remote family sites with read-only DCs; every site with the full
stack (2.1.9.9). This is the owner's deployment and a realistic test of fabric, not part of fabric's design.

### 2.3.1.10.3 What stays out of this repository

The repository is public. Addresses, domain names and accounts of the owner's machines are kept out of it (owner
decision 2026-10-03); they live in the owner's own notes, and an agent asks for them when a task needs them.

## 2.3.1.11 0.6.3: the work pulled forward because none of it needs the lab

The owner, 2026-10-08: "get everything that is not dependent on the new setup completed (move as many features as
possible to 0.6.3)" â everything that needs neither the isolated lab network, a switch or access point, nor the
arrow-key setup (0.7). Eight items joined the certificate work of 2.3.5.3; the owner answered each item's design
questions the same day. Each is designed here briefly; the part's own chapter in Volume 1 takes the design when it
is built (global Rule 5.3). Decisions recorded: 2.1.1.37, 2.1.1.38, 2.1.2.14, 2.1.5.10, 2.1.6.30, 2.1.6.31,
2.1.7.4, 2.1.7.5, 2.1.8.3, 2.1.12.2, 2.1.14.13, 2.1.14.14.

### 2.3.1.11.1 The real-container suites in CI (2.1.1.37)

- **On every push, both architectures** (GitHub's `ubuntu-24.04` and `ubuntu-24.04-arm` runners): lint, docs,
  render, zone, consent and the container suites of `tests/run-all.sh` (not the sandbox), each suite its own job so
  a failure names the suite; about 20 minutes. These checks are **required** before a merge to `main` (global
  Rule 8: the owner merges only on green).
- **Nightly and before a merge** (`workflow_dispatch` and a schedule): the sandbox on both architectures, the
  image-update test (2.1.14.12) and the full image smoke tests.
- Suites that need what a hosted runner may not give (loop devices for OpenBao's HSM tests, DHCP clients with
  `NET_ADMIN` for Kea) run there with `sudo`; anything that cannot is marked in the suite and reported as skipped
  with the reason, never silently passed. Fabric's images are built once per run and shared between the jobs.
- Logs and the suites' outputs are kept as artifacts for 30 days. Design home: 1.1 (and 1.14.2.6's regression
  workflow, which this partly builds).
- **Built 2026-10-10 for 0.9** (`suites.yml`, 3.1.2.4). First runs on GitHub's runners:
  1. Every suite passed, but 11 jobs failed after it: the artifact upload could not read the root-only folders the
     suites leave in `/tmp/fabric-tests`. Those hold the run's generated keys and the repository is public, so only the
     logs are kept.
  2. 32 of 34 suite jobs pass on both architectures. `openbao` failed its own prerequisite checks (SoftHSM2, PyKCS11,
     PyKMIP for the HSM and KMIP unlock tests): CI installs them. The sandbox passed **184/0 on amd64 and on arm64**
     (on the image that masks `systemd-binfmt`, 2.3.1.13 finding 5), on 145 GB runners with room to spare; the
     image-update step then could not write its log into the root-owned folder (`sudo tee` now).

### 2.3.1.11.2 The entry-point inventory (2.1.1.38, global Rule 9)

- **The inventory is generated**, never hand-kept: `scripts/docs/entrypoints.py` lists every entry point from its
  source â CLI commands and subcommands (`fabricctl`'s usage texts), interactive questions (each `ask` call given a
  stable ID such as `setup.ad_domain`), fabric-agent routes (the permission table), web pages and form actions (the
  web UI's routes), settings (`vars.yaml.j2`) â into `tests/entrypoints.yaml` (committed, so a change shows in review).
- **Tests claim coverage** with a marker beside the check: `# covers: cli:people.add +` (positive) or `-`
  (negative); the docs suite fails when an entry point lacks either, and when a marker names one that no longer
  exists.
- **Granularity:** each command and subcommand (flags covered through it), each question, each route, each form,
  each setting. A setting whose value cannot be wrong (free text with no rule) is listed with that reason instead of
  a negative case; the list of such reasons is reviewed like code.
- The first inventory will show many gaps: filling them is part of 0.6.3, largest areas first (setup's questions,
  the agent's routes); what is left at the release is listed in its notes, not hidden.

### 2.3.1.11.3 Consent carried over on upgrade (2.1.2.14)

Found by the upgrade test (2.3.5.3.17): an earlier yes to a group that was later split answers the groups it
covered. 0.6.1's `firewall` answers 0.6.3's `ports` and `firewall`; `own_rules`, new, is still asked when the host
has rules of its own. Each future split records its mapping beside the groups (`consent/groups.py`).

### 2.3.1.11.4 Certificate revocation, for everything (2.1.5.10)

- Step-CA publishes a **CRL** (its built-in CRL, signed by the intermediate, regenerated on every revocation and at
  least daily), served on `certs.<domain>` (HTTP, like the CA files) and named in every certificate fabric issues
  (CRL distribution point) from 0.6.3 on.
- **Revoke** any certificate fabric issued â device, person, LAN machine (ACME), service, a federation site's
  intermediate â from the web console's PKI page or `fabricctl certs revoke <serial|name> [--reason R]`, permission
  `pki:revoke` (new; in the PKI operator's bundle). Every revocation is audited.
- **Checked by** FreeRADIUS (EAP-TLS: `check_crl = yes` with the CRL fetched into its config) and nginx (client
  certificates: `ssl_crl`), both refreshed by the certificate timer (2.1.5.4). A revoked device is refused at its next
  authentication even if it is still linked. Answers open decision 2.1.5.2 (federation site CAs).
- Unlinking a device stays the quick way to cut one off; revocation is the lasting one.

### 2.3.1.11.5 The web console's leftovers (2.1.8.3)

- **Doctor on the console:** the Overview runs fabric's doctor checks (the same `verify_install` checks as
  `fabricctl doctor`) on demand, as a job the page follows (a few seconds to a minute), and shows each check with its
  result; permission `status:read`.
- **Image updates from the console:** an Updates section shows `fabricctl images status` and applies `update` or
  `rollback` per service, as a job with its progress; permission `images:update` (new, in the admin bundle), behind
  the admin tools' second factor when raised.
- **Revocation** (2.3.1.11.4), **ACME machines and public certificates** (2.3.5.3.14â15), **dynamic DNS**
  (2.3.5.3.16), **people's new actions** (2.3.1.11.7) and the **Linux join's host groups and sudo rules**
  (2.3.1.11.10) get their pages too.

### 2.3.1.11.6 The DNS leftovers (2.1.12.2, 2.1.14.13)

- **DNS-over-TLS on 853, by AdGuard** (2.1.12.2), so encrypted queries are filtered like the rest; its certificate is
  the DNS name's service certificate, or the public wildcard when that is on (2.1.5.9). Port 853 joins `ports`
  (consent) only when the DNS filter is on. Without the filter, DoT is not offered (BIND's DoH stays).
  **Revised** by the owner on 2026-10-08: the filter's engine becomes BIND (2.1.12.3, 2.3.12.1), and the BIND
  resolver answers DoT instead, in 0.7 (2.1.12.3): DoT leaves 0.6.3.
- **A DoH health check:** doctor and the web console's Overview query `https://<dns name>/dns-query` for a name fabric
  serves and check the answer; the sandbox and host suites do the same.
- **A stale published image is detected** (2.1.14.13): the lock records each published image's **source hash** (its
  Dockerfile, its staged build context and its base image's digest). The images suite and CI refuse a change to an
  image's sources without the image marked `pending` (to be rebuilt and published), so a stale image cannot ship.

### 2.3.1.11.7 `fabricctl people` (2.1.6.30)

- `fabricctl people list | show <uid> | add <uid> --first F --last L --email E | reset <uid> | disable <uid> |
  enable <uid> | remove <uid> | groups <uid> | group add|remove <group> <uid>`, as root on the host (the audit names
  the CLI). A one-time password is printed once on the terminal, never logged.
- **Disable** blocks sign-in everywhere at once: the AD account is disabled and Keycloak's sessions for the person
  end. **Remove** asks for the uid typed back, removes the account and its Keycloak user; their issued certificates
  are revoked (2.3.1.11.4). Only the site's own people; fabric's built-in accounts are refused with the reason.
- The web console's People page gains disable, enable, remove and groups; permissions `people:disable`,
  `people:remove` and `people:groups` (new; disable in the Helpdesk bundle, the others admin).

### 2.3.1.11.8 Offline install bundles (2.1.14.14)

- `fabricctl images export --arch amd64|arm64 -o <file>` makes **one bundle per release and architecture**: fabric's
  `.deb`, the Ubuntu packages it installs (Docker and the rest, for the host's Ubuntu release), every image in the
  lock by digest (`docker save`), and their cosign signature bundles, with a manifest (versions, digests, hashes).
- `sudo fabricctl setup --offline <bundle>` (and `fabricctl images import <bundle>` on an installed host) checks the
  manifest's hashes and **every image's signature offline** (the bundled cosign bundles against fabric's identity,
  2.1.14.6), then installs the packages and loads the images; nothing is fetched. No local registry.
- An image or package whose signature or hash does not check is refused, with which one and why.

### 2.3.1.11.9 OpenBao's leftovers (2.1.7.4, 2.1.7.5)

- **Keycloak's database password rotates monthly** (2.1.7.4): Postgres gets its own admin user, separate from
  Keycloak's (which stops being a superuser); OpenBao's database engine owns Keycloak's password as a static role and
  rotates it on a monthly schedule, at night; fabric then renders Keycloak's settings and restarts it (about a minute).
  **Postgres never depends on OpenBao to start**: the password lives in Postgres; only a rotation needs OpenBao. A
  rotation that fails leaves the old password working and is a warning. `fabricctl vault rotate-db` rotates now.
- **An SSH certificate authority for the domain's Linux machines** (2.1.7.5), OpenBao's SSH engine:
  - **user certificates**, 8 hours, with the person's AD groups as principals (sudo still from AD's rules), from the
    web console (paste a public key, download the certificate) or `bao ssh` after signing in with Keycloak;
    permission `ssh:sign` (new, everyone signed in);
  - **host certificates** for joined machines (the join script asks for one; renewed by the machine's timer), so
    people's clients trust the domain's hosts without the first-connection prompt;
  - the join script trusts the user CA (`TrustedUserCAKeys`, an option, on by default) and installs a known-hosts
    line for the host CA. OpenBao off means no SSH CA, said so on the page.

### 2.3.1.11.10 The Linux join's leftovers (2.1.6.31)

- **Host groups:** AD groups in the site's `OU=groups` (`<site>-hosts-<name>`), machines as members; log-on rules per
  host group (which people's groups may log on), enforced through SSSD's GPO-based access as today. CLI `fabricctl
  machines groups | group add|remove <group> <machine>` and the web console's domain section; permission
  `machines:admin`.
- **Sudo rules:** an editor for custom sudo rules beyond the site's default: who (people or groups), which commands,
  as whom, on which host group; stored as AD sudo rules (SSSD's `sudo_provider=ad`), under the site's OU so nested
  sites inherit as today. CLI `fabricctl sudo list | add | remove`, web console; permission `sudo:admin` (new, admin
  bundle). A rule granting ALL to everyone is refused.
- **systemd-resolved:** the join script points resolved at fabric's DNS with the domain as search domain (1.6.7.5),
  keeping the machine's other links' DNS as they are; an option, on by default.
- The manual's drift is fixed with it (4.6.6 names `<site>-admins` where the code uses `<site>-linux-sudo`).

## 2.3.1.12 Spike: older Ubuntu releases (20.04, 22.04)

Owner, 2026-10-09: "we will do backwards version testing and see if it works, if it doesn't we abandon it as a
supported one". A spike (global Rule 4's exception): fabric's hosts and the Linux join stay Ubuntu 24.04 and 26.04
(2.1.1.1, 2.1.1.10) unless it shows an older release works; then supporting it is a decision of its own, with its
tests. What is tried on the 20.04 and 22.04 machines of 2.3.1.9.6, and what is already known:

| Release | As a fabric host | Joining the domain (`join-linux.sh`) |
|---|---|---|
| 22.04 | the package installs (Python 3.10); setup warns "untested OS" and goes on: tried end to end | refused by the script's release check: tried with that check relaxed on the spike's branch only |
| 20.04 | the package cannot install: it needs Python 3.10 or later (`control.in`), 20.04 has 3.8; out of standard support since April 2025 | as 22.04 |

**Outcome (2026-10-10):** **20.04: not supported.** apt refuses the package (`python3 (>= 3.10)`, 20.04 has
3.8.2): nothing is installed, so fabric cannot run there without shipping its own Python, which is not worth it for a
release out of standard support. **22.04: works.** The first host-suite run failed 10 checks, all
from one cause: `fabric-agent` could not start, a `SyntaxError` in `dns/format_value.py` (an f-string split across
lines inside `{â¦}`, which only Python 3.12 parses, though the package claims 3.10 and ruff targets 3.10). Fixed, the
rerun passed 21 of 22; the one left fetched the certificate page from WSL, which cannot reach the Default Switch (it
answers from another VM). Every other file parses on 3.10. The owner chose to support 22.04 (2.1.1.43), with a Python
3.10 parse check in the lint suite so 3.12-only syntax cannot ship again.

**22.04 joining the domain: not yet.** In `tests/samba/linux_join.py` an `ubuntu:22.04` client joined (with a site
admin's password), resolved fabric's ids, got a Kerberos ticket and had sudo from the site's rule, but a domain person
could not log on: PAM authenticated them, then the account check (`acct_mgmt`) failed with "System error". sssd 2.6
enforces the log-on rights GPO (`ad_gpo_access_control = enforcing`) and its `gpo_child` reads the policy from the
DC's SYSVOL over SMB: there `smbc` open of `GPT.INI` failed, "Permission denied". With the machine's ticket, `kvno
cifs/dc1.<ad domain>` gets a service ticket, yet Samba 4.15's client (`smbclient --use-kerberos=required`) fails to
build its Kerberos session token, `NT_STATUS_NO_LOGON_SERVERS`. Ruled out: `dns_canonicalize_hostname = false`
(`rdns = false` is already set), a client `smb.conf` naming the realm. 24.04 (sssd 2.9, Samba 4.19) passes the same
test. The join script keeps refusing 22.04; what would unblock it: finding why Samba 4.15's client cannot use the
ticket against fabric's DC (its Samba version's Kerberos hardening is the first suspect).

## 2.3.1.13 The 0.8.0 release candidate: runs and findings

0.8.0 holds 0.7's DNS filter, 0.6.4's fixes, Python 3.10 and 0.8's DHCP (2.1.1.44). Branch `rc/0.8.0`, 2026-10-10;
images published as `0.8.0-rc.85` (both architectures, signed) and pinned; the package in apt testing from `7533a52`.

| Run | Where | Result |
|---|---|---|
| docs, lint and every local suite (`tests/run-all.sh`) | WSL | all pass but hardening (finding 1); hardening then 16/0 |
| sandbox, kept | WSL | 184/0 |
| image-update test (`tests/images/update.sh`) | the kept sandbox | 49/4 (finding 4); then 53/0 |
| upgrade from 0.6.2 (`tests/host/upgrade.sh`, apt testing) | test Pi | 14/4 (finding 2); with `APPROVE=accounts` 20/0 |
| host suite, fresh install (`tests/host/run.sh`, apt testing) | test Pi | 22/0 (finding 3) |
| DHCP lab suite (`tests/lab/dhcp.sh`) | Hyper-V lab: ub24 serving, ub22 and ub20 clients | void (finding 5); then 27/0 |

Findings:

1. **The render checks assumed the default LAN.** The hardening suite renders with its own BIND port (10053) and
   `host_ip` (127.0.0.1): the DNS filter's default case inherited 10053 (only an old 53 moves to 5053), and 0.8's
   subnet placement built the LAN interface from `host_ip`. A test fault: both checks now state their values.
2. **The upgrade stops at consent.** The move to the BIND resolver adds a service account; an unattended setup never
   approves a new host change on its own (2.1.2.14), so `--non-interactive` stops naming `accounts`. Correct; the
   upgrade note (3.3.1) now says it, and the test passes `APPROVE=accounts`.
3. **A host suite run over the upgraded install.** The first run skipped the purge the procedure asks for, so setup
   found the upgrade test's raised sign-in layer: four sign-in checks and the DNS check failed. Purged, then 22/0.
4. **Shared images move together.** The resolver runs the bind9 image; `fabricctl images update resolver` moves both
   and says `updated bind9, resolver`, which the test did not read as the resolver. The resolver itself moved,
   rolled back and came back healthy each time. The test reads the list now.
5. **The sandbox broke the lab's SSH.** `binfmt_misc` is the kernel's, shared by WSL and every container: the
   sandbox's systemd ran `systemd-binfmt` at boot, which unregisters every entry, WSL's one for Windows programs
   (`ssh.exe`, through which WSL reaches the lab, 2.3.1.9.6) with them. The lab run started after the sandbox's boot
   reached no VM. The sandbox image masks `systemd-binfmt` now (built and checked masked; the full sandbox has not run on it yet);
   the entry was registered again and the lab rerun.

6. **rc.2: the console's DNS filter group and safe-search forms answered 500.** Found by 0.9's real-agent console
   tests after `v0.8.0-rc.1` was tagged (owner: an rc.2 with only this fix, 2026-10-10): the web console's list of DNS
   filter changes (`webui/agentclient/dns_filter_change.py`) lacked `groups`, `groups/delete` and `safe-search`, so
   those forms failed (the CLI worked); the console test ran them against a stand-in agent, which accepts anything.
   Fixed; `tests/resolver/console.py` now checks that every change the tab posts is one the console sends on and the
   agent serves (it fails on rc.1's code). 0.9 tests the forms against the real agent (2.3.1.16 there).

7. **rc.2: BIND killed by the DNS filter's list check.** The upgrade test from 0.6.2 on the test Pi failed twice at
   setup's last check (`service bind9 â activating`): the lists job ran `named-checkzone` on each converted list
   inside the authoritative BIND's running container (`dns_filter/update_lists.py`), so a large list's check (about
   90 MB for AdGuard's) shared BIND's 128 MiB and the kernel killed `named` (exit 137, `CONSTRAINT_MEMCG`); systemd
   restarted it. It could happen at any list update, on any install; and the killed check also refused a good list.
   Fixed: the check runs in a container of its own from BIND's image (no network, no capabilities, read-only, its
   own 512 MiB). `tests/resolver/run.py` checks a 300,000-name list against a stand-in BIND limited to 48 MiB: it
   loads and the stand-in lives (rc.2's code got the stand-in OOM-killed and refused the list).

8. **After rc.2's tag: setup's last words skipped the names.** The owner's test install of `v0.8.0-rc.2`: "FIRST
   install fabric's root certificate â¦ open http://info.home.arpa/" cannot work on a computer that does not use
   fabric's DNS. Now the first step is the names (set the computer's DNS server to fabric, or a hosts-file line
   naming info, fabric, sso, certs, ca and vault, printed), then the root CA from `http://<host_ip>/certs/` (served by
   address, plain HTTP, no names needed), then sign in; the login kit's README follows the same order.
   `tests/render.py` checks the README's order; `tests/host/installer.py` the printed order (passed on the test Pi
   with rc.3). In rc.3.

9. **Every button of the web console failed in a browser.** The owner's test of `v0.8.0-rc.2`: "Fetch every list
   now" answered "Request rejected (CSRF check failed)". The console sent `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, under which
   browsers send `Origin: null` on their own form posts; the CSRF check (Origin must be the console's address)
   refused every post, whatever the form. The suites set `Origin` themselves, so none noticed. Now `same-origin`
   (other sites still get no referrer); `tests/webui/test_container.py` checks the header and that `Origin: null` is
   still refused. In rc.3 (with finding 8, and the console's "optional" pills removed at the owner's review).

10. **Not a finding: OpenBao's UI and fabric's own secrets.** At the same review OpenBao's UI said the console admin's
    policy "permits you to write" fabric's secrets. `fabric-console-admin` grants only a list on `fabric/metadata*`;
    a real token of that policy is refused (403) reading and writing `fabric/data/*` (`tests/openbao/run.py`, passed
    on rc.3). The wording is the UI's own.

**rc.2's runs** (2026-10-11; images `0.8.0-rc.100`, the package from `37c291f` in apt testing):

| Run | Where | Result |
|---|---|---|
| resolver, webui, hardening, images | WSL | pass |
| sandbox, kept | WSL | 184/0 |
| image-update test | the kept sandbox | 53/0 |
| DHCP lab suite | Hyper-V: ub24 serving, ub22 the client | 21/0 |
| upgrade from 0.6.2 | test Pi | 19/1 twice (finding 7, fixed); a run lost to WSL's route to the Pi (the Pi stayed up; WSL now reaches it through Windows' ssh); then 20/0 |

Also done for the lab: ub26 (upgraded to 26.04.1) was made a server: networkd, `eth1` 10.20.0.11/24 on the lab
switch, booting to multi-user. Its desktop's purge was still running when it stopped answering SSH (the port
open, no banner); not needed for this candidate, left for the owner to look at.

**rc.3's runs** (2026-10-11; images `0.8.0-rc.111` from `c727974`, the package from `5a1e085` in apt testing):

| Run | Where | Result |
|---|---|---|
| render, webui (122/122), resolver, images, openbao | WSL | pass |
| sandbox, kept | WSL | 184/0 |
| image-update test | the kept sandbox | 53/0 |
| upgrade from 0.6.2 | test Pi | 20/0 |
| interactive installer (setup's first steps, finding 8) | test Pi | 36/0 |
| DHCP lab suite | â | not rerun: nothing in rc.3 touches DHCP (21/0 on rc.2) |

## 2.3.1.14 Free-text labels went into files unquoted (found 2026-10-10)

Found while marking which tests cover which settings (2.1.1.38): the organisation's free-text labels
(`friendly_name`, `ca_name`, `copyright`, `trademark`, `service_mark`, `care_of`, `contact_email`, `contact_phone`,
`address_line1`, `address_line2`) were written into files as they were typed, since before 0.6.2.

- **The settings file.** `vars.yaml.j2` wrote them unquoted: `Acme: Inc` made the file unreadable, and `Smith # 1`
  was read back as `Smith` (the rest a comment).
- **The pages.** The info site's pages (`templates/nginx/www/`) are rendered by the deploy's environment, which did not
  escape: a label was HTML. The web console was never affected (its pages escape).
- **The client scripts.** `install-python-ubuntu.sh`, `install-chrome-ubuntu.sh`, `install-firefox-ubuntu.sh` and
  `install-certs.sh` held `friendly_name` inside double quotes: a name with `$(â¦)` or a backtick would run on every
  client that installs fabric's certificates, some steps with `sudo`. Only the host's admin sets the labels (setup and
  the menu), so it could not be reached by anyone else; the settings page planned for 0.11 (2.1.8.4) would have let a
  web console admin set them.

Fixed in 0.9 (the owner's choice: the release being worked on): JSON strings in the settings file, autoescape for
`*.html.j2`, a `sh` filter in the scripts; the rule is 1.1.5.7. With the defaults the rendered files are what they
were (the settings read back identical; the scripts differ only in `'â¦'` for `"â¦"`). The new check in
`tests/render.py` fails on the old templates. The labels need no negative case now: any value is valid, so they are
listed in `tests/entrypoints-reasons.yaml`.

## 2.3.1.15 `fabricctl --mint-certs` crashed and left vars.yaml changed (found 2026-10-10)

Found while writing the PKI entry points' negative tests (2.1.1.38). A days or key-size answer that was not a number
ended in a `ValueError` traceback; an output directory that did not exist, or a key type step-ca does not mint (DSA),
was saved to `extra_certs` in vars.yaml before the mint failed, so a refused certificate stayed recorded and was
retried at the next `--mint-certs` run. Fixed in 0.9: every answer is checked before anything is written (a whole
number of days and bits, an existing directory, RSA, EC or OKP), each refusal says why and exits 1; the certificate is
minted first and recorded only once it exists. `tests/pki/run.py` covers each refusal (and that vars.yaml and the
folder are unchanged) beside the positive case.

Seen at the same time, and fixed the same day: `fabricctl --client-cert` without a user printed a traceback (now
the usage, exit 2); a `pki_manual_max_days` that is not a whole number broke the web console's PKI page (now refused at
apply, with why). Both have their negative tests.

## 2.3.1.16 The console's DNS filter group and safe-search forms answered 500 (found 2026-10-10)

Found by the web console's new real-agent tests (2.1.1.38). The DNS filter tab's forms for client groups (add, change,
remove) and for safe search posted to routes the agent serves, but the console's own list of changes
(`webui/agentclient/dns_filter_change.py`, `CHANGES`) lacked `groups`, `groups/delete` and `safe-search`, so each answered
500: client groups and per-group safe search could be set only with `fabricctl dns-filter`. The console test that should
have caught it (`tests/resolver/console.py`) ran the route against a stand-in agent, which accepts anything: the stand-in
hid the bug (the reason 2.1.1.27 allows them only for pure logic). **In 0.8.0** (shipped in `v0.8.0-rc.1`); fixed in
0.9, with the forms' positive and negative cases against the real agent in `tests/webui/test_container.py` (whose test
install now has its `lan_cidr`, so a group's clients can be checked against the answered networks).

## 2.3.1.17 Bugs found by 0.9's tests, still open

Found 2026-10-10 while writing the entry points' tests, left as gaps until fixed:

- `fabricctl radius log -n abc` ends in a `ValueError` traceback; `radius log -n` and `radius map-group <g> --vlan` (no
  value) in an `IndexError` (`radius/run_radius_command.py`).
- `fabricctl images status --deploy-base X`: `cli.py` passes `--deploy-base` on to `images`, which prints its usage.
- `images/prune_images.py` on Docker's containerd image store: `rmi` of tag and digest removes the image but exits 1,
  so prune reports "no old images to clean up".
- `fabricctl vault revoke-token` says "revoked" for any input, a malformed one included (`vault/revoke_token.py`).
- Smaller: `verify_signature` gives no reason when cosign cannot run; an unparsable `resolver_mem_limit` turns the
  memory guard off silently; `| bool` reads a typo in `image_signature_check` as false.

## 2.3.1.18 Proposal: 0.7.0, DNS made right, and AdGuard Home reconsidered

Written 2026-10-11 (decision 2.1.1.49) from the owner's test of `v0.8.0-rc.3` on the test Pi. Nothing here is built.

**What the owner found**

- **Adding a list took minutes.** Measured on the Pi: the lists job itself is quick (5.9 s for "Fetch every list now":
  the AdGuard DNS filter, 362,758 records, downloads in 0.1 s, converts in 2.6 s and checks in 2.6 s). The time goes
  elsewhere: every DNS filter change from the console (`agent/post_dns_filter.py`) runs `apply_changes`, a full
  `fabricctl --apply` of every service, and the console waits for it. Adding one list was still applying 3 minutes
  later. A list, rule, group or safe-search change needs only the resolver's configuration written and
  `rndc reconfig` (or one zone reloaded): seconds.
- **Lists should be checkboxes, enabling them quick:** as AdGuard Home's page (the owner's screenshot): every list
  of the catalogue with a checkbox, its rules and when it was updated. With every catalogue list fetched and
  converted daily (disk and a little CPU; no memory until enabled), enabling one is a reload, and its memory is
  known before it is ticked.
- **Memory:** a list in BIND is a response policy zone held in memory, two records per blocked name (the name and
  `*.` below it), measured at 0.53 KB a record: the AdGuard DNS filter's 181,422 rules cost about 188 MiB of the
  resolver's 384 MiB. The owner's own AdGuard Home runs 15 lists (about 2.6 million rules, HaGeZi's Threat
  Intelligence Feeds 2.0 million of them): its filter is a matcher made for the job, rules kept as compact strings,
  one rule covering a name and every name below it. Merging the enabled lists into one zone, deduplicated, would
  save what the lists share (perhaps 20-40%), not the order of magnitude.
- **Zones:** the console adds and removes records in existing zones but cannot add a zone (only `sudo fabricctl`'s
  menu can).
- **The CA's address:** one place, `info.<domain>/certs`, and `http://<host_ip>/certs/` for a computer that does not
  yet use fabric's DNS; `certs.<domain>` goes, with no redirect (the owner).

**The engine, reconsidered: AdGuard Home behind fabric's console**

The owner: "consider returning adguardhome using api instead of it's default web ui". The shape, to be confirmed:

- AdGuard Home answers clients (53, and DoT/DoH), as in 0.6, with fabric's zones forwarded to the authoritative BIND
  and never filtered, internet names to Cloudflare over DoT. `bind9-resolver` goes; the authoritative BIND stays.
- **fabric's console is its only UI:** fabric-agent drives AdGuard Home's HTTP API (`/control/...`: filtering lists
  and their enabled flag, rules, clients and their settings, safe search, the query log, statistics). AdGuard
  Home's own web UI is not published: its HTTP port is on `fabric_net` only (no nginx site), its account a
  generated password in fabric's secrets. (AdGuard serves its UI and its API from the same port, so the UI cannot be
  turned off, only left unreachable.)
- **What maps directly:** lists (catalogue checkboxes are AdGuard's own model: it fetches and refreshes them), the
  owner's allow and block rules, upstreams, safe search per client, the query log and statistics, DoT and DoH.
- **What does not map:** a client group's own lists. AdGuard Home's lists are global; per client it has settings
  (filtering on or off, safe search, blocked services, upstreams) and rules may name clients (`$client=`). A group's
  own rules map; its own lists would not, unless shown as rules. The query log is AdGuard's, not BIND's
  (`querylog.py` and the 90-day statistics are rebuilt on its API).
- **The cost:** a reversal of 2.1.12.3 a release after it was built. The work of 0.7's steps 2 to 6 for the resolver
  is replaced, not extended; the move-from-AdGuard code (2.3.12.1.9) becomes a move back, for installs that ran a
  0.8.0 candidate (only test hosts). Its container must meet fabric's hardening (non-root, capabilities dropped,
  read-only root, memory limit: 1.2.8) and its image pinned and published like the others (2.1.14).

**Impact assessment (5.5)**

- **Architecture:** the client-facing resolver changes engine; the console's DNS filter page keeps its pages and
  routes, its data from AdGuard's API instead of BIND's files and logs. The authoritative BIND, zones, TSIG, ACLs
  and the CA are untouched.
- **Breaking:** for released installs, none: 0.6.x ran AdGuard Home, and 0.7 was never released. For the owner's
  test hosts on a 0.8.0 candidate: their resolver settings move to AdGuard Home at the upgrade, groups' own lists
  reported if any.
- **Scope creep risk:** high if both engines are kept; the proposal replaces one with the other. The spike below
  comes first so the choice is made on measurements.

**A spike before the choice (Rule 4's exception), awaiting the owner's go**

On WSL, then the test Pi (arm64, 4 GB), with the owner's 15 lists: AdGuard Home with them, against BIND's resolver
with the same lists merged and deduplicated. Measured: memory after loading, time to load and to enable one more
list, answer time for blocked and allowed names (p50, p99), a restart. In `spikes/dns-engine/`; nothing merged.

**Questions for the owner (after the spike)**

1. The engine: AdGuard Home through its API, or BIND with merged lists.
2. With AdGuard Home: a group's own lists become rules naming the group's clients, or go.
3. The memory limit for the filter on a 4 GB host.

**The spike's findings** (2026-10-11, WSL, `spikes/dns-engine/`; AdGuard Home v0.107.79, BIND 9.20.29; the owner's
15 lists, one download served to both). 0 checks failed.

| | A: BIND in front, AdGuard Home behind | B: BIND alone, the lists merged |
|---|---|---|
| The 15 lists | 2 771 618 rules, added through the API in 2.3 s | 5 016 955 records, 4 587 784 merged (deduplication: 8.6%) |
| Memory, 15 lists | 350 MiB (empty: 14 MiB); BIND in front 45 MiB | 2.33 GiB |
| A 16th list (HaGeZi's Ultimate, 3.0 million rules) | 0.2 s; 921 MiB while it rebuilds, 345-434 MiB after | 29 s (merge and reload); 3.15 GiB |
| A list off / on | 0.08 s / 0.93 s | a merge and reload |
| Answer, blocked name (mean) | 0.62 ms | 0.16 ms |
| Answer, ordinary name (mean, cached) | 0.60 ms | 0.54 ms |
| Restart until blocking again | 3.5 s (345 MiB, 16 lists) | 15.1 s (2.35 GiB) |

- **Checks passed (A):** fabric's zone answered by BIND though AdGuard has a rule blocking it; a listed name
  NXDOMAIN through BIND; an ordinary name resolves; the owner's rule blocks in BIND and BIND's log names it; a group
  (the kids' view forwarding from its own address) gets its own AdGuard rule, others do not; dnstap records the client,
  name and NXDOMAIN; AdGuard's query log and statistics off; `check_host` names the rule and its list in 0.05 s.
- **AdGuard Home's defaults to change:** `blocking_mode` answers `0.0.0.0` unless set to `nxdomain`.
- **No marker on a blocked answer:** through BIND, neither a blocked NXDOMAIN nor a genuine one carried an authority
  section, so an answer alone does not tell a block from a missing name: the log job asks `check_host`, cached per name.
- **Running it non-root, as fabric's hardening needs (1.2.8):** its first launch (no `AdGuardHome.yaml`) refuses any
  user but root, `--no-permcheck` or not; root with every capability dropped cannot write folders it does not own; as
  a non-root user `no-new-privileges` voids the binary's file capability (`cap_net_bind_service`), so port 53 comes
  from the namespace's `ip_unprivileged_port_start=53`, and NET_BIND_SERVICE stays in the bounding set only so the
  kernel will run the binary at all. fabric renders `AdGuardHome.yaml` itself (schema 34), so there is no first launch.
- **The owner chose A** the same day ("go with adguard behind bind"): decision 2.1.12.6.

**Confirmed for the design** (1.12.3.14, `spikes/dns-engine/clientid.sh`, 0 failed): a group's view forwarding over
DoT with `remote-hostname "kids.filter.<domain>"` is AdGuard Home's client `kids` (its rule applied, `check_host
...&client=kids` names it); the main view over plain DNS is not; a list read from a file (`/lists/...`, with
`safe_fs_patterns` set in `AdGuardHome.yaml`) blocks for everyone. AdGuard's TLS API takes inline PEM only
base64-encoded: paths are simpler; it warns that the chain does not verify against its own store (fabric's CA is not
in it), harmlessly. A fresh install adds AdGuard's default lists.

**Memory on the test Pi** (2026-10-11, arm64, 4 GB, beside the owner's rc.3 install; one AdGuard container capped at
1.5 GB, removed after; no OOM): empty 34 MiB; the owner's 15 lists (2 582 927 rules, added in 4.3 s from the
internet) 356 MiB, 609 MiB at most while loading; a 16th (HaGeZi's Ultimate, 2 813 027 rules) 726 MiB at once and 751
at most, 491 MiB a minute later; restarted with 16 lists, 338 MiB (567 at most), answering again after 33 s.


**Built** (2026-10-11, WSL; AdGuard Home v0.107.79, BIND 9.20): the resolver suite with real BIND, AdGuard and
resolver containers 73/73, the groups suite (each group's view to AdGuard over DoT with its ClientID, a test CA and a
`*.filter` certificate) 50/50, the query-log suite against Postgres and the console suite all pass. Found on the way,
each fixed with its test and written into 1.12.3 (listed in 1.12.3.15):
- AdGuard reached `tls://cloudflare-dns.com` through addresses with no DoT (104.16.x.x): every internet name timed
  out. `tls://1.1.1.1` (the certificate names the address) answers, so the upstreams are given by address.
- A first start reads a list given as a file only at AdGuard's first update, about 5 s later; until then nothing was
  blocked. The sync now asks it to read any ticked list it has not read; a restart reads them at once from its data.
- With AdGuard stopped the resolver answers SERVFAIL after its 10 s query timeout (a 5 s client times out first);
  nothing passes unfiltered.
- Docker leaves NET_BIND_SERVICE effective for the non-root process (`CapEff 0x400`): its one capability.
- The console's tick (`lists/set`) was missing from the agent's permission table and the console's list of changes:
  every tick would have been refused. Both added; the console suite checks every posted change against both.

## 2.3.1.19 The 0.7.0 release candidate: runs and findings

0.7.0 is DNS made right (2.1.1.49): the AdGuard-behind-BIND filter (2.1.12.6), the zones' upstreams, the CA at
`info.<domain>/certs` with the CRL named in service certificates (2.1.5.11), the DC's own name and the id layout
(1.6.5.23, 1.6.3.17). Branch `rc/0.7.0`, 2026-10-11; images published as `0.7.0-rc.126` and pinned (`d989cff`); the
package in apt testing.

| Run | Where | Result |
|---|---|---|
| docs, lint and every local suite | WSL | all pass after two render checks were given the host's address and base folder (`36ea19a`) |
| upgrade from 0.6.2 (`tests/host/upgrade.sh`, apt testing) | test Pi | 14/4 (consent: expected, 2.3.1.13 finding 2); with `APPROVE=accounts` 17/3 (2.3.1.19.1) |
| samba suite (`tests/samba/dc.py`) with the fix | WSL | 110/0 |
| sandbox, kept | WSL | void: one stale check (2.3.1.19.2), and a stray partial run of the suite overlapped its last sections |
| apt testing package from `d9be0f8` (the role owners' fix) | GitHub Actions | built |
| upgrade from 0.6.2, again (`APPROVE=accounts`) | test Pi | 20/0 |
| host suite, fresh install (apt testing) | test Pi | 21/1: the DNS filter's check (2.3.1.19.3) |
| sandbox, kept, from `68eede0` | WSL | 184/1: the new POSIX check passed `-s` to `ldbsearch`, its scope option, not a config file; fixed, then that check passes on the kept box |
| image-update test (`tests/images/update.sh`) | the kept sandbox | 58/0 |

### 2.3.1.19.1 A renamed AD site left the DC refusing schema additions

The upgrade from 0.6.2 stopped at setup's converge: `schema_data_add: we are not master: reject add request` while
adding `fabricIdMark`, on a DC that holds every role. Samba decides that it is the schema master by comparing the
schema object's `fSMORoleOwner`, as DN text, with its own NTDS object's DN (`schema_init.c`). Converge renames AD's
`Default-First-Site-Name` to the fabric site after the schema (S1.3), so every role object kept the old site's text
beside the right GUID: every install before 0.7.0 refused any schema addition, and 0.7.0 is the first release to add
one. Doctor's two follow-on failures (Keycloak's admin role, its database password) came from setup stopping there;
setup did exit 1.

Fixed: converge rewrites the owner of each role this DC holds to its current DN, before the schema and again after
the AD site (1.6.5.24, `repair_role_owners.py`). On the test Pi it rewrote seven owners (the schema, partitions,
domain, RID manager and three infrastructure objects), a second run nothing, and the schema took an addition again.
Samba keeps one schema per process, loaded by the first database opened, with that one's "schema update allowed":
the repair's database therefore allows updates as `ensure_schema`'s does (the first try, without, was caught by the
samba suite). `tests/samba/dc.py` reproduces a stale owner refusing an addition, then checks the converge's repair,
and the repair and an addition in one process.

### 2.3.1.19.2 The sandbox's POSIX check expected the old ids

The sandbox checked the first admin's uid as 5001 or above (up to six digits). A new install from 0.7.0 lays out its
block from 1 000 000 (1.6.3.17): the admin has uid 1000000 and Domain Users gid 1040009, as designed. A test fault:
the check now states both values.

### 2.3.1.19.3 Nothing validates DNSSEC

The host suite's DNS filter check still read the old engine's RPZ log and wanted the AD flag. Looking past that:
`dnssec-failed.org`, whose signatures are deliberately broken, resolves NOERROR through fabric (Cloudflare alone
answers SERVFAIL). A capture between the resolver and AdGuard Home shows BIND's forwarded queries carry CD; BIND's
source sets it on every query when the view does not validate. 1.12.3.2's "AdGuard Home validates" never held, and
the resolver suite checked only that example.com resolved.

Tried on the test Pi, each change put back after: BIND validating answers `dnssec-failed.org` SERVFAIL, example.com
and www.cloudflare.com with AD, fabric's zones as before, but every blocked name SERVFAIL (AdGuard also blocks the DS
query BIND needs to prove a zone unsigned); with an AdGuard allow rule for DS queries added, blocked names in unsigned
zones (`ads.doubleclick.net`, `pagead2.googlesyndication.com`) answer NXDOMAIN again and only a blocked zone's apex
(`doubleclick.net`) stays SERVFAIL. The host suite's check is split (blocking; validation) and the resolver suite
checks validation.

**Decided** (owner, 2026-10-11, "1 A"): option A, built (2.1.12.7, 1.12.3.2). Fabric's own zones are excepted from
validation (`validate-except`). The resolver suite passes with real AdGuard and Cloudflare: broken signatures
refused, example.com with AD, a listed name under a made-up TLD NXDOMAIN; example.org, listed in the test and signed,
now answers SERVFAIL (still blocked): the resolver and groups suites expect that.

### 2.3.1.19.4 Applies restart one service at a time

Owner, 2026-10-11 ("we should fix roll out gates now for 0.7"): 2.1.14.15 and 1.14.2.11 came over from `dev/0.9`, and
an apply's restarts are now one at a time in setup's order, each health-gated, stopping at the first failure
(`deploy/restart_changed.py`; the order moved to `setup/service_order.py`, which setup and the apply share, since
importing it from `start_services` would have made a cycle). `tests/deploy/restart_changed_test.py` checks the order
and the stop with systemd stood in for (5/5); the sandbox runs real applies. Rolling a failed service's configuration
back is not built: the previous rendering is not kept (1.14.2.11).

### 2.3.1.19.5 Fabric's `info` name cannot simply be turned off

Found 2026-10-12 while building 2.1.2.22 item 6 for 0.7.1. The decision asks that deleting the landing page's
`info.<domain>` in the DNS tab turn its name off through its setting (`landing_page_cname`). It cannot be done by
the setting alone, so 0.7.1 refuses it like fabric's other names (1.4.1.4), naming the setting, and the owner
decides how it should work:

- **The setting cannot say "off".** `vars.yaml.j2` reads `landing_page_cname | default('info', true)`: an empty value
  means `info`, on purpose since 0.6.3 (installs that had none got `info`, 2.1.4.3).
- **The landing page's address does not follow it.** `hostname_landing` is rendered into the installed settings
  (`info.<domain>`) and kept from then on, so changing `landing_page_cname` later moves the DNS name but not the page.
- **The page is load-bearing.** `info.<domain>` serves the CA certificates over plain HTTP to new devices
  (`/certs/`), the join scripts point at it, and every certificate step-ca issues names its CRL there
  (`http://info.<domain>/certs/crl/â¦`, 2.1.5.11). Moving the page to `<domain>` (where it was before 0.6.3; nginx
  already handles `hostname_landing` equal to the domain) needs nginx's certificate for the new name before nginx
  restarts â nginx will not start without it â and leaves the certificates already issued pointing their CRL at a
  name that no longer answers until they are renewed.

Options for the owner: (a) keep `info` as fabric's, never deleted (as built); (b) a real "landing page at
`<domain>`" setting: an explicit off value for `landing_page_cname`, `hostname_landing` re-rendered from it, the
landing certificate re-issued first, and the CRL kept answering at the old name until the last certificate naming it
expires; (c) keep serving the page at `info.<domain>` but drop only the DNS name (not useful: nothing could reach it).

**Settled by 2.1.4.6 (the owner, 2026-10-12):** none of the three. The page moves to a path fabric keeps
(`http://<host_ip>/info/`, and fabric's own names), `info.<domain>` becomes an ordinary record the admin may rename,
repoint or delete, and certificates name their CRL at `ca.<domain>` (1.4.2.5, 1.5.1.6).

## 2.3.1.20 0.7.1: dev/0.9 merged in

On 2026-10-12, as 2.1.1.51 directs, `dev/0.9` (everything but 802.1X) was merged into `dev/0.7.1`, which starts from
0.7.0. The two had grown apart since `rc/0.8.0`: 0.7.0 rebuilt the DNS filter around AdGuard Home and gave the DC its
own name (2.1.6.45) and each site an id block of a million (2.1.6.46); `dev/0.9` added `ask()`, the entry-point
inventory, host access and the host's own domain membership.

### 2.3.1.20.1 How the conflicts were settled

- **0.7.0's design wins where the two disagree:** the DNS filter's suites keep 0.7.0's tests (AdGuard behind the
  resolver); `dev/0.9`'s CLI tests for `fabricctl dns-filter` were rewritten to 0.7.0's layout (lists in
  `dnsfilter/lists/`, the words it prints) and kept with their coverage markers. The CA stays at
  `info.<domain>/certs/` only (`hostname_certs` and `cname_certs` are gone, and their four gaps with them); the id
  layout is 0.7.0's.
- **`dev/0.9`'s additions are kept whole:** `ask()` everywhere (0.7.0's new DC-name question converted, ID
  `setup.dc_name`), the free-text label checks, host access, the group allocator's site groups beside 0.7.0's
  built-in ranges.
- **The host's domain identity follows the DC's name:** the keytab holds `<dc_name>$` and its `host/` names, sssd and
  Kerberos point at `hostname_dc` (1.6.11.8, 2.1.6.34).
- **The webui image** is pending again (its sources changed since 0.7.0-rc.126); 0.7.1's candidate publishes it.

### 2.3.1.20.2 Entry points 0.7.0 added

0.7.0 predates the inventory, so the merge found 32 entry points without a positive or negative test. 28 were tested
already and gained their markers; the DC-name question, a zone menu's "hide the public name" yes, the console's
refused list tick and `fabricctl dns-filter apply` (its refusals in the resolver suite, the real one in the sandbox)
gained tests. Four have no refusal anywhere in the code, as their siblings on the gaps list: `adguard_mem_limit`,
`image_adguard`, `ip_adguard` and `posix_id_start` (a bad memory limit, image or address is refused by Docker at
start, not by fabric's settings check); they join the gaps list rather than inventing a check in a merge.

**Since 2026-10-12** (the owner: "Add checks") each is checked before an apply starts anything: every image and
`ip_` setting and the uid/gid block by `check_service_settings` (with `image_kea` and `posix_id_block`, their
siblings on the gaps list), `adguard_mem_limit` by the memory plan (2.1.12.8).

### 2.3.1.20.3 The console's DNS filter changes were refused on a default install

Found by the webui suite after the merge (its DNS filter checks had not run since 0.7.0 brought AdGuard back): every
change from the DNS filter tab (add a list, block a name) was refused with "dns_filter_upstreams must list at least
one", on any install that kept the default upstreams. The console's changes check `vars.yaml` as saved, and an
install that kept the default has no `dns_filter_upstreams` there (the template supplies Cloudflare's). Fixed in
0.7.1: `check_filter_settings` reads an unset (or null) value as the template's default; an empty list is still
refused. The resolver suite checks both.

### 2.3.1.20.4 A list added while its job could not start looked refused

Found by the same webui run: a list added in the console is saved and made at once, then fabric-agent starts the
lists job to fetch its first copy; when that start failed, the agent answered 400 and the console showed only the
error, though the list was saved. Since 0.7.1 the change answers as saved, `waiting` naming why ("the lists job
could not start â¦; run sudo fabricctl dns-filter lists to fetch it"), and the console says "Saved, but not in use
yet" with it. The resolver's console suite checks it.

### 2.3.1.20.5 Memory as a budget meets AdGuard Home

Merging `dev/0.9-setup-ux` (memory as a budget, 2.1.1.47, written before 0.7.0) into 0.7.1: its plan gave the resolver
384 MiB because it held the lists; since 0.7.0 AdGuard Home holds them (1.12.3.9), so the resolver's floor is 0.7.0's
128 MiB, weight 0. AdGuard Home could not join the budget as it stands. A floor that holds the owner's lists (their 751
MiB rebuild peak; the tick rule's 2.5 Ã their 380 MiB steady size, about 950 MiB) puts a 4 GB host with Keycloak, the
web console and the DNS filter past its budget (3872 MB of floors with a 768 MiB floor against 3584 MB), and the
owner's Pi is that host. So for now AdGuard Home keeps 0.7.0's limit outside the budget (`adguard_mem_limit`, 1 GiB
at 4 GB): the budget is overcommitted by that much, as 0.7.0 is. Open for the owner: let the budget overcommit the DNS
filter, lower other floors to make room, or ask 4 GB hosts to leave a part off.

**Settled 2026-10-12 by 2.1.12.8:** the rebuild's spike is temporary, so the budget counts AdGuard Home's steady
size and warns about the minute its limit may pass it (1.2.4.3.5).

### 2.3.1.20.6 A log-on change outlived the console's wait; the webui suite's install could deploy

Found by the webui suite on `dev/0.7.1-all`, after the console fixes (2.1.2.22) were merged in:

- **The console gave up on a log-on change before its apply ended.** Setting or clearing who may sign in per OU
  saves and applies at once (1.6.11.4), but the console called fabric-agent with the default 30 s wait, while an
  apply may run for minutes (0.7.0 restarts one service at a time, each health-gated, 2.1.14.15). The console showed
  503 while the apply went on. Fixed in 0.7.1: the call waits as long as Apply changes does (960 s).
- **The suite's test install could apply.** The suite's console changes run fabric's apply on the machine running
  the suite, and must be refused there (its check: no apply ever exits 0). The settings the console fixes added for
  the Domain view (`ad_domain`, `dc_name`, the password policy) let that apply get through on WSL: it wrote fabric's
  `bind9` and `openbao` units into the WSL distribution's systemd and ran for 10 minutes. The suite now records
  fabric's own services as declined (1.2.9.4), so every apply it starts is refused at once, as designed, whatever its
  settings allow.

### 2.3.1.20.7 A renamed landing alias has no HTTPS (settled 2026-10-12: 2.1.4.8)

Found 2026-10-12 while building 2.1.4.6. The landing page answers on HTTPS only at the names its service certificate
names: `hostname_landing` (rendered once at install, `info.<domain>` by default), `<domain>` and the host's own name.
When the admin renames the `info` record (say to `welcome`), `http://welcome.<domain>/` shows the page (nginx sends
any name it does not serve to `/info/`), but `https://welcome.<domain>/` gets a certificate for another name, and the
page's links to the manual go to the host's own name. Built as is: nothing fabric depends on uses the alias any more,
and a renamed or repointed alias breaks nothing of fabric's. **Open for the owner:** (a) keep it so (HTTP at a renamed
alias; HTTPS at fabric's names); (b) the landing certificate also names every record of the site's zone that points
at the host, so a renamed alias gets HTTPS after the next apply (one certificate re-issue per rename); (c) a setting
for the landing page's HTTPS name, which re-renders `hostname_landing` and re-issues its certificate.

Settled 2026-10-12: the owner chose (b), and that only the new name be on the certificate, the old one revoked
(decision 2.1.4.8; design 1.4.2.6, 1.5.1.7). Built the same day: the landing names are the host's own name, `<domain>`
and the zone's aliases of the host at each apply (`dns/common/landing_names.py`), nginx serves the page at each, and
an apply that finds the certificate's names changed issues it again at once and revokes the one it replaced
(`deploy/renew_landing_cert.py`, `setup/mint_service_certs.py`). The page's links needed no change: they are relative,
and `/manual/` over plain HTTP goes to HTTPS at the same name, which the certificate now names; the default server's
`/manual/` (a name the zone did not hold at the last apply) still goes to the host's own name. Left as they are, for
the owner to change if wanted: an AAAA record is not a landing name (fabric's host is reached at `host_ip`, IPv4);
setup's first steps and the admin's README still give `hostname_landing` in the hosts-file line, which is right on
a new install, where `info` points at the host.

Revised 2026-10-12 by the owner (2.1.4.8): the replaced certificate is not revoked; it expires on its own. The
revocation step and its failure handling were removed the same day; the immediate re-issue stays (1.5.1.7).

### 2.3.1.21.1 An image update returned before Keycloak was back behind Postgres

The image-update test failed all five Keycloak checks: when it reached Keycloak, its running image read empty. The
Postgres steps just before restart `postgres.service`, and Keycloak's unit `Requires=` it
(`deploy/service_units.py`), so systemd restarts Keycloak too; `images/switch_image._apply` waited only for the
services it moved, so `fabricctl images update postgres` returned while Keycloak was still starting (15 s delay and
its boot), and status showed it not running. A user saw the same: an update reported healthy with the sign-in down
for a minute or two. Fixed: `images/constants.py` `DEPENDENTS` names Keycloak behind Postgres, and `_apply` waits
for every installed dependent of what it restarted to be healthy again (900 s), failing into the usual rollback if
one is not (1.14.2). WSL's Docker restarted on its own after the run, which ended the kept sandbox; the image-update
test is run again on a new kept sandbox.

That fix moved the failure to Postgres: the re-run (2026-10-13, sandbox 222/0) failed all five Postgres checks, and
the audit log read `keycloak, which needs postgres, did not become healthy again (unhealthy)`. Waiting for systemd's
cascade read the old Keycloak container, already marked unhealthy after losing its database, before the restarted
one replaced it, and `wait_healthy` stops at the first "unhealthy". Fixed the second time: `_apply` restarts each
dependent itself through `start_unit` once what it needs is healthy, health-gated like the moved services, so the
status it reads is the new container's. Docker's restarts in WSL came from WSL idling out between commands, with no
keepalive running.

### 2.3.1.21.2 A fresh install from setup's Advanced menu gave the DC the host's own name

The owner's clean install on the test Pi (2026-10-13: uninstall, `apt install fabricctl` from testing, interactive
`fabricctl setup` choosing Advanced) stopped about three minutes in, after Step-CA and the DNS server: no Postgres,
Keycloak, Samba or nginx, so no landing page or web console, while the timers it had installed kept failing on the
missing services. Setup run again unattended with the same answers refused at once: `dc_name: 'temp-ub' is this
host's own name, which the host takes in the domain as a member (2.1.6.51)`. The saved `vars.yaml` held
`dc_name: temp-ub`.

Cause: the Advanced menu shows the DC's name as `dc-<hostname> (suggested)` but Proceed did not save it (it does
the AD domain's), so the settings had none; the first apply then ran `keep_old_install`, which reads a missing
`dc_name` as an install from before 0.7.0 and keeps the host's name for the DC (2.1.6.45). On a fresh install that
is exactly the name 2.1.6.51 forbids, and the host's domain join refused it. The tests never met it: the suites
set `dc_name` or take the guided question, which saves it.

Fixed: `keep_old_install` keeps the host's name only where the domain already exists on this host
(`samba/data/.fabric-provisioned`; setup's `upgrade_vars` and every apply pass it), and the menu's Proceed saves
the suggestion (1.6.5.23). Tests: `tests/render.py` (a fresh install without `dc_name` keeps nothing and renders
`dc-<hostname>`), `tests/consent/run.py` (Proceed with the DC item untouched saves `dc-<hostname>`).

Seen on the same Pi before the uninstall, on the 0.7.0 development install it replaced: Postgres logged
`role "keycloak" is not permitted to log in` every ten seconds. That install is gone; the clean install's run
checks for it again (`fabric-db-rotate` last ran there on 2026-10-08). The host suite's fresh install
(2026-10-14) logged it 5 times, then not again, and so did rehearsal 2's Advanced install: all within the 40 seconds
after setup's `DB_ROTATE role=keycloak taken over` (OpenBao taking the role over, 2.1.7.4), while Keycloak retries;
setup finishes and doctor passes. Cause found the same day: see 2.3.1.21.4.

### 2.3.1.21.3 The rehearsal: a host name of 24 characters or more broke the main zone; the installer test was stale

The first rehearsal (2.1.1.54, 2026-10-14) on the purged test Pi from apt testing: the host suite (unattended)
23/0; the installer test (`tests/host/installer.py`, Guided then Advanced) 22/15. Two causes:

- **A real bug:** both runs stopped at the bootstrap with `zone home.arpa does not load: db.home.arpa:24: unknown RR
  type '192.168.5.57'`. The test's 25-character host name, refused before 0.7.0 and valid since (a host name may be any
  DNS label, 2.1.6.45), met `zone.j2`, which padded each name to 24 characters with nothing after it: the line read
  `averyveryverylonghostnameA  192.168.5.57`. Any long record name did the same, a console-added one included. Fixed:
  `zone.j2` and `reverse-zone.j2` pad to 23 and always add a space, so a name up to 23 characters renders as before
  and a longer one stays apart. Test: `tests/render.py` (a 25-character A, a 40-character CNAME, a 30-character PTR).
- **A stale test:** the installer test had not run since 0.6.x. 0.7.0 put the DC's name at item 5 of setup's menu, so
  every number after it moved on and the test's 14 (the ports), 6 (the first admin) and 16 (the host's own rules)
  touched the wrong items; and its long host name, now valid, must be over 63 characters to be refused. Updated, and it
  now checks the route of 2.3.1.21.2: Advanced with the DC's item left alone saves `dc-<hostname>`.

After the run the owner's 80 and 443 were removed by hand (the owner keeps 9090, 2026-10-14), and with them
`9090/tcp` from the LAN and `OpenSSH`, taken then for the installer test's own: wrongly, the test adds no rules (it
reads the host's), so they were most likely rules an earlier uninstall put back. The owner is asked whether to
restore them; 9090 from anywhere and fabric's SSH rule from the LAN cover both meanwhile.

### 2.3.1.21.4 Postgres's health probe signed in as the role setup locks

Every fresh install logged `role "keycloak" is not permitted to log in` in Postgres, from about the moment setup's
`dbrotation` step handed Keycloak's database password to OpenBao (2.1.7.4); the 0.7.0 development install on the
test Pi logged it every ten seconds for days. Keycloak was not the one refused: it runs as its own role,
`keycloak_db`, from the take-over on. Postgres's own container health probe ran `pg_isready -U keycloak` every ten
seconds, and `keycloak` is the bootstrap role the take-over locks (NOLOGIN); `pg_isready` counts any answer as up, so
the probe stayed healthy and only the log showed it. Fixed: the probe signs in as `keycloak_db_user` once it is set
(the bootstrap role before the take-over), so the take-over's apply re-renders Postgres's compose file and restarts
it once, before the bootstrap role is locked. Tests: `tests/render.py` (the probe's role before and after). And, at
the owner's word, setup's `ready` step now waits for Postgres's log to be quiet before it says it is ready
(2.1.2.25; `tests/setup/wait_ready_test.py`).

### 2.3.1.21.5 CI on the pull request: two failures that did not repeat

- **The sandbox (amd64), on `03fea576`:** 211/11, everything from `fabricctl uninstall` on (the export missing,
  containers and the package left; DNS gone). The same code passed the sandbox in CI's other runs and in four local
  runs (222/0), and the last commit changed only notes and a test. CI kept only the summary log, so the uninstall's
  own output could not be read: CI now keeps every sandbox log, none holding a secret of its run (3.1.2.4, owner
  2026-10-14). On `29d0ea0f` the sandbox passed on both
  architectures; a repeat would be read from those logs.
- **The keycloak suite (amd64), on `29d0ea0f`:** 52/1: the site's Windows baseline GPO held only its default entry,
  without the browsers' Kerberos allowlist for `sso.<domain>` (2.1.6.28); it passed in the seven other runs of the
  same code. A guess, not proven: two converges writing the baseline (`src/containers/samba/converge.py`), the later
  one without `sso_host`. Kerberos sign-in is off by default (2.1.6.32). To investigate in 0.8.0.

## 2.3.1.22 The 0.7.2 release candidate: BIND's restart fix

Decision 2.1.1.57. Built 2026-10-15 on `rc/0.7.2` from `main` at `929e462a` (0.7.1).

**The bug** (found by 0.8.0's sandbox, 2026-10-15): before BIND restarts (an apply that changes its image or its
configuration, or setup's deploy), each changed zone was frozen and its new file installed as soon as `rndc freeze`
answered. BIND writes the zone's journal into the file after it answers, so when the journal held changes (names
registered by DHCP or RFC2136), BIND's copy of the old zone replaced the new file a moment later; the restart served
the old zone, and a changed record was missing until a later live swap of that zone. Reproduced by hand in the
sandbox: frozen, the file copied in at once, BIND overwrote it within 2 seconds when its journal was newer than the
file.

**The fix:** `dns/freeze_and_install` waits up to 3 seconds for BIND's write when the zone's journal is newer than its
file (the only case in which BIND has something to write), then installs the new file. Used by
`deploy/finish_without_start` and `deploy/restart_changed`. Test: `tests/dns/freeze_and_install_test.py` (the `zone`
suite).

| Run | Where | Result |
|---|---|---|
| every suite (`tests/run-all.sh`, 21 suites; the `zone` suite with `freeze_and_install_test.py`) | WSL | all passed |
| the sandbox, kept (`41a914b2`) | WSL | 222 passed, 0 failed |
| the image-update test on it (the images as 0.7.1 pins them, unchanged: `source_hash.py --check`) | WSL | 58 passed, 0 failed |
| apt testing (`package.yml`, run 38817093940) | CI | `0.7.2~git20261015222705.41a914b` published |
| the owner's test: the 0.7.1 acceptance install upgraded in place to 0.7.2 from apt testing, then `sudo fabricctl setup` | test Pi | passed (the owner, 2026-10-17: "its good"). The scripted upgrade test (`tests/host/upgrade.sh`) and the rehearsal were not run: the owner tested the upgrade from 0.7.1 by hand on their own install |

### 2.3.1.23.2 The setup wizard without DHCP: what to bring back in 0.9.0

On 0.8.0 the wizard offers and writes no DHCP or address-manager setting (2.1.1.58; 1.8.5.10, 2.3.8.1). Left out,
all present on `dev/0.9.0-dhcp`:

  (`read_settings._first_subnet`, from `ipam/planned_subnets`), form fields (`form_from_settings`,

- `plan_settings`' DHCP turn-on check and its `networks` argument; `apply_settings`' DHCP write, its firewall refresh
  after DHCP (`refresh_firewall`) and its `networks` and `firewall` arguments; `plan_is_current`'s DHCP rule;
- `settings_overview`'s `interfaces` and `dhcp_first_subnet` (`ipam/common/plan_holds_subnets`);
- the dev preview's DHCP sample rows; the DHCP cases of `tests/settings/run.py` and `console.py` (here replaced by
  Fluent Bit cases, with a check that a file's `install_kea` and `dhcp` are ignored as console only).

When 0.9.0 merges `dev/0.8.0`, keep its versions of these files and drop this branch's check that `install_kea` is
not a wizard key.

*Brought back (0.9.0's port, area 8, 2026-10-23):* all of the above except `set_dhcp_on`'s split (ported with
area 4) and the old branch's log-on policies (not 0.8.0's); the 0.8.0 checks that DHCP is not the wizard's are
replaced by DHCP cases. See 1.8.5.10 and 2.3.10.11.14.
