## 2.3.1.24 The D5 build: the move to 0.8.0 (2026-10-20)

Step D5 of [1.6.12.15](../volume_1_description_and_architecture/1.6.12-domain-management.md#161215-build-order) on
`dev/0.8.0`, in steps, each with its suites green: setup's import of a 0.7.x settings file (2.1.1.60, 3.3.5), the
release's move test, and the 0.7.x upgrade code retired (2.1.1.61). It is the move's work, chapters 1.1 and 3.3, so
its note is here rather than with D1 to D4's in 2.3.6.

### 2.3.1.24.1 Setup takes a 0.7.x settings file as it is

- **The one table:** `src/fabriclib/setup/common/retired_settings.py` (`RETIRED_SETTINGS`, with why for each key),
  which 3.3.5.3 cites. `setup/drop_retired_settings.py` takes them out of the `--file` (or a checkout's
  `custom-vars.yaml`) before anything else reads it; `collect_vars` prints each with why and writes the list to the
  audit log as `SETUP_IMPORT`.
- **A 0.7.x file** is one holding a key only 0.7.x wrote (`OLD_FILE_MARKS`: the id settings, `logon_policies`, the
  site OUs, `webui_admin_group`; every 0.7.x vars.yaml has them all). From such a file `dc_name`, `image_pins` and
  `image_rolled_back` are ignored too, and the entries of `ldap_groups` that are exactly 0.7.x's own groups
  (`OLD_DEFAULT_GROUPS`): 0.7.x's `admins` has the gid 0.8.0 gives `directory-admins`, so keeping it would have given
  two groups one gid. A group the admin added is kept.
- **The first Directory Administrator:** unattended, `ask_first_admin` now names every key it refuses in one message
  (it named the first only): a 0.7.1 file has 0.7.1's default `webui_admin_user: fabric-admin` (the Enterprise
  Admin's name in 0.8.0) and no first or last name. Interactive, those keys are asked and only those.
- **Refused, nothing changed:** a file that is not YAML, holds no map, is a wizard export (`fabric_settings:`, which
  `fabricctl settings import` takes) or has none of fabric's keys (`setup/common/read_vars_file.py`; fabric's keys are
  the template's top-level keys, as the entry-point inventory reads them, and the retired ones).
- **What the rest of the file does:** the rest of a 0.7.x vars.yaml is the rendered settings, which 0.8.0's template
  takes as it takes its own (an in-place re-run reads its rendered vars.yaml the same way). The pure test renders the
  imported file with 0.8.0 and a fresh install from the same answers: they are identical.

### 2.3.1.24.2 What the build settled where the design was silent (asked in the build's report)

1. `dc_name`, `image_pins` and `image_rolled_back` are ignored only from a 0.7.x file: in a newer file `dc_name` is
   still refused for a new install (2.1.6.65) and the pins are an install's own.
2. 0.7.x's own groups in `ldap_groups` are ignored, a group the admin added kept (the design keeps "the parts chosen
   and their settings" and makes the domain new; it names no group).
3. The ignored list goes to the audit log (`SETUP_IMPORT`), the log fabric already keeps for changes.
4. A file with none of fabric's keys is refused; a fabric file with some keys fabric does not know is taken as before.
5. The committed example is made by 0.7.1's own code (`tests/setup/make_vars_071.py`, its `render_vars` over its
   template), as an install's apply writes it.

**Approved 2026-10-20 by the owner** ("yes to all"), as built: `dc_name` and the image pins are ignored only when the
file carries a 0.7.x key; 0.7.x's default `ldap_groups` are dropped only when they match exactly; the ignored list
goes to the audit log only; and the fourth and fifth as above. Nothing in the code changes.

### 2.3.1.24.3 Tests

`tests/setup/import_old_vars_test.py` (pure, `consent` suite), against `tests/setup/vars-0.7.1.yaml`: the table,
the import (the ignored keys listed, logged, kept the zones, records, TSIG keys' rights and parts, the render equal to
a fresh install's, 0.8.0's settings check passed), what 0.8.0 adds (refused unattended naming every key, asked
interactively, accepted with the keys added) and the refusals (damaged, a list, empty, a wizard export, a compose
file). Results of the suites: below, with each step.

### 2.3.1.24.4 The move test, and the upgrade test from 0.8.0

- **The move test**, `tests/sandbox/move.sh` (2.1.1.60's "a 0.7.1 install's settings file, imported into a fresh
  0.8.0, gives the same zones, records, keys' rights and parts"): a sandbox of its own (`fabric-move`, network
  `10.78.0.0/24`, its own Docker volume; the sandbox suite's lock, so never beside it). 0.7.1's package is built from
  the tag `v0.7.1` by 0.7.1's own packaging (a shared clone in the test's output folder, nothing downloaded but the
  images) and set up by 0.7.1's own setup with two zones, three records, two TSIG keys with their rights and the
  optional parts; `move_state.py` reads what is kept and made new before and after; then 3.3.5.4 is followed:
  `uninstall --export`, the candidate's package, `setup --file` with the copied vars.yaml (refused unattended naming
  the first administrator's three keys, then accepted with them). A key's rights are checked for real (RFC2136 with
  its secret: its name taken, another refused), and the old secret refused after the move.
- **Wired** into the release: 3.14.1.4 step 4 and the `/rc` skill's step 5 (0.8.0 only), replacing the upgrade test
  from 0.7.1; from 0.8.1 the upgrade test runs `FROM=0.8.0` (step 6).
- **`tests/host/upgrade.sh`** takes `FROM` 0.8.0 and later (`sort -V` against `FLOOR=0.8.0`) and refuses an older one,
  or one that is not a version, before the host is reached, saying 0.7.x moves (3.3.5); `FROM_CHECK_ONLY=1` stops
  after the check (`tests/host/upgrade_from_test.sh`, in the `consent` suite). Its 0.6 and 0.7 checks went (the
  admin role's rename of 0.6.4, AdGuard Home's retirement in 0.7), and `upgrade_state.py` seeds the blocked name
  through the resolver's `dns_filter_block`. Its `FROM` install now names the first Directory Administrator, with a
  password made on the host (root only, never on a command line), as 0.8.0's setup needs.

### 2.3.1.24.5 The upgrade code from before 0.8.0, retired

Every path that upgraded state from before 0.8.0 was looked for in the code, templates, service units, tests and the
manual (2.1.1.61). What had no value is deleted, each listed in the stale-code register's removed table
(2.3.1.5.2, 2026-10-20 rows); what might have value is kept and registered for the owner (2.3.1.5.1, S12 to S17). The
manual's topics that described the moves are removed (1.12.2.12, 1.12.3.11), and the sentences elsewhere that named
the retired code are rewritten (1.2.4, 1.2.9.8, 1.5.1.5, 1.6.2, 1.7.1, 3.3.1, 4.12.1).

Kept, though they name older releases, because 0.8.0's own installs use them: `upgrade_vars`' images (every upgrade
from 0.8.0), the template's merge of an install's `service_users` and `ldap_groups`, `deploy_config`'s removal of a
checkout's `/usr/local/bin` wrapper (a checkout install moving to the package), `mint_service_certs._crl_moved` (a
certificate naming another CRL point than the current one), `init_pki._single_intermediate`, and the refusals of
input from before 0.8.0 (a joiner older than 0.8.0, a DC name that is not `dc-NNNN`, an id range that is not a DC's).

One behaviour changes for 0.8.0 itself: a `*_mem_limit` equal to a value one held by default before 0.7.1 (e.g.
`kea_mem_limit: 128m`) was read as unset; it is now the admin's, as any other value.

### 2.3.1.24.6 Found while testing

  /opt/stepca/data/certs/root_ca.crt not found`): `deploy_fluentbit` copies fabric's root CA before the PKI step has
  made it. 0.7.1 does the same (the move test's first run, both releases). Not D5's: the move test leaves Fluent Bit
  off; asked in the build's report. **Fixed in 0.8.0** (the owner, 2026-10-20): 2.3.1.25.4.
- **The move test's own settings first broke the main zone**: its `dns.dynamic_zone_var` replaced the default entry
  without `zone_authority: true`, so `ns.<domain>` had no address and BIND refused the zone (0.7.1 and 0.8.0 alike;
  the import carried it faithfully). The test now keeps `zone_authority`.

### 2.3.1.25.4 Fluent Bit at a fresh install

Found by the move test's first run (2.3.1.24.6): with `install_fluentbit: true` a fresh install stopped at the deploy
step, which runs before the PKI step (3.3.1's steps), because `deploy_fluentbit` copies fabric's root CA for every
destination without a `ca_file` of its own. The owner: fix it in 0.8.0 ("yes to all", 2026-10-20).

- **Built:** `logs/fluentbit_pending.py` says when Fluent Bit must wait: it is on, a destination needs fabric's CA,
  and Step-CA was never initialised (no `ca.json`, no root CA). The deploy then leaves it, saying so; setup's start
  step, after the PKI step, writes its files (restarting it when they changed) before the units start. An initialised
  CA whose root is missing is still refused, as is a destination's own missing `ca_file`; `deploy_fluentbit` now
  checks its CA files before it writes anything.
- **Tests:** `tests/fluentbit/run.py`: a fresh base waits, Fluent Bit off or every destination with its own CA does
  not; an initialised CA without its root is refused naming the file, nothing written. The sandbox installs with
  `install_fluentbit: true` and checks the deploy's message, `fluentbit` running and its CA file. The move test
  keeps Fluent Bit off: 0.7.1's own setup, which it runs first, still stops with it on.

## 2.3.1.26 The later milestones' review, and the owner's answers (2026-10-22)

A read-only review of 0.9.0 to 1.0 against 0.8.0's new directory model, so their frameworks can be built before their
labs are ready. Its findings, in brief:

- **0.9.0 DHCP:** built on `dev/0.9.0-dhcp`, 73 commits behind `dev/0.8.0`; a merge conflicts in about 130 files, and
  its address plan's storage and access assume site OUs, `OU=organisation` and site admins (2.1.10.12, .16, .23, .28,
  .30). Ported by area onto a new `dev/0.9.0` from `dev/0.8.0`, not merged.
- **0.10.0 802.1X:** on `dev/0.10.0-8021x`, which holds the DHCP work too, 86 commits behind; its role scoping and
  2.1.9.21â24 contradict the tiers (2.1.6.68). Re-cut onto the new 0.9.0.
- **0.11.0 HA:** least designed (1.9.2.1.1.1 "to be designed"; 2.1.6.59 proposed). Design first.
- **0.12.0:** designed, nothing built; the offline bundles started 2026-10-22 on `dev/0.12.0-bundles`.
- **1.0:** the export (2.1.1.56) undesigned; S8.6's grants in Postgres (2.1.9.14) duplicate 0.8.0's AD grant store.
- **A schema clash:** class OID 7 is `fabricDcInfo` on `dev/0.8.0` and `fabricVlanRecord` on the DHCP and 802.1X
  branches; schema OIDs are permanent once a domain has them.

The owner's answers, 2026-10-22, "all as recommended", each to be recorded as a decision with its milestone's
redesign:
1. The DHCP and 802.1X classes are renumbered after 0.8.0's (`fabricDcInfo` keeps 7): 0.8.0 ships first.
2. A site's address block lives on the site's record in `CN=fabric,CN=System`.
3. The address plan, DHCP options and scan policy are edited through grants (network-admins and the Directory
   Administrators); the organisation's options move out of `OU=organisation`.
4. Device roles are domain-wide, limited by grants or the NAS's site.
5. 2.1.9.21â24 (layered roles, the site helpdesk) are dropped as replaced by 2.1.6.68; a helpdesk is a grant template.
6. The RODC items (2.1.6.55, .57, .58), SSH certificates (2.1.7.13, .14) and SCEP are re-keyed to grants, in 0.10.0.
7. DHCP and 802.1X are tested on the Hyper-V lab, extended with a router and an authenticator (2.1.1.64; HA too, the
   owner having the room).
8. HA's open points (1.9.2, 2.1.6.59) are settled before any HA code; Kea HA and BIND-secondary spikes may start now.
9. 0.12.0's Cloudflare work is tested against Pebble and a recorded stand-in, with one live run.
10. 1.0: the export is designed (contents, key, restore), whether sign-in factors are in 1.0 is decided, and 2.1.9.14 is
    revised so federation uses `CN=grants`.

## 2.3.1.27 The Hyper-V lab's first unattended build (2026-10-22 and 23)

The agent built gaming-23's lab (3.1.5; 2.1.1.63 to 2.1.1.65) after the owner's one admin step and sign-out, with
Hyper-V Administrators' rights only. All ten VMs answered SSH with the lab key and have the checkpoint `clean`.

| VM | Install time | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `g23-vm-rtr-1` | about 20 min | routes 10.88.0.0/24 and VLANs 10, 20, 30 out of the Default Switch; DNS works through it |
| `g23-vm-ub24-1` | 13 min | |
| `g23-vm-ub26-1` | 10 min | `lsb_release -cs` is `resolute` (the bundles' assumption, 2.3.14.3) |
| `g23-vm-ub26-2` | 12 min | |
| `g23-vm-ub24-2` | 13 min | |
| `g23-vm-auth-1` | 13 min | |
| `g23-vm-kub26-1` | 51 min | Ubuntu Server plus `kubuntu-desktop`: about 1,500 packages |
| `g23-vm-pve9-1` | 4 min | |
| `g23-vm-ws25-1` | 13 min | after the key fix below |
| `g23-vm-w11-1` | 68 min | after the key fix below |

What the first run found, each fixed in `scripts/lab/` and 3.1.5.3:
1. Windows PowerShell 5.1 turns a native command's stderr into an error under `$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'`, so
   `install_vm.ps1`'s first refused SSH try threw instead of waiting. The SSH calls now run under `Continue`.
2. Ubuntu's live installer runs its own SSH server at the VM's address; the first try recorded its host key, and the
   installed system's (other) key was then refused as changed. The wait now records no host key; the installed
   system's is recorded once `labadmin` gets in. The router's new key was checked against its console first.
3. Eight Enter presses over 12 seconds missed both Windows ISOs' *Press any key* prompt; the VMs fell into PXE and
   timed out. Forty presses over 20 seconds catch it.
4. Windows Setup restarts several times; with the DVD first in the boot order each restart meets the prompt again.
   The boot order is set to the disk right after the presses; Hyper-V takes it while the VM runs.

## 2.3.1.28 The tests leave the owner's real names: test.lan.j-j.family (2026-10-24)

The owner, 2026-10-24: the tests use `test.lan.j-j.family` instead of `lan.j-j.family`, the owner's real domain.
Found by the keycloak suite's Kerberos failures (2.3.6.23): a container on the owner's machine resolves the owner's
real names, so a test that used them could reach the owner's network.

- **Checked first:** on WSL and in a container, `test.lan.j-j.family`, `sso.`, `dc1.`, `fabric-ctl.`, `ad.` under it
  and a random label (`x7q9z.test.lan.j-j.family`, no wildcard) resolve to nothing; `sso.lan.j-j.family` does (the
  owner's host). The tests' AD domain, `ad.j-j.family`, is the owner's real AD (it resolves, with its `_ldap._tcp`
  SRV records); it moves too, to `ad.lan.j-j.family`, which is what setup suggests for `test.lan.j-j.family`
  (`samba/suggested_ad_domain`: `ad.` plus the domain without its first label) and resolves to nothing. The realm
  follows (`AD.LAN.J-J.FAMILY`); the NetBIOS name stays `AD` (the AD domain's first label); host names, SPNs
  (`HTTP/sso.test.lan.j-j.family`, 28 characters) and certificate names stay well inside their limits; the base DN
  follows (`dc=test,dc=lan,dc=j-j,dc=family`).
- **Changed:** `tests/render.py` (the shared install's vars and every name its checks expect), `tests/keycloak/run.py`,
  `tests/freeradius/run.py`, `tests/hardening/run.sh`, and the samba suite's `start_dc.py` (its default domain and
  AD domain), `site_join.py` (each site under the test domain) and `run.py` (the AD domain suggestion's example).
  The other suites (the sandbox, webui, zones, resolver, kea, federation) already used names of their own
  (`lan.test`, `example.test` and the like). Not changed: the manual's examples of a real deployment (1.4.1, 1.6.3,
  2.1.6.11's, 2.3.5's wildcard) and `suggested_ad_domain`'s docstring, which describe the owner's own install; the

  `render`'s output); `freeradius` 87 passed, 0 failed; `samba` passed (170 in `dc.py`, every file green; a first run

## 2.3.1.29 A same-size change within one mtime tick was never copied: filecmp's cache (2026-10-24)

Found on `dev/0.9.0` (its 2.3.10.11.19, 24a236c9: a "flaky" `copy_tree_with_perms` test was this bug), and on `main`
and `dev/0.8.0` too. `filecmp.cmp(a, b, shallow=False)` keeps a cache keyed by each file's (type, size, mtime) and
answers from it when both signatures match, so a rewrite of the same size whose mtime did not move (two writes inside
one tick of the file system's clock, or a copy that kept the old mtime) read as unchanged and the deploy kept the old
file, saying nothing. Seen here too: two equal files, then one rewritten with the same size and its mtime set back,
and `filecmp.cmp` still answered True.

- **Fixed:** `common/same_content` compares the sizes, then every byte, on every call (`dev/0.9.0`'s
  `_same_content`, the same code). It has four callers, so it is its own file (Rule 1): `common/copy_tree_with_perms`
  (trees: fabric's code, web assets, service config), `common/copy_if_changed` (compose files, units, nginx.conf),
  `federation/deploy_federation_endpoint` (its unit), and `pki/publish_ca_certs` (fabric's CA in the host's trust
  store), the fourth found while fixing the three named. No `filecmp` is left in `src/`.
- **Tests both ways,** in the `consent` suite: `tests/common/same_content_test.py`,
  `copy_tree_with_perms_test.py`, `copy_if_changed_test.py` and `tests/federation/deploy_federation_endpoint_test.py`
  (as root, its unit path a temporary file): a same-size change in the same mtime tick is copied and reported; an
  mtime-only change is not. `publish_ca_certs` runs `update-ca-certificates` on the host when its trust files change,
  so no test calls it; its comparison is `same_content`, tested above.
- **Merging `dev/0.9.0`:** its `copy_tree_with_perms` keeps `_same_content` private; this branch's imports
  `common/same_content` instead, so that file meets a small conflict: keep the import. Its test file also checks
  bytecode caches (`dev/0.9.0`'s efa63059, not on this branch); keep its checks and these.
- **Results (2026-10-25), each suite alone:** `lint`, `consent` (the four new tests: 9, 6, 5 and 4 passed, 0
  failed), `deploy`, `render`, `pki` and `federation` passed; the sandbox 291 passed, 0 failed.

## 2.3.1.30 Two worktrees' suite runs replaced each other's containers: per-run names (2026-10-25)

The keycloak suite's GPO check failed now and then while another worktree's agent ran suites (2.3.6.24): six runs alone
passed (68 passed, 0 failed each), and every suite named its containers and networks the same in every worktree
(`kc-dc`, `bind9`, `sambatest_net` on 10.254.x), removing any container by that name with `docker rm -f` before it
started. So a run in one worktree could remove, or reuse, the other's containers mid-run. Only the samba and web UI
suites could be told apart (`SAMBA_TEST_ID`, `WEBUI_TEST_ID`), and only when someone set them.

- **Fixed (the owner, 2026-10-25: "give every container suite per-run names"):** `tests/common/test_run.py` (3.1.2.6)
  gives each worktree a run id, names, subnets (`10.<100-199>`), host ports and an output folder of its own, labels
  what a run makes, and removes only what carries its label: never a container a run did not create. Every container
  suite uses it: keycloak, freeradius, kea, zones, resolver, samba, webui, federation, ntp, openbao, fluentbit, pki,
  images, nginx and hardening. Network creation refuses a subnet that overlaps one this run did not make.
- **Product names:** suites that start fabric's containers under the names the product reads (`bind9`, `postgres`,
  `openbao`, `samba`, `step-ca`, ...) keep them and take one machine-wide lock instead (3.1.2.6.4), as the sandbox
  does. Renaming them would mean making those names settable in the product.
- **Not covered:** a branch without this change (e.g. `dev/0.9.0` until it merges it) still names and removes as
  before, so a run there can still remove this branch's product-named containers; its own fixed names cannot clash with
  this branch's per-run ones.
- **The openbao suite's hang:** a stick's key write ends with a flush before the unmount (`vault/common/mounted_stick`).
  It ran a plain `sync`, which waits on every file system the host has; in WSL it stuck in the kernel's
  `super_lock` for over 30 minutes with the test's loop-device stick mounted, and blocked the whole distribution until
  it was restarted (the owner's coordinator restarted it). The flush is now `sync -f <mount point>`, the stick's own
  file system only, which is also right on a real host (a plain `sync` there waits on network shares too). The test
  now unmounts and detaches its stick images' loop devices when it exits, also part way, so none outlives a run. One
  loop device of the stuck run (`/dev/loop0`, autoclear set, no holder left) stays until WSL's kernel restarts.
- **Results (2026-10-25), each suite alone:** `lint`, `consent` (`test_run_test.py`: 22 passed, 0 failed),
  `keycloak`, `freeradius`, `kea`, `resolver`, `webui`, `federation` and `ntp`; then, after the zones fix (DHCP's
  reverse zones named from the old fixed prefix) and the flush fix, `zones`, `openbao`, `fluentbit`, `pki`, `images`,
  `render` + `nginx`, `hardening` (17 passed) and `samba` (every part, 0 failures): all passed; then the sandbox
  alone, 291 passed, 0 failed. The docs suite passed.

## 2.3.1.31 The tests' AD domain moves to ad.test.lan.j-j.family (2026-10-25)

2.3.1.28 moved the tests' AD domain to `ad.lan.j-j.family`, what setup suggests for `test.lan.j-j.family`. The owner
(2026-10-25): "ad would collide with my production one", so it is `ad.test.lan.j-j.family`, a sub-domain of the tests'
own domain, which 1.6.3 allows.

- **Checked first:** it resolves to nothing, from WSL, from a container and from Windows (NXDOMAIN under the owner's
  `lan.j-j.family` zone, no A and no `_ldap._tcp` SRV records; no wildcard).
- **Name lengths:** the realm is `AD.TEST.LAN.J-J.FAMILY` (22 characters); the longest host name the tests make,
  `dc-pi-core.ad.test.lan.j-j.family`, is 33, so DNS names, SPNs (`HTTP/â¦`, `host/â¦`) and certificate CNs stay well
  inside 64; computer names are unchanged (at most 15). The NetBIOS name stays `AD`, the domain's first label.
- **Where:** `tests/samba/start_dc.py`'s default (every suite starting a test DC through it: samba, keycloak,
  freeradius) and `tests/render.py`. `tests/samba/run.py` still checks that setup suggests `ad.lan.j-j.family` for

## 2.3.1.32 0.8.0's candidate (v0.8.0-rc.4): its runs and findings (2026-10-25)

`/rc 0.8.0` (2.1.1.40, 3.14.1.4) under the feature freeze (2.1.1.70). `rc/0.8.0` (the old DNS-filter candidate's
branch, an ancestor of `main`) was fast-forwarded to `main` and `dev/0.8.0` merged into it (df56cbc8): `main`'s rename
(2.1.1.59, PR #23) and `dev/0.8.0`'s redo of it (2.1.1.68) are one decision, so 2.1.1.59 is kept as replaced by
2.1.1.68; `main`'s `apt policy` check before setup (3.3.1.1) is kept. The tags `v0.8.0-rc.1` to `rc.3` are the old
candidate's, so this one is `v0.8.0-rc.4` (2.1.1.58); image tags carry the workflow's run number and apt is rebuilt
from scratch on each publish, so nothing it publishes collides with them.

**Runs:**
- Gate and every suite but the sandbox (`tests/run-all.sh`, WSL): all 22 passed, on df56cbc8.
- Images published from `rc/0.8.0` as `0.8.0-rc.270` (images workflow run 270, every job passed) and pinned, after
  finding 1; then `docs`, `lint`, `images` and `webui` again: passed.

**Findings:**
1. **The images moved to `fabric-ctl`'s path on their own.** The images workflow pushes to `ghcr.io/<repository>`,
   so since the rename it publishes to `ghcr.io/archdukejim/fabric-ctl/<name>`, while the lock, the pin script and
   every host still read `ghcr.io/archdukejim/open-fabric/<name>`: pinning refused (`not found`). Decision 2.1.14.26
   (the owner, 2026-10-23, recorded on `dev/0.13.0-bundles`) already says the first release signed from `fabric-ctl`
   moves the path and the signer, and this is it. **Fixed:** the lock's `registry` and the build's default registry
   moved to `fabric-ctl`, and the signer trusts `fabric-ctl`'s workflow only (every image 0.8.0 pins is signed there);
   `tests/images/published.py` now refuses the old name. The image-update test's previous fabric images (0.7.2's, at
   the old path under the old signer) are skipped for this release, with the reason; the upstream ones are still
   tested. 2.1.14.26 is copied here with its status.
2. **The host suite's unattended setup had no first Directory Administrator.** Since 2.1.6.69, `--non-interactive`
   needs `webui_admin_user`, `webui_admin_first_name` and `webui_admin_last_name` in the file; `tests/host/run.sh`
   gave none, so setup stopped at once on the test Pi (3 passed, 20 failed). The manual's checklist (3.2.1) still said
   the first admin defaults to the account that ran `sudo`. **Fixed:** the host suite names one (`host.admin`), the
   checklist lists the three settings and that unattended setup needs them; `tests/lab/dhcp.sh` gets them too.
3. **The host suite's plain sign-in still expected a person without a fabric role to be refused (403).** Since
   2.1.8.11 they sign in to My account only (the sandbox's strict sign-in already checks that). **Fixed:**
   `tests/sandbox/login_test.py`'s plain mode checks the same: signed in, the Overview sends them to `/account`, DNS
   refused (403).
4. **Once, on the Pi: a person's reset, applied right after their creation, did not run** (the Review came back with
   it still pending and no `PERSON_RESET` in the audit; the web UI's apply hit a broken pipe writing its 303, which
   is harmless). Not seen again on the rerun (23 passed, 0 failed) nor in the sandbox. Suspect: the grant compile's
   timer, which added the new person to a grant group in the same second, moved the person's version between the
   hold and the apply, so the apply held it again as "changed since" (the designed refusal, 1.8.6.5). The check now
   prints the Review's whole text when it fails. **Fixed** (e1d06a0f): the cause was that: a person fabric created
   was left out of their site's grants until the 5-minute timer (here it added them to `grant-desktops-who` one
   second after the reset was held). 1.6.13.4 already said fabric compiles at once for a change it makes, as
   `add_machine` does; `create_person` now does too. The `grants` suite checks it both ways: a person fabric creates
   keeps their version across the timer's compile, and a person made outside fabric is reached only by the timer.
5. **The interactive rehearsal's driver did not know 2.1.6.69's questions.** `tests/host/installer.py` waited for
   "First admin's user name", which setup no longer asks: each run sat 15 minutes, then failed (18 passed, 23
   failed). **Fixed:** it answers the first Directory Administrator's user name, first and last names and e-mail
   address (each refused first: `fabric-admin`, none, `<b>`, not an e-mail address), and checks the names saved.

**Runs (continued):**

  skipped: finding 1). Then the move test (0.7.1's settings into a fresh 0.8.0, 2.1.1.60): 21 passed, 0 failed.

  21 passed, 2 failed (findings 3 and 4), then 23 passed, 0 failed.

   Enter for none)") was never shown: 2.1.6.93 lets the person give one at setup. Found by the rehearsal (43 passed,
   1 failed). **Fixed:** interactively it is asked when the file gives none, Enter leaving it empty; unattended, a

7. **0.7.2's first setup on the lab stopped at Keycloak's restart** (two starts overlapping, the container "already

8. **`info.<domain>` is lost for good when a failed first install is rerun with the same `--file`.** The file's `dns:`
   replaces the stored zone and `hostname_landing` is already stored, so the alias is never added again (2.1.4.6
   adds it on the first render only); 0.8.0 had the same rule. **Fixed** (2.1.4.10, the owner, 2026-10-30): every
   render adds it until the install has once been ready (`install_was_ready`, recorded by setup's `ready` step);
   `tests/render.py` (added again while not ready, never once ready or on an older install; a value not true or
   false refused) and `tests/setup/wait_ready_test.py` (recorded when ready, not when the gate fails).
9. **A new record is not answered for up to three hours to anyone who asked for the name before:** the resolver
   caches the NXDOMAIN (the zone's negative TTL is a day, BIND caps it at 3 h), clients' resolvers too; the
   authoritative BIND has it at once. **Fixed** (2.1.4.9, the owner, 2026-10-28: "at most 30 seconds"): every zone
   fabric serves keeps "no such name" 30 s (an older install's DHCP subzone given it by the next apply, frozen), and
   dev/0.9.0's flush (2.1.10.46 (c)) is in 0.8.0: an apply and DNS' Apply make the resolver forget the changed
   names, a zone's add or removal the whole zone; a failed flush is a warning (1.4.1.6).
10. **Stale texts about who may sign in** (grants decide since 0.8.0): the Linux join's last line ("until grants
    decide who"), the certificate page ("a site admin's account", "the site's people log on"), 4.6.6.1, 4.6.5.4,
    4.6.7.1, 4.6.7.4 (a `<site> log-on rights` policy that no longer exists) and 4.6.7.5; 4.6.6.8 said restarting
    sssd reads a new sudo rule at once: it does not (tried: the rule appeared only after `sss_cache -E`). **Fixed:**
    the join's line, the page and the manual.
11. **A Linux machine joined with a Directory Administrator's password loses fabric's management a minute later:**
    its own `fabric-managed.timer` finds it not yet in `fabric-managed` (the root's hourly sort adopts it later) and
    removes the configuration the join just applied, for up to about two hours. **Fixed** (2.1.6.97, the owner,
    2026-10-30, both halves): the join adds the machine to `fabric-managed` itself, the sort records it as seen, and
    the machine's check keeps the configuration 2 hours when the join could not add it (2.3.6.25).
12. **A site in another network cannot reach the upstream to join:** the upstream's firewall opens fabric's ports to
    `lan_cidr` and `security.firewall_allow` only, and the overlap check makes every site another network; it took
    `firewall_allow` and `setup --step firewall --approve ports,firewall` (an apply does not re-open the ports).
    **Fixed for 0.8.0** (2.1.9.28 B, the owner, 2026-10-30): 4.9.1.4 and 3.9.1 say so, and a join that cannot reach
    the upstream at all names `security.firewall_allow` and the step (`tests/federation/run.py`: named for an upstream
    not answering, not for a TLS refusal). The invitation that opens the endpoint (A) is deferred.
13. **A joined site's last words named nobody:** "sign in â¦ as , with the password you chose in setup", and a login
    kit (its README, the root certificate) a joined site does not have. **Fixed:** the domain's Directory
    Administrators sign in, the kit is named only where it is (`tests/setup/print_first_steps_test.py`, in
    `consent`); 3.9.1 says so. The rerun below found the plan's half too ("Create the first Directory Administrator
    'None' (None None) â¦ login kit in ~/fabric-admin" on a joining site): the plan says it only where setup makes
    them (`first_admin_needed`; `consent` checks both ways).
14. **A joined DC registered fabric_net's gateway as its own address:** `samba-tool domain join` names the DC at every
    address of its interfaces, and those include `10.255.0.1` (2.1.2.15 keeps it out of the DC's later updates, not
    of the join); every site's gateway is `10.255.0.1`, so the root's DC replicating from `dc-0002` reached itself
    (`WERR_GEN_FAILURE`, never a success) and Kerberos to `dc-0003` by name failed half the time. **Fixed:** the
    image's join uses the LAN address alone, the gateway added to the interfaces after; the samba suite's read-only
    DC now joins with a second address and checks its name has the LAN address only (`samba`: passed). The image
    changes, so it is published again.

15. **Every purge (and `uninstall --export`) left the host's own computer account in the domain.** The export stops
    the stack for a cold copy, and the uninstall then removed the account (1.6.11.8), which needs OpenBao (the
    agent's secret) and the DC: both stopped, so it always failed. A single site's domain goes with its DC, but a
    joined site's account stayed at the root. **Fixed:** with an export, `run_uninstall_command` removes the account
    first, while the stack runs, and `uninstall` does not try again; without one, `uninstall` removes it as before
    (`tests/setup/run_uninstall_command_test.py`, in `consent`: both orders and a refused command that removes
    nothing; it fails on the old order). 1.6.11.8 says so. The refusal was also printed with a tick (â): it is a
    warning (!) now, checked both ways in the same test.

17. **A reinstall after a purge failed at setup's verify when the last install's daily renewal was a day old.**
    `fabric-certs.timer` is `Persistent=true`; uninstall disabled it but left systemd's stamp of its last run
    (`/var/lib/systemd/timers/stamp-fabric-certs.timer`), so the next install's enabling it fired the missed run at
    once, a minute before step-ca was started; the failed renewal was recorded and doctor stopped setup. Earlier
    rehearsals ran within a day of the last renewal, so the stamp was not yet missed. **Fixed** both ways: uninstall
    removes the stamps of fabric's timers (`tests/setup/uninstall_test.py`, in `consent`), as Rule 12 asks; and
    setup removes a timer's stamp when it installs that timer (its unit not there before:
    `tests/deploy/install_timers_test.py`, in `deploy`), because a purge runs the uninstall of the code the last
    setup deployed: a host purged by an older release (a 0.7 install moved to 0.8.0, 3.3.5) keeps its stamps.
    The two-sided fix confirmed by the owner on 2026-10-31 ("yes").
    Seen again: the Pi purged by d346bf2's deployed code after its stamp was set two days back kept it, and the
    host suite on 5a3e880 failed the same way (17 passed, 6 failed). 3.2.4.1 says so.
18. **The fabric host's own sssd sockets failed, and a purge left failed units.** Found by the coordinator on the
    test Pi after the rehearsal on 213d136 (2026-10-31): `sssd-nss`, `-pac`, `-pam` and `-sudo.socket` enabled and
    failed at 07:34 UTC ("consider either adjusting services' line or disabling the socket"): the fix of 2.1.6.95 was
    in the Linux join's script only, while the host's `member` step wrote the same `services` line and restarted sssd
    without touching the sockets. After the purge the four stayed failed, and `keycloak.service` was "not-found
    failed": at the uninstall its `compose up` exited 143 (systemd's SIGTERM after `ExecStop`, before it had ended),
    which its unit counted as a failure, and nothing cleared it once the unit file was gone (`fabric-machines` and
    `fabric-dns-lists` likewise, from runs while the stack was down). **Fixed:** the host's join masks the sockets
    from the one place the Linux join's script follows (`setup/common/sssd_sockets.py`; 2.1.6.95 revised), records
    how they were, and the undo puts them back with their failures cleared; the uninstall clears every fabric unit's
    failure (`systemctl reset-failed`, approved by the owner with this fix); every compose unit counts 143 at a stop
    as clean, as it does 130. The rehearsal of that fix (a99221f) then failed the host suite's new check after
    setup: `fabric-machines.service` was failed, its first run at 05:07 having found no directory ("the directory is
    not reachable"): `install_timers` enables the timers at deploy, and their first runs (machines, grants, dns-log)
    came while setup was still building the stack (grants and dns-log recovered at their next run, five minutes
    later; machines runs hourly). So every timer's job now has an `ExecCondition`: a run while `fabric-setup.service`
    is active or `fabric.target` is not (`fabricctl stop`) is skipped, not failed (its next run does the work).
    The owner kept it as part of this fix, with no decision of its own (2026-10-31: "fix"). dev/0.9.0's two DHCP
    timers (`fabric-dhcp-names`, `fabric-plan-watch`) took the same condition, and their stamps the same removal at
    an uninstall, when 0.8.0 was merged into it (2026-10-31). Tests:
    `tests/setup/common/sssd_sockets_test.py` and `tests/setup/uninstall_test.py` (in `consent`),
    `tests/deploy/install_timers_test.py` (the condition on every timer's job), and the host suite: no failed sssd or
    fabric unit after setup, after sssd and `sockets.target` start again (as at a boot), and after the purge. Not
    fabric's, left as they are: the test Pi's `logrotate.service` (cloud-init's duplicate log entry) and
    `fwupd-refresh.service`.
- **The rehearsal on 5074b8f** (3.14.1.4 step 4; CI on 5074b8f3 green: 81 passed, 7 skipped, the sandbox and the
  image-update test on amd64 and arm64; apt testing `fabricctl 0.8.0~git20261031102824.5074b8f`, package workflow run
  41192877539), on the test Pi from a purge that left no fabric timer stamps, `/opt/fabric`, `/etc/fabric` or failed
  sssd or fabric unit (four sssd sockets and `keycloak.service` that the earlier code left failed were cleared by hand
  first, `systemctl reset-failed`):
  - the host suite, purged at its end (`CLEANUP=1`): 27 passed, 0 failed, setup 11 min; its new checks passed: no
    failed sssd or fabric unit after setup, after sssd and `sockets.target` start again, and after the purge, the
    sockets unmasked again. The runs before it: 213d136, 23 passed (the host's sockets still failed: finding 18);
    a99221f, 25 passed, 2 failed (a timer's first run during setup: finding 18);
  - the installer test: 44 passed, 0 failed;
  - the landing page (`http://192.168.5.57/info/`, `info.<domain>`: 200) and the console (`fabric-ctl.<domain>` to
    its login, then Keycloak's sign-in; Keycloak's console at `sso.<domain>`), each certificate verified against the
    install's root CA;
  - left purged, only that `fabricctl` installed: no containers, `/opt/fabric`, `/etc/fabric`, fabric units or timer
    stamps, the sssd sockets enabled and not masked; `systemctl --failed` listed `logrotate.service` alone (the
    Pi's own, above).
  - The owner's answers of 2026-10-31: root renames warned of only (2.1.6.96); the DNS filter's 5-minute timer kept
    (2.1.12.10); finding 17's two-sided fix; a moved 0.7 install counted as ready (2.1.4.10); the timer runs skipped
    during setup kept in finding 18; DNS caching bounded to 5 minutes (2.1.12.11), in 0.9.0, since there is no 0.8.1 (2.1.1.71: its work goes into
    0.9.0, begun right after 0.8.0's release).
- **The owner's own test of 5074b8f** (2026-10-31, on the test Pi): to be recorded here.

## 2.3.1.33 The joined campaign for 0.9.0 (2026-11-01)

3.1.5.11, approved by 2.1.1.73; from Git Bash on gaming-23 (WSL could not run Windows programs that day), the lab's
rtr-1, ub24-1, ub26-2 and ws25-1 from `clean`, the floor `v0.8.0`'s `.deb` from its GitHub release and the branch's
from `d4b9cd57`.

- **Run 1, abandoned in its join step (no PASS/FAIL summary written).** The floor installed and its doctor passed;
  ub26-2 joined ("fabric-managed: applied"); then the Windows join waited for ever. Its PowerShell
  (`[Console]::In.ReadLine()`) never got the one-time password: Windows' OpenSSH hands a remote PowerShell only what is
  on standard input when the session opens, and the line came about four seconds later, when `add-machine` printed
  it (reproduced with a harmless line: sent at once it arrived, sent six seconds late it never did). ws25-1's
  NetSetup log showed no join attempt, and the account `g23-vm-ws25-1` was made at the DC. The run was stopped (its
  processes on gaming-23 ended), the four VMs put back to `clean`. Fixed in the script: the line is read on gaming-23
  first, kept in the shell's memory only, and the session opens with it (3.1.5.11 step 2); checked with the same
  harmless line.
- **Run 2, abandoned at its first check.** "FAIL fabric at the floor: doctor passes": 0.8.0's own setup stopped at
  `systemctl start postgres`, the first-start health bug 0.9.0 fixes (1.2.4.5, found by the dhcp campaign the same
  night). 0.8.0 has it, so a fresh 0.8.0 install on a slow disk can stop there (installed hosts are not affected: the
  bug is in a first start); setup's message says to run it again. The run was stopped and the VMs put back to
  `clean`; the script now runs setup again once when the floor's doctor fails, saying so (3.1.5.11 step 2).
- **Run 3, stopped in its images step (the checks before it could not be trusted).** The floor installed (the
  resume was not needed) and its doctor passed; ub26-2 joined; the upgrade's setup finished. But three checks were
  wrong, not the product: (a) ws25-1 had not joined: `Add-Computer -Options UnsecuredJoin,PasswordPass` with a
  PSCredential refuses because a credential carries a user name ("Machine password and the user account are passed.
  Error out.", 0x57, in its NetSetup log); (b) "a Kerberos ticket as the machine" asked `kinit -k "NAME$@REALM"`
  through the remote shell, which expanded `$@` to nothing; (c) "adcli testjoin" on the fabric host: setup installs
  adcli there only while the machine password is renewed (1.6.11.12). Fixed in the script: the Windows join through
  `Win32_ComputerSystem.JoinDomainOrWorkgroup` with no user and options `0xC1` (a wrong password then reached the
  DC's LDAP bind and was refused as invalid, so the call is right); the account's `$` escaped; adcli asked only where
  it is installed, the machine ticket being the proof elsewhere. The run was stopped and the VMs put back to `clean`.
- **What run 3 showed about the upgrade: nothing broke.** Every failure after the upgrade (the machine tickets on the
  fabric host and ub26-2, ws25-1's secure channel, adcli on the fabric host) had already failed in the same way at
  "the join (0.8.0)", before the upgrade ran: the same four checks, with the same results, on both sides of it. They
  were the three test faults above, not a join the upgrade broke: the principal "lost its `$`" in the remote shell
  only, the keytab still held `NAME$@REALM` (shown in the check's own label), and ws25-1 had never joined. A run of the
  fixed script, with the checks before and after the upgrade passing, is what proves it (run 5).
- **Run 4, stopped in its join step: Ubuntu's mirror, not the lab.** `apt-get update` on the fabric VM failed with
  "File has unexpected size (1407856 != 1407664). Mirror sync in progress?" from `us.archive.ubuntu.com`: the mirror
  was replacing its index files while the VM read them. The lab's network was up (the VM reached the mirror and got a
  file, of the wrong size), and nothing in the lab changed. But the script went on: `apt-get install` of the floor's
  `.deb` failed, then setup ("setup exit 1", fabricctl not installed) and the doctor check, all on a host with no
  fabric. Fixed (3.1.5.9, 3.1.5.11 step 2): `install_dev_build.sh` tries `apt-get update` three times, 30 s apart, and
  stops with "refused: fabricctl did not install" when the package is not there; the campaign stops (`STOP`) when its
  floor did not install, its doctor still fails after the one workaround, or its root CA fingerprint is not a SHA-256
  fingerprint, and it uses a value read back from a VM (the fingerprint, the DC's name, a machine account) only after
  it matches its pattern, never as text to run. The workaround for 0.8.0's known issue (2.2.2.1), setup run again
  once, is reported on its own `FLOOR first setup:` line, so the campaign's PASS/FAIL count says nothing about a
  floor that needed it. The run's processes on gaming-23 were ended and the VMs put back to `clean`.
- **Run 5 (the branch at `8e293bfa`), hung in its move step: the harness, not the product.** `FLOOR first setup:
  clean` (0.8.0's first setup finished; no workaround needed). Join, upgrade, images, restart and restore passed: 79
  passed, 0 failed when it stopped. Every join check, the machine tickets on the fabric host and ub26-2 and ws25-1's
  secure channel among them, passed both before and after the upgrade step, which settles run 3: its failures were the
  script's. (Corrected by run 8: that step installed nothing, so both sides ran 0.8.0.) Then the move's step 2
  (4.2.4.2) hung: the netplan file was changed and `netplan apply` started through
  `nohup setsid sh -c 'sleep 2; netplan apply' &`, but the SSH session did not close before the address changed, and
  ssh, with no keepalives, waited on the dead connection for good (12 minutes and more with nothing written). The
  procedure's own line did the same, so an operator's session would hang too. The product's move worked: by hand at
  10.88.0.13, `fabricctl --apply`, the member step and `fabricctl doctor` all exited 0 (before the apply, doctor's
  eight failures all named the old address, as step 3 expects). Fixed: 4.2.4.2 step 2 now has systemd apply the
  change 10 s later (`systemd-run --on-active=10 â¦ netplan apply`); tried on the lab VM, the command returned 0 in
  1 s and the VM answered at its new address, where the old way returned only when the keepalives ended it, after
  36 s. Every ssh in the lab scripts now has keepalives (`ServerAliveInterval=15`, `ServerAliveCountMax=4`), the
  move's call a 60 s limit, and the step checks that the old address no longer answers. Only that run's processes on
  gaming-23 were ended; the VMs went back to `clean` for run 6.
- **Run 6 (the same `.deb`, the script of `9d8d5606`): 86 passed, 29 failed, every failure from one script fault in the
  move.** `FLOOR first setup: clean`. Join through restore passed again, and the move's new step 2 worked: it returned
  0 at once and the old address stopped answering. Then "the fabric VM answers at 10.88.0.13" failed, and with it
  everything after (the move's steps 3 to 6, the clients after it, the whole rename). The VM was at 10.88.0.13 all
  along (it answered there afterwards, uptime unbroken), but the script's `on` set the host key alias from `$FABRIC`,
  which the move had just changed to the new address, and the lab's `known_hosts` holds the VM's key under its first
  address only: "Host key verification failed." Not reached before: run 5 hung earlier. Fixed: the alias is the
  constant `FIRST`, tried against the moved VM (reached at 10.88.0.13; the old address refused), and a VM that does
  not answer at its new address now stops the campaign rather than failing 28 checks after it. Not a stale entry
  from run 5: `ssh-keygen -F` finds no entry for 10.88.0.13 or the renamed name in the lab's `known_hosts` (its only
  entry for the VM is 10.88.0.10; every probe of the moved VM used that alias), so the refusal was the empty match
  under `BatchMode`, and host-key checking stays on. The rename does not touch `known_hosts`: the script reaches
  the VM by address. Also seen: "Connection timed out during banner exchange" from ws25-1, in the call that points
  its DNS at the new address (4.2.4.2 step 5). At that moment ws25-1 is a domain member whose only DNS server is the
  address that just went away; that its sshd waits on a domain lookup before its banner is the likely cause, not
  proven. The script now says so when it happens, with the time it took, and tries once more with 90 s to connect,
  so run 7 records whether it recurs and how long it lasts. The VMs went back to `clean` for run 7.
- **Run 7 (the same `.deb`, the script with the alias fix): 102 passed, 13 failed, `FLOOR first setup: clean`. The
  harness held; the failures are the product's, in the move and the rename.** Join, upgrade, images, restart and
  restore passed (79, 0 failed). The move's own steps passed: step 2 returned at once, the VM answered at 10.88.0.13,
  the apply (step 3), the member step (step 4) and doctor (step 6). ws25-1 was again slow to send its SSH banner while
  its only DNS server was gone ("no SSH banner within 20 s"; the second try got in after 8 s), which the retry now
  absorbs. What failed is what clients find after the move (7 checks: fabric's DNS answers for the host's name with
  10.88.0.10 as well as 10.88.0.13; the DC's name resolves to 10.88.0.10 alone, so the machine tickets, adcli and
  ws25-1's secure channel and nltest fail), and after the rename the same plus doctor (6 checks). Found on the VMs
  before they were put back:
  - **The site's zone keeps fabric's own records at the old address and name.** `templates/vars.yaml.j2` adds
    fabric's names (`default_a`: the host's name and `@` at `host_ip`; `default_cnames` to the host's name) to the
    zone's records, and the installed `vars.yaml`, which the operator edits, already holds the previous render's
    copies of them. So after the move `dns.dynamic_zone_var.A` held `g23-vm-ub24-1` at 10.88.0.10 and at 10.88.0.13,
    and `@` at 10.88.0.10 only (an `@` was already there, so none was added). After the rename it held each CNAME
    twice (`ca` to `g23-vm-ub24-1` and to `g23-vm-ub24-1b`, and so on) and the old name's A records. BIND then did
    not serve the new name (doctor: "DNS g23-vm-ub24-1b.lab.test â no answer").
  - **The DC's own records in the domain's DNS keep the old address.** Samba's configuration follows the move
    (`interfaces` and `dns update command â¦ --current-ip=10.88.0.13`), but `samba_dnsupdate` cannot update its records:
    each of its 13 `nsupdate` calls failed, most likely because it finds its server through the zone's own SOA and NS
    (`dc-0001.ad.lab.test`), which still named 10.88.0.10. Run by hand with `--use-samba-tool` (over RPC), it added
    all 13 records at the new address, but the records at the old address stayed beside them (BIND then answered both
    10.88.0.13 and 10.88.0.10 for the DC).
  - **`/etc/hosts` on the fabric host keeps the old line first.** `join_host_domain` appends `<host_ip> <DC name>` when
    the host cannot resolve the DC at its address; after the move the file held 10.88.0.10 and then 10.88.0.13 for
    the DC, and the first one wins.
  - **The move's doctor passed with all of this wrong**: nothing checks that the zone's and the domain's records for
    this host and its DC name only its own address.
  - After the rename doctor also reported the host's local break-glass account: "OpenBao's current version None, the
    host's 2" (1.7.1.4), not yet looked into.
  Not fixed yet: the fixes change what an apply does when `host_ip` or `hostname` changes, and 4.2.4, so they are a
  design change put to the owner first. The lab's processes ended, the four VMs off and back at `clean`.
  (Corrected by run 8: run 7's upgrade step installed nothing either, so all of it ran on 0.8.0. The DNS findings
  are in 0.8.0's code and unchanged on the branch, but run 7 showed nothing about 0.9.0's own move and rename.)
- **Run 8 (the branch at `c7bd58dd`, the procedures of the owner's answers of 2026-11-01): 66 passed, 13 failed,
  `FLOOR first setup: clean`. Its upgrade never happened, nor did any before it.** The upgrade step's apt said "E:
  Packages were downgraded and -y was used without --allow-downgrades." and the script went on: the lab's `.deb`
  was `0.8.0~git…`, and dpkg sorts `~` below the floor `0.8.0`. Runs 5 to 7 built theirs the same way, so every
  check "after the upgrade" in them ran 0.8.0, the claims of runs 5 and 7 above are corrected, and the campaign has
  not yet tested 0.9.0 over a joined domain. That explains the rename's failures: 0.8.0 has no `admin rename-host`
  (it printed its usage) and no refusal of a DC host's rename, so nothing was renamed, and the zone's, the keytab's
  and 4.2.4.7's checks failed with it. Two script faults hid it: the upgrade step did not stop on apt's refusal,
  and "rename-host" passed on `| tail -3`'s status, not the command's. Fixed: the step stops when apt fails, and when
  `dpkg-query` then names another version than the `.deb`'s; rename-host is checked on its own exit status; the lab
  builds its `.deb` as `<next version>~git…` with `DEB_VERSION` (3.1.5.9 step 1). What run 8 did show, on 0.8.0:
  4.2.4.5's clean-up as written made 4.2.4.7 pass on the host (the zone, the domain's DNS and `/etc/hosts` named
  10.88.0.13 alone; `samba-tool dns delete` answered "does not exist" for each record, `samba_dnsupdate
  --use-samba-tool` having already replaced them, which the procedure allows), and ws25-1 passed every check after
  the move. ub26-2 failed four (its machine ticket, adcli, the DC through fabric's DNS, fabric's own names): it
  still asked 10.88.0.10. It is a member fabric's join set up, and its DNS for the domain is in
  `/etc/systemd/resolved.conf.d/fabric.conf`, written from `FABRIC_DNS` in `/etc/fabric/managed.env`, which
  `fabric-managed` puts back at each check, so 4.2.4.2 step 5's `resolvectl dns` could not move it. A procedure
  gap: step 5 now says to change `FABRIC_DNS` there and run `fabric-managed apply`, and the campaign does that.
- **Run 9 (the branch at `f7958b49`, its `.deb` built as `0.9.0~git20261101154416.f7958b49`; only the join, upgrade,
  move and rename steps): 80 passed, 0 failed, `FLOOR first setup: clean`.** The first run that upgraded: dpkg
  named the `.deb`'s version after it. The move, 4.2.4.5's clean-up and 4.2.4.7 passed; ub26-2 followed the move
  through `managed.env`; the apply with another hostname was refused, naming break glass; `fabricctl admin
  rename-host` renamed the host (exit 0, local-admin under the new name, the old one marked moved), and 4.2.4.6's
  clean-up and 4.2.4.7 passed after it. Its launcher first waited for ever: its probe ran `true` on ws25-1, whose
  SSH shell is cmd ("'true' is not recognized"), so the wait looped although the VM answered; the probe is now
  `exit 0` (both shells), and the run was started again from `clean`.
- **Run 10 (the same `.deb`, every step): 131 passed, 2 failed, `FLOOR first setup: clean`.** Join, upgrade,
  images, restart and restore passed, and the rename and every check after it. The two failures were after the move:
  - **The fabric host's own machine ticket** ("kinit: Cannot contact any KDC"): a product bug, found on the VM.
    The uninstall before an export removes the host's account and forgets it in `config/member.json`, but kept
    `placed` naming it; the restore's member step made the account again and, `placed` already naming it, did not
    place it, so it stayed out of `fabric-managed` (the DC's group listing had no `G23-VM-UB24-1$`). The move's
    apply then ran `fabric-host-managed`, which said "G23-VM-UB24-1$ is not in fabric-managed: fabric's management
    removed" (18:13:05) and took away the host's own DNS for the domain, and the check after it failed. The
    rename's new account was placed (`MACHINE_PLACE` at 18:16:23), which is why the checks after the rename passed.
    Not reached in run 9, which had no restore. Fixed (1.6.11): setup places an account it makes whatever `placed`
    says, and removing the account forgets `placed`; `tests/consent/run.py` covers a record that still names a
    removed account.
  - **ws25-1's secure channel** (`Test-ComputerSecureChannel` False, while nltest found the DC and the DC
    resolved): its System log has NETLOGON 5719 at 18:15:04, "domain isn't available", while its DNS still named
    the old address, and Netlogon had not tried again by the check. `nltest /sc_reset:ad.lab.test` set it up at
    once ("NERR_Success", then True). A procedure gap: 4.2.4.2 step 5 now has it for a Windows member, and the
    campaign runs it.
  The lab's processes ended; the VMs went back to `clean` for run 11, from a `.deb` with the fix.
- **Run 11 (the branch at `090d80fc`, its `.deb` `0.9.0~git20261101183254.090d80fc`, every step): 132 passed, 2
  failed, `FLOOR first setup: clean`.** Its first launch stopped before the campaign: ub26-2 hung at boot (Hyper-V:
  running, heartbeat "NoContact", both adapters "LostCommunication", no SSH for 22 minutes); it was turned off, put
  back to `clean` and the launch started again, and the launcher now does that itself, once, for a Linux VM silent
  for 10 minutes. Both of run 10's failures were gone: the host's machine ticket after the restore and the move, and
  ws25-1's secure channel (`nltest /sc_reset` answered "NERR_Success"). The two failures were one: ub26-2's machine
  ticket after the move (and still after the rename), "Cannot contact any KDC", while `adcli testjoin`, SSSD's
  lookups and the DC's name through fabric's DNS passed. `KRB5_TRACE` showed `kinit` asking 10.88.0.10: SSSD's file
  for Kerberos, `/var/lib/sss/pubconf/kdcinfo.AD.LAB.TEST`, still named the old address (SSSD had not looked for
  the DC again). `systemctl restart sssd` rewrote it with 10.88.0.13 and `kinit -k` got its ticket. Runs 9 and 10
  passed it only because SSSD happened to look again in time. A procedure gap: 4.2.4.2 step 5 now restarts SSSD on a
  joined Linux member, and the campaign does that and checks the file.
- **Run 12 (the same `.deb`, the script of `f02cea96`, every step): 135 passed, 0 failed, `FLOOR first setup:
  clean`. The campaign is green.** 0.8.0 installed and joined (the fabric host, ub26-2, ws25-1); the upgrade
  installed `0.9.0~git20261101183254.090d80fc` (dpkg's version checked); every image updated and rolled back; the
  stack and the VM restarted; an export and a restore; the move to 10.88.0.13 with 4.2.4.5's clean-up and 4.2.4.2
  step 5 as written (ub26-2's `managed.env` and SSSD, ws25-1's DNS and `nltest /sc_reset`) and 4.2.4.7 passing; the
  rename through `fabricctl admin rename-host` after the refused apply, with 4.2.4.6's clean-up and 4.2.4.7; and
  every client checked after each step. ws25-1 was again slow to send its SSH banner while its DNS was gone (the
  retry got in after 24 s). The four VMs off and back at `clean` afterwards.

## 2.3.1.34 Known issue of 0.9.0: a move or rename leaves the old address and name in DNS

*Recorded 2026-11-01 (decision 2.1.1.74; found by the joined campaign's run 7, 2.3.1.33).* Applies to 0.9.0's
move and rename (4.2.4) until 0.9.1 fixes it in the product.

- **What the operator sees.** After 4.2.4.2 or 4.2.4.3 the host's own checks pass (the apply, the member step,
  `fabricctl doctor`), but clients still find the old address or name: `getent hosts <host>.<domain>` gives the old
  address beside the new one; the DC's name resolves to the old address alone, so members' Kerberos (`kinit -k`,
  `adcli testjoin`) and Windows' secure channel and `nltest /dsgetdc` fail. After a rename BIND serves the zone as it
  was before (each alias twice, under both names), so doctor says "DNS <new-name>.<domain> â no answer".
- **Causes** (2.3.1.33, run 7): fabric's own names in the site's zone are merged with the previous render's copies
  that vars.yaml keeps (`templates/vars.yaml.j2`); the DC's update of its own records (`samba_dnsupdate`) asks the old
  address and fails, and nothing removes its records at the old address; `join_host_domain` appends the DC's line to
  `/etc/hosts` rather than replacing it; and no doctor check looks at any of it.
- **Workaround:** the clean-up of 4.2.4.5 (after a move) and 4.2.4.6 (after a rename), then the check of 4.2.4.7. The
  joined campaign runs them exactly as written (3.1.5.11).
- **Fix:** 0.9.1 (2.1.1.74): the zone rendered without the previous render's own names; the DC re-registered at its
  new address over RPC, with its records at the old address removed; `/etc/hosts` replaced; doctor checking that the
  zone, the domain's DNS and `/etc/hosts` name only the host's own address and name.
- **Fixed in 0.9.1** (built 2026-11-02, designed in 1.4.1.8): the render keeps a list of its own records
  (`dns_fabric_own`) and drops them before adding its own again; the DC's records are put right by the domain's
  convergence on the DC's own database (its address added, its old one removed from its own names), not over RPC,
  since convergence runs after every start and apply and needs no DNS; `/etc/hosts` rewritten; doctor's checks of
  4.2.4.7 (`setup/address_checks`). 4.2.4.5 and 4.2.4.6 are removed; 4.2.4.2 step 5's member steps stay manual
  (2.3.1.39).

## 2.3.1.35 0.8.0's first-setup issue forced on a throttled disk, and setup run again (2026-11-01)

*Decision 2.1.1.76.* gaming-23's lab, `g23-vm-rtr-1` and `g23-vm-ub24-1` from `clean`, the `v0.8.0` release's `.deb`
installed by 3.1.5.9 with `tests/lab/joined_vars.yaml` (script: the session's scratch `throttle_proof.sh`).

- **The throttle:** `Get-VMHardDiskDrive -VMName g23-vm-ub24-1 | Set-VMHardDiskDrive -MaximumIOPS 20`, set at
  13:40:20Z the moment setup's `postgres` container existed (a watcher asked the VM every 5 s), so the image builds and
  pulls before it ran at full speed.
- **What the operator saw:** setup's start step printed `â postgres: started` and `â keycloak: started`, then
  stopped at the next unit: "Job for nginx.service failed because a timeout was exceeded. See "systemctl status
  nginx.service" and "journalctl -xeu nginx.service" for details.", then setup's traceback ending
  `subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['systemctl', 'start', 'nginx']' returned non-zero exit status 1.`, and
  "setup did not finish". `fabricctl doctor` exited 1 (nginx, the landing page, DNS-over-TLS and -HTTPS, the
  resolver). On this disk Postgres's first start did not stop setup; the unit after it did. So the known issue is
  wider than its first description: on a slow disk 0.8.0's first setup can stop at any unit's start that runs out of
  time (Postgres, Keycloak or nginx seen in the lab), and the recovery is the same.
- **The recovery:** with the disk still slow (`-MaximumIOPS 300`), at 13:54:41Z, setup run again with the same file
  and flags (`sudo fabricctl setup --file /root/lab-vars.yaml --non-interactive --yes --approve all
  --admin-password-file /root/lab-admin-pw`): it resumed and finished, exit 0, with its first steps printed. Then
  `sudo fabricctl doctor` exited 0, its only output two warnings that every fresh install has (the break-glass
  password's file still in the setup user's home; the setup user `labadmin` not approved as break glass).
- **Afterwards:** the throttle removed (`-MaximumIOPS 0`, read back 0), both VMs turned off and back at `clean`
  (14:05:49Z).
- **Corrected:** 2.2.2.1 and 3.3.1.3 now name any unit's start, nginx's among them, not only Postgres and Keycloak.

## 2.3.1.36 Design note: moves outside 4.2.4 (another network, linked sites)

*Written 2026-11-01 (decision 2.1.1.77): investigation only, nothing built.* 4.2.4.1 limits a move to one install
with no linked sites, on the same network. This note sets out what the other cases touch, from fabric's design
(1.2.11, 1.9, 1.10) and the joined campaign's findings (2.3.1.33, 2.3.1.34), so the owner can decide. None of it is
tested: the lab has run only 4.2.4's own case.

- **2.3.1.36.1 Case A: one install moved to another network.** The new address is in another subnet, so `lan_cidr`
  and `lan_gateway` change with `host_ip` (1.2.11.1.1 to 1.2.11.1.3). What follows from them:
  - the host firewall allows SSH and fabric's published ports from `lan_cidr` only (`configure_firewall`,
    `apply_docker_firewall`): until it is changed, clients on the new network are refused; changed too early, the
    operator's own session is;
  - BIND's `dns-resolvers` ACL is built from `lan_cidr` (`builtin_acls`): clients on the new network get no
    recursion until it changes;
  - DHCP: the address plan's subnets and Kea's interface are the old network's; the new network's subnet must be
    added to the plan (1.10.2) and the old one retired, and Kea hands out the new DNS server only for subnets it
    serves;
  - the AD site's subnets come from the address plan (1.9.8.5): Windows machines find their site's DC by subnet, so
    the new network must be in it;
  - netplan's route and gateway change with the address (a console is needed: the host is unreachable between the
    two);
  - everything 2.3.1.34 lists (the zone, the DC's records, `/etc/hosts`), as for any move.
  What breaks if done like 4.2.4.2: everything in the first five items, at once, with the operator possibly locked
  out by the firewall.
- **2.3.1.36.2 Case B: a site with linked sites moved to a new address.** Each link stores where its peer is, and
  the peers' DCs replicate with this one:
  - AD replication finds its partners by their names in AD's DNS (1.9.8.4); after a move this DC's records keep the
    old address (2.3.1.34), and they replicate to every other DC, so every partner asks the old address until they
    are fixed (at a writable DC: the clean-up of 4.2.4.5 step 2, run where the change replicates from);
  - the ports between DCs (1.9.8.5) and fabric's own links (the federation endpoint, Postgres's logical replication
    between sites, 1.9.8) are opened in each peer's firewall for this site's address, and the links name it; the
    peers must learn the new address;
  - a read-only DC's site and a relay (1.9.8) reach their parent by its name or address: a parent's move affects
    every site below it.
  What breaks if done like 4.2.4.2: replication and the links in both directions, until every peer is changed; a
  read-only site cannot sign anyone in whose password it does not cache.
- **2.3.1.36.3 Case C: a site with linked sites renamed.** The site's own name (`site_name`), its DC's name and its
  AD site do not change with the host's name (1.6.11.14, 2.1.6.45), so replication is not affected. What changes:
  the host's computer account (the member step), its certificates and pages, and fabric's names in its zone. A peer
  that reaches the site by the host's name (rather than by the federation alias or the DC's name) would break; which
  links do is not established here.
- **2.3.1.36.4 Options.**
  1. *Keep them out of scope* (as 4.2.4.1 says now): the procedure refuses to cover them, and an operator who must
     move such a site reinstalls it at the new address and links it again. Simple and safe; the reinstall loses the
     site's own state unless an export and restore carries it (3.2.4).
  2. *Manual procedures, case by case* (like 0.9.0's clean-up): a Volume 4 procedure for A, then B and C, each run in
     the lab first. Needs a lab with a second network (A) and two linked sites (B, C) that the joined campaign does
     not have; each procedure is long and error-prone, and B needs every peer's operator.
  3. *A product move:* `fabricctl` changes the address (and network) as one operation: the firewall changed in a safe
     order (the new network allowed before the old is dropped), the ACLs, the plan's subnets and the AD site's, the
     zone and the DC's records (0.9.1's fix), and for linked sites a change message to each peer (as joins and status
     travel today, 1.9.8) so the peers update their links and firewalls themselves. The most work and the most
     tests (federation's suite and a two-site lab), but the only one that keeps linked sites working through a move.
  4. *A hybrid:* option 3 for case A only (one install), option 1 for linked sites until federation's change messages
     exist.
- **2.3.1.36.5 Trade-offs.** Option 1 costs nothing now but a site that must move loses its links for the
  reinstall. Option 2 can ship earliest for case A, but B needs several operators acting in step, which is where
  manual steps fail. Option 3 needs 0.9.1's fix first (the zone and the DC's records) and a design of federation's
  change messages; it is a release's worth of work. Option 4 spreads that over two releases.
- **2.3.1.36.6 Blocker: moves outside 4.2.4 wait on the owner's choice and a lab that can test them.** **Unblocked
  by:** the owner's answers to 2.3.1.36.7, then a lab with a second network on gaming-23 (for case A) and two linked
  sites (for B and C), whichever the chosen option needs. Until then 4.2.4.1 keeps them out of scope. **Resolved
  2026-11-02** by 2.1.1.77: option 1, out of scope for good; nothing is built, so no lab is needed. 4.2.4.1 now says
  to uninstall and reinstall.
- **2.3.1.36.7 Questions for the owner.** *Answered 2026-11-02 (2.1.1.77):* 1 and 2, every case out of scope (option
  1), users told to uninstall and reinstall to reuse a host; 3, linked sites too: unlinked, uninstalled and installed
  again (the backup idea: 2.3.1.38); 4, no release.
  1. Which cases matter, and when: does production (or a site you plan) need to move to another network, or to move
     or rename while linked to other sites?
  2. Which option (2.3.1.36.4): 1 out of scope, 2 manual, 3 product, or 4 hybrid?
  3. For linked sites: may a move take the links down until each peer's operator acts, or must the peers follow on
     their own?
  4. Which release: with 0.9.1's fix of the zone and the DC's records, or later?

## 2.3.1.37 The 0.9.0 release candidate: runs and findings

0.9.0 holds DHCP and the address manager, and 0.8.1's work (2.1.1.71): the joined machines through fabric's updates,
an install moved and renamed (the joined campaign, green at run 12, 2.3.1.33), and DNS caching bounded to 5 minutes.
Branch `rc/0.9.0`, cut 2026-11-01 from `dev/0.9.0` at `d3a25859` (with #25 and #26); `config/VERSION` 0.9.0 at
`b1dd92f8`.

Findings:

1. **A federation check named a fixed version.** CI's suites on `b1dd92f8` failed in federation (amd64 and arm64):
   "invite: the invitation carries the upstream's version" compared with `"0.8.0"`, so the version bump broke it. A
   test fault: it now reads `config/VERSION`. The floor itself is unchanged: an upstream or joiner older than 0.8.0
   is refused (`MIN_UPSTREAM_VERSION`, 2.1.6.65), whatever the tree's own version.
2. **Images published and pinned.** The images workflow (run 309, from `62398a19`) published every image as
   `0.9.0-rc.309`, both architectures, signed; `pin_published.py 0.9.0-rc.309` pinned them (kea and webui no longer
   pending) and `source_hash.py --check` passed (`86f97a9b`). CI on `62398a19`: lint, package and suites green, after
   finding 1's fix.
3. **apt testing and the repository's key** (3.14.1.6 steps 2 to 4, 2.1.3.5). The `package` workflow's `apt_testing`
   run from `86f97a9b` put the candidate's `.deb` in `testing` and republished `public.key`. `apt_key_test.sh` read
   the old key at first (1 passed, 3 failed): Pages was still deploying. Once deployed, 4 passed, 0 failed. The test
   now runs in the apt job before every publish.
4. **`tests/images/previous.yaml` refreshed** for the image-update test: fabric's own images go back to 0.8.0's pins
   (`0.8.0-rc.294`, promoted as 0.8.0), which 0.8.0's skip notes said would return from 0.8.1 on; the upstream
   images are unchanged since 0.8.0, so their previous versions stay.
5. **The local suites, the sandbox and the image-update test.** Every local suite but the sandbox
   (`tests/run-all.sh`, WSL, on `b1dd92f8`): 22 passed, federation failed on finding 1 alone; with its fix docs, lint
   and federation pass. The sandbox, kept (`KEEP=1 tests/sandbox/run.sh`, on `86f97a9b`'s pinned images): 295 passed,
   0 failed, exit 0. The image-update test on it (`tests/images/update.sh --box fabric-sandbox`): 63 passed, 0 failed,
   exit 0; every image updated to its previous version and back, fabric's own to 0.8.0's for the first time since
   0.8.0 (finding 4), OpenBao skipped as before (the first release on its track).
6. **A federation check that one root in 256 passes.** CI's suites on `ab846e84` (docs only since the green
   `6f32c8c8`) failed in federation (arm64) alone, 13 checks from "join: a root other than the pinned one is refused
   before anything is sent" (an empty reason: `join_upstream` did not refuse at all). The cause is the test's: it
   forged the pin as `"AA:"` plus the real fingerprint's rest, so a root whose fingerprint began with `AA` made the
   forgery the real pin. The job's endpoint log shows it: right after the wrong-secret refusal, `GET
   /certs/root-ca.crt` then `POST /v1/join ... 200`, a join that went through and spent branch1's invitation, so
   every join check after it failed. `tests/pki/site_ca.py` had the same fault and its fix (2.3.1.32); the federation
   check now uses `AB:` when the real pin begins with `AA:`. The failed job, run again on `ab846e84` alone, passed
   (another root, another fingerprint).
7. **The test Pi (step 6; the owner approved its wipe, 2026-11-02).** `tempuser@192.168.5.57` (temp-ub, Ubuntu
   26.04), the candidate `0.9.0~git20261102002437.86f97a9` from apt `testing`; the production host never touched,
   Kea never installed:
   - **Upgrade from the floor** (`FROM=0.8.0 TO_SUITE=testing tests/host/upgrade.sh`): 17 passed, 0 failed, exit 0.
     The previous release is the floor (0.8.0), so it ran once. A first attempt reached the upgrade (the Pi ran the
     candidate afterwards) but its log was lost: WSL's Ubuntu-24.04 restarted under it (systemd and Docker up again at
     07:12:41 EST, `/tmp` empty; not restarted by the agent), so it was run again with its log kept on the Windows side.
   - **The host suite on a fresh install** (`apt-get purge fabricctl`, then `APT_SUITE=testing tests/host/run.sh`):
     25 passed, 0 failed, exit 0.
   - **The rehearsal, interactive** (`tests/host/installer.py`, Guided then Advanced with every default left, then a
     dropped connection re-attached): 44 passed, 0 failed, exit 0. Then as a user: `http://192.168.5.57/certs/` 200
     (the CA certificates page, `root-ca.crt` "Fabric Installer Test CA Root CA"); `fabric-ctl`, `info` and `sso`
     `.home.arpa` resolve at the Pi's DNS to 192.168.5.57; `https://fabric-ctl.home.arpa/` verified against that root,
     303 to `/login`, then Keycloak's "Sign in to Fabric Installer Test" (200).
   - **Left for the owner's install:** purged (`/opt/fabric` absent, no containers, `fabric.target` inactive), with
     only `fabricctl` `0.9.0~git20261102002437.86f97a9` installed from `testing`.

## 2.3.1.38 Investigation: backups to make leaving the domain easier on a reinstall (an idea, 2026-11-02)

*Decision 2.1.1.77 (owner, 2026-11-02): moves outside 4.2.4 are out of scope; a host reused elsewhere, or a linked
site moved or renamed, is uninstalled and installed again (4.2.4.1). The owner added: "maybe using some backups to
capture data to make seperation from the domain easier".* Investigated from the code and the manual only; nothing
built, nothing run.

- **2.3.1.38.1 What an export carries across a reinstall today.** `fabricctl uninstall --export` (3.2.4.1,
  `setup/export_install.py`) copies, cold and whole, every fabric folder (`uninstall.DIRS`: fabric's config and
  `vars.yaml`, nginx, bind9, stepca, keycloak, postgres, webui, openbao, fluentbit, kea, freeradius, samba,
  federation, adguard, the resolver, the DNS filter), OpenBao's vault key and outside data folders, and fabric's
  secrets in plain text. So it holds:
  - **people, groups, machines, devices, policies**: the whole AD database (`samba/`), with the domain's identity
    (its SID, its DCs, this host's DC);
  - **the address plan**: it lives in the domain (`ipam/load_address_plan.py` reads it from the DC), so it is in
    `samba/`, plus Kea's own files (`kea/`);
  - **zones**: the site's zone and its TSIG keys (`bind9/`, `vars.yaml`'s records);
  - **links**: the federation registry and its DNS link keys (`federation/`, the secrets);
  - **the CA, certificates and OpenBao's data** (`stepca/`, `openbao/`, the vault key);
  - Keycloak's database (second factors).
  `fabricctl restore` puts all of it back and runs setup on it (3.2.4.1). The other carrier, the settings file
  (4.8.3.2), holds only the wizard's settings: no people, zones' records, plan or links.
- **2.3.1.38.2 Why restoring it does not separate a host.** Restore brings back the same installation: the same
  `vars.yaml` (`host_ip`, `lan_cidr`, the name), the same domain and DC, the same CA, the same links. On another
  network that is a move (case A, 2.3.1.36.1) with every defect of 2.3.1.34 and the firewall besides; for a linked
  site, its parent still holds it in its registry. An export is a whole-install backup, not a way to take data out of
  one domain and into a new one.
- **2.3.1.38.3 What must be undone to leave the AD domain and the federation cleanly** (what the code does today):
  - *The host's own membership:* uninstall removes the host's computer account (`undo/remove_host_account.py`) and
    undoes `member` (sssd, the keytab, PAM).
  - *The site's link:* `fabricctl federation remove <site>` on the parent (`federation/remove_site.py`) drops the
    registry entry and the DNS link key and revokes the site's CA. It does **not** take the site's domain controller
    out of the domain, nor its AD site, site link, service accounts or grants, nor move its OU to the Lost OU, which
    1.9.8.12 and 1.9.8.17 design (the Lost OU is marked "to settle when it is built"). Nothing in `src/` demotes a DC
    (`samba-tool domain demote`): uninstalling a site's host leaves its DC object, and the other DCs keep trying to
    replicate with it.
  - *The machines joined to it:* a reinstall makes a new domain, so each member leaves and joins again (4.6.6, 4.6.7);
    nothing carries their accounts across.
  - *The root site:* reinstalling the root ends the federation (a new root CA and domain); every site would join
    again.
- **2.3.1.38.4 Gaps, for the owner.**
  1. **A site leaving the domain:** `federation remove` lacks the AD half of its design (the DC demoted or
     `--remove-other-dead-server`, the AD site and its links, service accounts, grants, the OU to the Lost OU). Build it
     as 1.9.8.12 says, or record that a removed site's DC objects are cleaned by hand (no procedure exists yet)?
  2. **A selective export:** nothing exports only what a new install should take in (people and groups without their
     SIDs and passwords' history, the zone's records, the address plan, DHCP reservations) and imports it into a
     fresh domain. Is that the backup the owner means, and which of these matter?
  3. **Passwords and second factors:** a new domain cannot take AD's password hashes from an export safely without
     a design (people would reset passwords and second factors); acceptable?
  4. **Members:** a reinstall makes every joined machine leave and join again. Should a future import pre-create
     their accounts (one-time join passwords, 4.6.7.6) to shorten that?
  5. **A linked site's reinstall** joins with a new invitation and a new CA; under the same site name its old
     records at the parent must be gone first (gap 1).
  None is scheduled: each needs the owner's answer, then a design in Volume 1, before any work (2.1.1.77).
  **The owner, 2026-11-02 (2.1.1.78):** all five stay open for now, "will address later, this is likely a 1.0
  target"; they are listed as candidates in the roadmap's 1.0 row (1.1.4.6).

## 2.3.1.39 Design question: how fabric reaches its members after a move (0.9.1, for the owner)

**Answered 2026-11-03, option 1: 2.1.1.79; design 1.4.1.8.7.** *Raised 2026-11-02 while building 0.9.1's move fix
(2.1.1.74 as revised 2026-11-02).* The revision gives fabric the
three member steps of 4.2.4.2 step 5 for the members it manages: `FABRIC_DNS` in a Linux member's
`/etc/fabric/managed.env`, SSSD's restart (its `kdcinfo` file keeps the DC's old address), and Windows' secure channel
set up again (`nltest /sc_reset`). It leaves *how* to 0.9.1's design, and no design says it yet, so the host's half is
built (1.4.1.8) and these three stay manual until the owner chooses.

- **2.3.1.39.1 Why it is not a small step.** Every way fabric reaches a member today starts from the member and goes
  through the old address: a managed Linux member's hourly `fabric-managed check` signs in to the DC by name, asking
