  the DNS server in `managed.env` (the old address, gone after the move), and a Windows member takes Group Policy from
  the DC it finds through the same DNS. Once the host has moved, neither can learn the new address from fabric. The
  host has no channel to its members (no SSH or WinRM into them, by design: 1.6.11.6).
- **2.3.1.39.2 Options.**
  1. **Announce before the move** (recommended): a new command, `fabricctl move prepare <new address>` (with a
     console form later), records the coming address in the domain before step 2 of 4.2.4.2. Linux members'
     hourly check reads it (as the machine, from the DC, as it reads its membership) and lists both addresses as
     their DNS servers, and once the old one stops answering keeps the new one alone and restarts SSSD;
     Windows members get both addresses by a fabric GPO (DNS Client: DNS servers) and a scheduled task, by Group
     Policy Preferences, that runs `nltest /sc_reset` when Netlogon logs 5719. The operator waits for the members'
     next check (up to an hour; Group Policy up to 90 minutes, or `gpupdate`) before moving. *Trade-offs:* a new
     entry point, a new GPO and a two-phase procedure; members fabric does not manage still follow step 5 by hand.
  2. **Find fabric again after the move:** members keep fabric's name, not its address, and look it up by another
     route when the DNS server stops answering (the network's own DHCP-given DNS, or a list of fallback
     addresses). *Trade-offs:* works only where the network's DNS knows fabric, which is the job fabric's DNS does;
     unreliable in the lab and on most LANs.
  3. **Keep the three steps manual** (rejected once by the owner's revision, 2.1.1.74 option (a)): no new code;
     4.2.4.2 step 5 stays as it is, and is proved by the joined campaign as today.
- **2.3.1.39.3 What it blocked:** nothing else in 0.9.1; 4.2.4.2 step 5 now says what is left by hand.

## 2.3.1.40 Run 13 of the joined campaign: the move and rename fix proven (0.9.1, 2026-11-02)

The joined campaign (3.1.5.8), all seven steps, on the Hyper-V lab from checkpoint `clean`: fabric installed at the
floor (0.8.0), the three members joined (ub24-1's own join, ub26-2, ws25-1), upgraded to
`0.9.1~git20261102175448.dec46383` (the commit of 2.1.1.74's fix), the images updated and rolled back, a restart and a
restore, then the install moved to another address and renamed (4.2.4.2, 4.2.4.3).

- **Result: 129 passed, 0 failed** (17:58 to 19:16 UTC). The VMs were put back to `clean` and left off.
- **What the fix was checked by** (4.2.4.7's checks, in place of run 12's clean-up by hand): after the move's apply,
  no record at the old address in fabric's zone; after the member step, no `/etc/hosts` line for the DC at the old
  address, and the DC named at most once, at the new one; after the rename, no record names the old host name; every
  member's sign-in, Kerberos ticket, SSSD lookup and (Windows) secure channel hold.
- **Still by hand:** the members' three steps of 4.2.4.2 step 5 (`FABRIC_DNS`, SSSD's restart, Windows' secure channel
  reset), run by the campaign as 4.2.4 gives them, until 2.3.1.39 is answered.
- The DC's own records in the AD zones (`ensure_dc_address`) are checked by the samba suite's `dc.py` (174 passed):
  an old address on the DC's name and the domain's `@` is taken away; another name's record at that address is the
  admin's and stays.

## 2.3.1.41 Run 14 of the joined campaign: the place step, machines moved within OU=machines (0.9.1, 2026-11-02)

The joined campaign (3.1.5.8), every step with the new `place` step (3.1.5.11) after the upgrade, on the Hyper-V lab
from checkpoint `clean`: fabric at the floor (0.8.0), the three members joined, upgraded to
`0.9.1~git20261102211057.90b01864`, then placed, the images updated and rolled back, a restart, a restore, the move
and the rename (4.2.4.2, 4.2.4.3).

- **Result: 149 passed, 0 failed** (21:14 to 22:28 UTC). The VMs were put back to `clean` and left off.
- **The place step, within today's rule** (1.8.8.9.3: each kind moves only within its own folder's tree until 2.3.8.6
  is answered): an admin's OU made under `OU=machines` (`machines/branch`) and one at the domain's root (`lab-root`);
  ub26-2 and ws25-1 moved into `machines/branch`; a move into `lab-root` refused, saying why; the fabric host's own
  account refused (2.1.6.100).
- **What held after the move:** on ub26-2, the access check (`sssctl user-checks`) and `sudo -l` gave the same answer as
  before; on ws25-1, the same fabric GPOs applied (`gpresult`) and the same local Administrators; every member's
  Kerberos ticket, SSSD lookup, DNS and (Windows) secure channel, as in the members' checks. The GPOs are linked at the
  domain's root (2.1.6.98), so a move within `OU=machines` changes none of them.
- **A weakness to note:** ub26-2's access check denied the checked person both before and after (they hold no grant
  on that machine), so it shows that nothing changed, not that an allowed person stays allowed. The compare of the GPOs
  and the Administrators on ws25-1 is the stronger check. A grant on the moved machine before the step would close
  this; it waits for 2.3.8.6, which decides whether the step moves machines further.

## 2.3.1.42 Runs 15 and 16 of the joined campaign: the place step proven both ways; a member's DNS written back by its own hourly check (0.9.1, 2026-11-03)

Both on `0.9.1~git20261103014254.811b74e5` (the package), with the campaign's uncommitted place step of 2.3.1.41's
weakness: two people made before the move, `placeok` in a group with a grant on ub26-2 alone (a host group; remote
sign-in and sudo of `/usr/bin/id`) and `placeno` in none; ub26-2's access check and sudo read for each, waiting until
the grant had reached the machine.

- **The place step, both runs:** before the move `placeok` allowed (`pam_acct_mgmt: Success`, `(root) /usr/bin/id`)
  and `placeno` refused (`Permission denied`); after ub26-2 and ws25-1 moved to `machines/branch`, the same for each.
  So a person allowed stays allowed and a refused one stays refused, not only "nothing changed".
- **Run 15: 149 passed, 1 failed** (2026-11-02, ended 22:14 UTC; the VMs were left running overnight and put back to
  `clean`, off, at 08:06 on 11-03). The failure, "4.2.4.2 step 6: each name gives 10.88.0.13 alone", was the test's:
  the host name it asks for was read with `sudo hostname` through `on`, which keeps stderr, and sudo's warning
  "unable to resolve host g23-vm-ub24-1: Temporary failure in name resolution" became part of the name. Every real
  name answered 10.88.0.13 alone. Fixed in the campaign: the name is read without sudo. **Why sudo warned:** the
  host's own name is not in `/etc/hosts` (the lab's install puts no `127.0.1.1` line; run 16 logged "not in
  /etc/hosts" before and after the move); it is answered only by systemd-resolved, which synthesises the host's own
  name. While resolved restarts (the move's `netplan apply`, the member step's DNS drop-in) sudo cannot resolve it and
  warns; stopping resolved on the moved host showed the same warning, starting it again cleared it. sudo still runs
  the command: a warning, not a failure. Whether fabric should name the host in `/etc/hosts`: no (2.1.1.82, the owner, 2026-11-03).
- **Run 16: 142 passed, 8 failed** (2026-11-03, 13:11 to 14:43 UTC). Every failure was ub26-2 after the move and
  then the rename: its KDC file (`kdcinfo.AD.LAB.TEST`) missing after SSSD's restart, `info.lab.test` not answered at
  the new address, `adcli testjoin`, the machine's Kerberos ticket, the DC's name. **Cause, a product race (not the
  campaign's change, not the VM):** on ub26-2, `/etc/fabric/managed.env` said `FABRIC_DNS=10.88.0.13` but
  `/etc/systemd/resolved.conf.d/fabric.conf` said `DNS=10.88.0.10`. Its hourly `fabric-managed check` had started at
  14:38:49 (one hour after the join), reading the old address; the campaign changed the file at 14:38:50 and its own
  `fabric-managed apply` wrote the new address; the check, slow because the DC was no longer at the address it asked,
  finished at 14:38:55 and wrote the old address back. Runs 13 to 15 missed the hour by chance. An operator following
  4.2.4.2 step 5 can hit the same; the next hourly check would mend it.
- **The fix:** `fabric-managed` (written by `join-linux.sh`) takes a lock for its whole run and reads `managed.env`
  only once it holds it, so a run waits for one under way and then writes the address of that moment. Tested in the
  samba suite's `linux_join.py` (a run started while the lock is held waits, and writes the address set while it
  waited, never the one before: all passed). Members joined before 0.9.1 keep the old script (it is written at the
  join): 4.2.4.2 step 5 now says to let a check under way finish first and to read the drop-in afterwards, and the
  campaign does both (its members join with the floor's script) and checks the drop-in.
- The VMs were put back to `clean` and turned off after run 16 (after ub26-2's state and the fabric VM's name
  resolution had been read).
- **Run 17: 151 passed, 0 failed** (2026-11-03, 15:28 to 16:55 UTC, on `0.9.1~git20261103152419.cdee7869`, the
  lock and the campaign's changes committed). The same tests as run 16, plus the new check that the member's drop-in
  names the new address after step 5 (passed); the place step's allowed person still allowed and refused one still
  refused; the host's own name logged before and after the move ("not in /etc/hosts", answered by systemd-resolved).
  The members joined with the floor's script, so the run proves the procedure's wait (4.2.4.2 step 5), and
  `linux_join.py` proves the lock. The VMs were put back to `clean` and turned off.

## 2.3.1.43 The lab's VMs moved to V: (2026-11-03)

2026-11-03: the owner migrated the lab's VMs, their disks and their `clean` checkpoints to `V:\Hyper-V\fabric-lab\<vm>`,
on the SSD, for performance. The seed media (`D:\Hyper-V\fabric-lab\seed`) and the ISOs (`D:\os\AMD64-x86`) stay on D:,
and no VM has a DVD attached. `$VmDir` in `new_hyperv_lab.ps1` and `remove_hyperv_lab.ps1` now defaults to V:. Neither
script derives the seed's path from it, and `remove_hyperv_lab.ps1` deletes only VM disks under `$VmDir`, never the seed;
the seed's path is `install_vm.ps1`'s own `$SeedDir` and `make_seeds.sh`'s `LAB_SEED_DIR`. Checked by reading and a
parse check: neither script was run, since both would change the lab (3.1.5).

## 2.3.1.44 Run 18 of the joined campaign: Windows did not follow the announcement (2026-11-03)

On `8dcf049b`, on the VMs at their new place on V:: **160 passed, 2 failed**. Everything but one thing held, the any-OU
placement and `fabricctl move prepare` on real members included; a Linux member (ub26-2) followed the move with nothing
run by hand. The two failures are one finding:

- `FAIL ws25-1 has its secure channel, nothing run by hand` (15 minutes allowed)
- `FAIL ws25-1's DNS Client policy names the new address alone after the move`

**What was seen on ws25-1.** The policy value held both addresses, and the Scheduled Task was registered and did fire on
Netlogon's 5719. But `ipconfig /all` still named the old address alone, the task's one `nltest /sc_reset` ended with 1, and
5719 was logged only that once, so it never ran again.

**Tried by hand on that VM** with its adapter at the dead old address, each answer a name lookup of the DC:

| State | Answer |
|---|---|
| Policy both addresses | nothing, 12 s |
| Policy the new address alone | nothing, 12 s |
| ...after `gpupdate /force` | nothing, 12 s |
| ...after restarting the DNS Client service | nothing, 12 s |
| Adapter itself both addresses, old first | the new address, 1 s; `nltest /sc_reset` then succeeds |

So on this Windows Server 2025 the DNS Client's `NameServer` policy did not take effect, for a reason not found; a reboot was
not tried. Whether other Windows versions honour it is unknown.

**What changed:** the GPO no longer holds the policy. Its task sets the adapter's servers itself and tries the secure
channel again every 5 minutes while a move is announced (1.4.1.8.7, 2.1.1.79). The rerun: 2.3.1.47.

## 2.3.1.45 A site link's `CNF:` copy, in Configuration, that no check could see (2026-11-04)

The `samba` suite's `no conflict objects in the domain` failed once `read_conflicts.py`, new in 0.9.1 (2.1.8.35), searched every
partition instead of the domain's alone: `CN=lan-lab2\0ACNF:...,CN=IP,CN=Inter-Site Transports,CN=Sites,CN=Configuration,...`.

- **It predates this work, shown:** the same all-partitions search, added to `tests/samba/site_join.py` on an export of
  `7d0810cb` and run alone, found the same object (`CN=lan-lab2\0ACNF:3db95dd7-...`), and that run's own check passed (51 passed,
  rc 0): the old `ldbsearch` did not look in Configuration. The Federation tab was blind to it the same way.
- **The cause, from the code and that sequence:** when a site with its own DC moves here (1.9.8.14), the parent's DC makes
  the link in `prepare_site`, and the site's own convergence makes it at its DC before the two have replicated. Both add
  `CN=<parent>-<site>`, and AD keeps one and renames the other. That the two adds are what collide is inferred, not
  watched happening. `ensure_site_link.py` is unchanged from `7d0810cb` up to this fix.
- **What it does:** the copy names the same two sites at the same cost, so the sites still replicate over the kept link;
  it stays for good, and shows as a second link for those sites in `ad_sites`. No harm beyond that was looked for.
- **The fix:** the check stays wide. `ensure_site_link` removes this site's own `CNF:` copy at the next convergence; another
  link's copy is left and shown. Tested with a copy made on purpose, the negative, and the race's own copy gone in
  `site_join` (53 passed). A first attempt narrowed the search back to make the test pass; it was not committed.

## 2.3.1.46 Setup's console said it had ended while it ran on (2026-11-04)

A fresh `fabricctl setup` on the test Pi printed `fabric-setup.service ended before setup finished (stopped or killed)` and
exited 1 after a minute, during `[certs]`. The unit was in fact still active, and finished successfully minutes later: the
install is complete and the run's log says so. So the console's answer was wrong, not the setup.

`follow_run` stops at the first time `unit_running` says no, and `unit_running` reads any answer outside its running states,
an empty one included, as not running. **The cause is not proved:** a `systemctl is-active` answering badly during a
`daemon-reload` would fit, and setup does reload about there, but it was not caught happening. It may have been behind other
"setup stopped" reports; that was not looked into.

**What changed:** `follow_run` asks again over a few seconds before it believes the unit has ended. Seen once; not reproduced.

## 2.3.1.47 Run 19 of the joined campaign: Windows follows a move; a member undid its own join (2026-11-04)

On `0.9.1~git20261104081230.356b0e26`, the first run of Windows' redesigned part: **129 passed, 33 failed.** 32 of the
failures are ub26-2 and one cause; one is a check that could never pass.

- **Windows, what this run was for:** ws25-1 took both addresses on its adapter, old first, from the announcement; after the
  move its secure channel held with nothing run by hand, its adapter named the new address alone, by the task, and the
  announcement went with the move. It logged event 5719 once. Run 18's two failures did not come back.
- **The check that could not pass:** "by the Group Policy Preferences task ... every 5 minutes" looked for `PT5M` in
  `Triggers | Out-String`, which prints `Repetition : MSFT_TaskRepetitionPattern`, never the interval. So this run does not
  show the 5-minute trigger, which is only there while a move is announced; after the move the task had its two other
  triggers and no interval, as designed. The check now reads the task's own XML.
- **ub26-2 undid its join:** it joined with the floor's script (0.8.0), and from then until the rename step's hand-run
  `apply` it had no DNS drop-in: no ticket ("Cannot contact any KDC"), no SSSD lookup, no DC name; so the "before the move"
  grant read, its following of the move and its settling all failed too. Its journal: the machine booted at 09:09:35; at
  09:16:12.427 the join's `systemctl enable --now fabric-managed.timer` started the timer, and 4 ms later the first check began,
  because `OnBootSec=5min` had already passed; at 09:16:12.530 that check logged
  `removed (no longer in fabric-managed)`, with `MANAGED_PENDING=0` in its settings, so the join had added it to the group.
- **Cause, in two parts:**
  1. The first check runs at the same moment as the join's own `apply`, whenever a machine joins more than 5 minutes after
     boot. The floor's script has no lock (2.3.1.42 added it for 0.9.1). The lock keeps the two from overlapping, and
     `apply` then has the last word, or the check runs on what `apply` wrote.
  2. `member()` read a failed search as "not a member": with `pipefail`, a failed `ldapsearch | grep` returns `grep`'s 1,
     the same as a real no. **That one is in every version until this one, and worse than the race:** a search failing
     once, the ticket having come, removes fabric's DNS, after which the machine cannot find the DC's name, cannot get a
     ticket, and so stays that way, with the hourly check saying only that the DC did not answer. Seen here during the run's move.
- **Not proved:** why the search failed at that moment; the journal does not say, and `ldapsearch`'s errors go to
  `/dev/null`. An `apply` part-way through restarting `systemd-resolved` would fit. Why this run lost a race earlier runs
  did not is not known either: earlier runs' uptimes at the join were not kept.
- **What changed:** `member()` answers 2 when the search fails, so only a real "no" removes
  (`tests/samba/linux_join.py`: the DC's LDAP name not answering, positive and negative). **A member joined before 0.9.1 keeps
  the old script** until it is refreshed (2.1.1.84, the owner's answer to this, 4.6.6.12): it can still lose this the same way.

## 2.3.1.48 Run 20 of the joined campaign: all of it passed (2026-11-04)

On `0.9.1~git20261104113447.2652535b`, the same tests as run 19 with its two fixes: **161 passed, 0 failed.** That includes
the check run 19 could not make: ws25-1's task has its `PT5M` trigger while a move is announced. Windows as in 2.3.1.47:
secure channel held, nothing run by hand, the new address alone; one 5719 logged.

**ub26-2 had the same overlap, and it went the other way.** It booted at 11:36:38, and its first check began at 11:43:36, at the
join again (over 5 minutes after boot), with the floor's script: `the DC did not answer; nothing changed`, exit 1. So the
race of 2.3.1.47 is not rare; what varied is where it failed: here the ticket, which the script has always treated as no
answer; in run 19 the search, which it did not. This does not show that fix working, since the floor's script ran here
too: `tests/samba/linux_join.py` shows that. The floor's first setup was clean.

## 2.3.1.49 Run 21 of the joined campaign: the refresh on a real floor member (2026-11-04)

On `0.9.1~git20261104140816.95263ab3`, the first run of 4.6.6.12 on ub26-2, really joined by the floor's script: **182 passed,
2 failed.** The floor's first setup stopped, its known issue (2.2.2.1). Both failures were real findings:

- **"The same fetch against a root it does not trust" was fetched anyway: the procedure's trust was not what it said.**
  `curl --cacert <another root>` succeeded, while `openssl s_client -CAfile` with the same file refused. `curl -v` showed why:
  `CAfile: /tmp/other.pem`, `CApath: /etc/ssl/certs`. Ubuntu's curl keeps its CApath beside a `--cacert`, and that directory holds
  fabric's root, since the join added it, and every public CA. So the fetch, which is then run as root, would have accepted
  any certificate a public CA issued for that name. **Fixed:** `--capath /usr/local/share/ca-certificates`, which holds no hashed
  names. By hand on the member: another root refused (curl exit 60), fabric's root fetches. The campaign's negative takes it next.
- **Other uses of `--cacert` alone:** nothing else fabric documents fetches something to run. 3.1.1's line and
  `src/fabriclib/common/doh_query.py`, `setup/verify_install.py` and `setup/wait_ready.py` are probes on the host, and tests'
  are tests: a probe that passes because of a public CA is a weaker check, not a way in. Not changed.
- **"The floor's fault reproduced" failed, so this run does not show recovery from being cut off.** The member stayed reachable
  after the removal: the campaign's own `resolvectl dns` (`members_dns`) had given its link fabric's address, which a real
  member has not, so the DC's name still resolved. "--refresh on the cut-off member" passed, but on a member that was not
  cut off; it counts only for waiting out a running check, and for refreshing. The step now returns the link to what its
  netplan says and restarts SSSD. By hand on that member, with the link's address no longer answering: no name and no KDC file.
  `linux_join.py`'s containers have the DC in `/etc/hosts`, so its case shows the drop-in coming back, not a machine cut off.

**What did show:** the refusals on the real floor script (fingerprint; the DC not answering, with script, settings and DNS
hashed the same after), the wait for a running check, the HTTPS fetch by address, and members healthy afterwards.

Also from this run: CI failed on this commit, in `render` (with `hardening` and `images`, which render first): the page named
`info.<domain>`, which an admin may repoint. The gate before that commit left `render` out, which is how it got through. The fetch
now uses the host's own name, and `tests/render.py` asserts it, with `--capath`.

## 2.3.1.50 Run 22, stopped: ub26-2 never joined; and wget cannot be told to trust one root (2026-11-05)

On `0.9.1~git20261105021456.68caccbe`. Stopped by hand partway, at 59 passed and 11 failed, with no summary line. Its failures, all
ub26-2 from the first step: **it had never joined.** The floor's script stopped at `E: Could not get lock
/var/lib/dpkg/lock-frontend. It is held by process 2128 (unattended-upgr)`, and the campaign printed `join: 0` and went on,
because the status it read was `tail`'s. There was no `managed.env` and no keytab. So the run could show nothing about the
refresh, and was stopped; **recovery from being cut off is still not shown** (2.3.1.49).

- **The product:** a machine joined soon after boot can meet this. The floor's script is as released; this build's now waits for
  the lock, up to 10 minutes (`DPkg::Lock::Timeout`). That wait has no test of its own.
- **The campaign:** it waits for the lock before the floor's join, and stops if the join left no settings behind.

**wget (found building 2.1.1.85, by `tests/samba/join_steps.py`):** with the server's root in the system store and another root
given, wget 1.21.4 on Ubuntu 24.04 still fetched, and the script ran. Tried by hand, each still fetching: `--ca-certificate` alone,
with `--ca-directory=.`, with a directory holding only the other root, with an empty one, and `--ca-directory` alone.
`openssl s_client -no-CApath -no-CAstore -CAfile` refused the same server, and accepted it with the right root, so a machine without
curl fetches the script with that. The same test on Debian 13 passed with wget's options; that was not looked into further,
since the one way now serves both.

## 2.3.1.51 Run 23 of the joined campaign: the refresh and the HTTPS join on real machines (2026-11-05)

On `0.9.1~git20261105044457.531c3bdb`: **174 passed, 29 failed.** Every failure is ub26-2, in the steps before the refresh
(join, upgrade, place, images, restart, reboot, restore), and every one has the same cause: **the floor's script cut it off,
as in run 19.** Its journal: booted 04:47:14; `removed (no longer in fabric-managed)` at 04:54:15, the second of its join, with
`MANAGED_PENDING=0`. Its link's DNS was `1.1.1.1`, no drop-in. From the refresh on, nothing failed.

- **How often:** of the runs whose member really joined by the floor's script over 5 minutes after boot, 19 and 23 lost this and
  20 and 21 did not. That is a small count, but it is not rare. **A member joined by a released fabric can be off the domain
  from its first minute.** `v0.9.0`'s script has the same fault, read in its source (no lock; `ldapsearch` piped into `grep`; `OnBootSec=5min`
  and `enable --now`), though no run joined with it, so **0.8.0's and 0.9.0's joins both can**. This release's `member()` and lock cure it
  (2.3.1.47); those joined earlier need 4.6.6.12, and 0.9.1's release notes say so (2.1.1.84).
- **Recovery from really being cut off, now shown** (2.3.1.49 could not): with the link back on netplan's address and SSSD
  restarted, `no drop-in`, `the DC's name does not resolve` and `no ticket as the machine` all passed; the refresh then waited for a
  running check (which said `the DC did not answer; nothing changed`, its old script) and brought it back; afterwards the drop-in,
  the name and a ticket all passed, with the link still on `1.1.1.1`, and every client check. The cut-off was made by the step, the
  same as the earlier one the floor made by itself; on this run it had been so since its join.
- **The refresh's fetch:** another root refused, now that `--capath` is there (2.3.1.49's failure); fabric's root fetches, at the
  host's own name.
- **The HTTPS join (2.1.1.85), the new last step:** the page's block, taken from the page: a wrong fingerprint refused, saying why, the
  script not fetched, the machine unchanged; a root that is not fabric's, its own fingerprint held, refused by curl, saying why, with
  the machine's store trusting fabric's root, nothing kept or run; then ub26-2 joined by those steps with a one-time password, under the
  new name `g23-fresh-1`, on a host already moved and renamed, and every client check passed.

**What changed:** the campaign reports the floor's own cut-off apart from the checks, as it does the floor's first setup, counted in its
summary (`FLOOR known cut-offs`), and applies by hand as an operator would, so that the steps after it test what they are for. **The owner has
not accepted or rejected this reporting yet.** It covers only the floor's own join: a member joined by this build's script that cuts itself
off is a failure (the fresh step's own check). 4.6.6.12's step still reproduces the fault on purpose.

**CI on this commit: `webui (arm64)` failed once;** amd64, and arm64 on the commits before it, passed. "Apply: one job; the policy refused
... without a DC" found the change still pending, no job and no message on the page. What can be said: nothing in `src`, `templates/webui-app` or
`tests/webui` differs from 68caccbe, whose arm64 job passed. `pending_store.apply` puts the queue's entry in before it starts the job, under its lock,
so an Apply it accepted shows, and the page's whole text in the log has no queue; so either the POST never reached it, or the Review was read without
the page's read permission. `applied()` throws away what the POST answered, and a refusal goes there, so the log cannot say which. **Not explained.**
CI was not rerun to clear it. The test now prints that answer when it fails, so it can be told if it comes back.

## 2.3.1.52 The 0.9.1 fix release candidate: runs and findings

*Why it exists:* 2.1.1.86. *The fault's record, from the directory browser's branch (`dev/0.9.1`, now `dev/0.9.2`):* a member's journal in the lab, `removed (no longer in fabric-managed)` in the
second of its join, `MANAGED_PENDING=0` (so its join had added it), no DNS drop-in, `Cannot contact any KDC`, for good. With `pipefail`, a failed
`ldapsearch | grep` returned `grep`'s 1, the same as a real no. Why the search failed at that moment was not proved.

**A four-part number was tried first, as 0.9.0.1:** dpkg's order, `sort -V`, federation's version check, the deb, render and the docs suite all took it;
`tests/host/upgrade.sh` refuses a `FROM` that is not three parts. The owner chose not to have one (2.1.1.86). Nothing was pushed under that name.

**The tests can fail:** `tests/samba/linux_join.py` with 0.9.0's own `join-linux.sh` in place failed 4 of its new checks: the wait for a run going, the DC's
LDAP not answering (`removed (no longer in fabric-managed)`), and both orders of the race. With this release's script all passed. The race is made certain there:
a stand-in `ldapsearch` takes 3 s and fails, so a check overlaps the join's apply each time.

**The campaign waits for dpkg's lock before ub26-2 joins:** a machine just booted can have unattended-upgrades holding it, and a join does not wait for it. In a run on the
directory browser's branch that stopped the join, and the campaign's `join: 0` was `tail`'s status, so the campaign now stops if the join left no `managed.env`.

**The gate, 2026-11-05, before the first push:** every suite but the sandbox passed except webui: `tests/webui/test_devserver.py`, its first request refused
(`ConnectionRefusedError`). The same test alone, then the webui suite again, passed. **Likely cause, not proved:** its `serve()` waits at most 15 s and then goes on silently; five
starts measured on this host took 5.6, 5.9, 14.6, 10.4 and 14.4 s. The failed run did not keep the server's output, so it cannot say whether the server was still starting. These
passes do not clear it: it is an intermittent failure, left as it is here because this release's scope does not cover it.

**CI on aa13eae2:** `lint` and `package` passed; in `suites` every job passed but `kea (amd64)`: `tests/kea/run.py` line 122, Kea's check refusing "interface 'eth0' doesn't exist in the
system". The test names `eth0`, the check runs on the host's network, and that runner has none. The release does not touch Kea; `kea (arm64)` passed. Not rerun. The test's fix is on `dev/0.9.2` (its 2.3.1.53, with
every intermittent failure seen); this branch takes it only if the owner agrees.

**The joined campaign on aa13eae2** (build `0.9.1~git20261105141721.aa13eae2`; FROM_DEB=TO_DEB, so fabric is at this build from the start and the members join by its script), 2026-11-05: **136 passed, 0 failed**, the
floor's first setup clean. ub26-2 booted 14:19:26 and joined 14:26:47, over 5 minutes after boot, so its first check started beside its join's apply in the same second, and it removed nothing. One run cannot prove a
race: the proof is the deterministic checks above; this shows a member joined the way that can fail is not cut off, on real machines. Leaving out the floor, it has no upgrade across versions or `--refresh` (not in this release).

**The sandbox on aa13eae2, 2026-11-05:** 295 passed, 0 failed (`tests/sandbox/run.sh`). **The image-update test on it:** 63 passed, 0 failed (`tests/images/update.sh --box fabric-sandbox`). The campaign's own image step
passed on real VMs too.

**No image is published:** `source_hash --check` is clean, no image's sources changed.

**The owner, 2026-11-05:** this release is to be short-lived, effort going to 0.9.2: no extra fix here, test-only ones included. Apt testing and the test Pi were approved for it.

**Apt testing:** `package` run 42120170509 on eb5980af: `testing` serves `0.9.1~git20261105221715.eb5980a`. Nothing went to stable.

**The test Pi** (`tempuser@192.168.5.57`, Ubuntu 26.04), that build from `testing`; the production host never touched, Kea never installed:
- **Upgrade from the floor** (`FROM=0.8.0 TO_SUITE=testing`): 17 passed, 0 failed. **From 0.9.0** (`FROM=0.9.0`): 17 passed, 0 failed. **The host suite** on a fresh install (`APT_SUITE=testing`): 25 passed, 0 failed.
- **The rehearsal** (`tests/host/installer.py`): 44 passed, 0 failed, twice. Then as a user, by HTTPS trusting fabric's root alone: `http://192.168.5.57/certs/` 200 with the root; `fabric-ctl`, `info` and `sso` `.home.arpa` resolve at the Pi's DNS to its address; `https://info.home.arpa/certs/` 200; `https://fabric-ctl.home.arpa/` 303 to `/login`, 303 to `sso.home.arpa`, Keycloak's "Sign in to Fabric Installer Test" 200.
- **Left for the owner's install:** purged, `/opt/fabric` absent, no containers, `fabric.target` inactive, with only `fabricctl` from `testing`.
- **Two attempts that do not count, as they happened:** (1) the first `apt purge`, of the directory-browser build there, crashed: `cp: cannot stat '/opt/postgres/data/pgdata/postmaster.pid'` in the export, leaving fabric half removed; the two upgrades and the start of the host suite ran on that and failed; stopped, the uninstall finished by the command the purge printed, which discards the export (the wipe was approved), and all rerun. (2) The rehearsal's first run, 28 passed, 16 failed, `ssh.exe: cannot execute binary file`: the agent ran `dev/0.9.2`'s gate beside it, and WSL lost the entry that runs Windows programs, likely to `samba`'s systemd containers; rerun alone.

**A fault this release has and keeps (the owner: no code changes here). Its export can copy a running stack.** `fabricctl uninstall --export`, and so `apt purge`, runs `systemctl stop fabric.target fabric-agent`, ignores the result and copies at once. Measured on `dev/0.9.2`'s sandbox with this same code: the stop returned in under a second with Postgres running; stop and copy in one shell, 6 rounds of 6, `pg_controldata` on the copy said `in production`; in the export's real order, 2 rounds of 3. On an idle database those copies recovered when started, which promises little. The export's README says the stack was stopped. **0.9.0's and 0.9.1's exports should not be relied on as backups.** It is older than this release, and fixed on `dev/0.9.2` (its 2.3.1.53).

**The `deploy` suite was red at 4fac5be1, and that commit went in with it red; CI cannot see it.** `tests/deploy/apply_deployment_test.py` stopped at `record_build` (added in 356b0e26) writing to the test's
`/nonexistent`. How it got through: the full gate's output keeps only its last 8 lines, the agent read those and saw `kea FAIL`, fixed kea, then gated `docs lint` and `kea` alone; `deploy`'s row was above the cut. `deploy` runs its tests joined by `&&`, so the ones after it, this note's
new `pending_store_test.py` among them, had not run either. Both a stale test and a real, smaller fault: the folder cannot be missing on a real install (the apply reads its settings there first), but a full or read-only disk would fail the write **after all the apply's work**, reporting a finished apply as failed, and skipping the record that DNS is current. `record_build` now says so and goes on; `tests/deploy/running_build_test.py`.

**Suites `tests/run-all.sh` has and CI never ran (`suites.yml`'s header said every suite): `deploy` and `zones`.** `lint` runs in its own workflow and `sandbox` in its own job. On the owner's word of 2026-11-05 both are now in `suites.yml` on this branch, and `tests/docs/check_ci_suites.py` fails if one is left out again. `rc/0.9.1` keeps its list.

**0.9.1 stays short-lived (the owner, 2026-11-05):** `rc/0.9.1` takes no extra fix, test-only ones and `pending_store` included; they are this branch's.

**Run 26 of the joined campaign** (4fac5be1, build `0.9.2~git20261105183353.4fac5be1`, the floor to this build): **220 passed, 0 failed**, `FLOOR known cut-offs: 0`, the floor's first setup known. The floor did not lose the race this time: so 3 of 6. **Its `certs` step, the install-all fix on a real member:** the page's block fetched the root by HTTP and the script by HTTPS; a wrong fingerprint refused, naming what was served, nothing fetched or installed; the right one installed root and intermediate, each script by HTTPS, and curl then trusted fabric's server by the system's store; the clients checked afterwards. The certificates page, from its HTML on that build: the install-all block, the other installers by HTTPS, no `http://` link to an install script, nothing double-escaped. **The commits after it change `record_build`, the export's stop, tests, CI and notes;** the run was not repeated for them; the sandbox covers the export.

**The export copied a running stack (found on the test Pi 2026-11-05; proved on a sandbox; 0.9.0 and 0.9.1 have it).** `apt purge` on the Pi crashed: `cp: cannot stat '/opt/postgres/data/pgdata/postmaster.pid'`.
`export_install` does stop first, `systemctl stop fabric.target fabric-agent`, its result ignored. Measured with 4fac5be1's code: the stop returned rc 0 in under a second with Postgres still running. Stop and copy in one shell, six rounds out of six: `postgres=running`, `postmaster.pid` there, still running when the copy ended, and `pg_controldata` on the copy says `in production`. **In the export's real order** (`DIRS`, three rounds): Postgres was running when its copy began in two, those copies `in production`; in the third everything had stopped, `shut down`. On the Pi the same race ended in the crash.
- **Why the stop returns early (read from the units, and it fits the timing; not traced in systemd):** each service is `PartOf=` and `Before=fabric.target`, so the target's stop job ends first, the services' stops follow, and `systemctl` waits only for what it was named.
- **Restorable?** Each same-shell copy with a log read recovered and accepted connections (`not properly shut down; automatic recovery`). Tables were read back from three more, with `postgres --single`, as the image's own user: `user_entity` 2 people, `realm` `lan.test,master`, 100 public tables, the same as the fixed export's. **All on an idle database, so that proves little:** `cp` of a running Postgres is not atomic, and under writes a recovered copy is not promised; that case was not tried. The `keycloak` role says "not permitted to log in" because it is `rolcanlogin=false`, on the export's clean copy too: `keycloak_db` and `fabric_admin` log in (OpenBao's take-over, 2.1.7.4), so that was no sign of damage. The README the export writes said "while the stack was stopped, so databases are consistent", which was untrue for these. Other services' copies have the same window: `nginx`, `bind9`, `stepca`, `keycloak` come before Postgres in the order; only Postgres's state in its copy was measured.
- **A failed purge's advice lost the export:** it said to finish with `--no-export`, the only way it offered. It was followed on the Pi, as the wipe was approved. A second purge cannot redo the export while the stack is down ("OpenBao is not reachable"). It now gives both ways. The host's computer account is removed before the export runs, so a failed export has already taken that; not changed.
- **The fix:** `_stop_stack`: fabric's timers stopped, every unit named so `systemctl` waits for each, then Docker asked: a container of fabric's own folders still running after 90 s refuses with no copy and nothing uninstalled. A failed copy says so, with no traceback. `tests/setup/export_install_test.py`; the sandbox checks the export's Postgres has no `postmaster.pid`. **Proved on the sandbox at d440b047:** 295 passed, 0 failed; the uninstall's export: no `postmaster.pid`, `pg_controldata` says `shut down`, and the tables above read back from a copy of it. One export, by the real code: the deterministic test covers the refusals. The old stop, on the same box, three more rounds: `postgres=running`, `in production`. **A real fabric restored from the export** is the sandbox's own `fabricctl restore` check. **`rc/0.9.1` keeps the fault:** no code changes there, by the owner's word, so 0.9.1's exports should not be relied on.

**A gate beside the Pi broke the Pi's run (2026-11-05).** `rc/0.9.1`'s rehearsal failed at 28 passed, 16 failed with `ssh.exe: cannot execute binary file`. WSL reaches the Pi through Windows' `ssh.exe`, and WSL's `WSLInterop` entry had gone from `binfmt_misc`, while this branch's gate ran `samba`, whose `linux_join.py` boots containers with systemd as PID 1. **Likely, not proved:** their `systemd-binfmt` unregistered it, as the sandbox's did (2.3.1.13 finding 5, masked there only). Those images now mask it too. The entry was registered again and the rehearsal rerun alone: a gate and anything over `ssh.exe` do not run together.

**d440b047:** the full gate, every row read: all passed but `lint` (three long lines in the export fix), then, those fixed, `lint`, `consent` and `docs` passed. CI: `suites` 42 jobs passed, 0 failed, `deploy` and `zones` running for the first time, both architectures; `lint` and `package` passed. **Run 27 of the joined campaign** (build `0.9.2~git20261106025633.d440b047`): **220 passed, 0 failed**, `FLOOR known cut-offs: 1`, the floor's first setup known. So the floor lost the race in 4 of 7 runs; this build's script in none.

**The install-all fix (2.1.1.87, 1.5.1.8):** `tests/samba/join_steps.py` passed in full in real containers, its own first run with one check of the test's own wrong: `grep -c` counted "Verify return code: 0" twice on Debian
13, where TLS 1.3 printed it twice. Lab results: run 26, above.

## 2.3.1.55 The 0.9.5 release candidate: runs and findings

Each run as it happened, on `rc/0.9.5`: `dev/0.9.5` (with `dev/0.9.4` and the DNS filter work merged in, 2.1.1.96) merged into main (0.9.2)
with `--no-ff` as 9d57da30, whose tree is `dev/0.9.5`'s; main's only commit beyond it was pull request 29's merge, with no file changes.

- **The merges into `dev/0.9.5`:** `dev/0.9.4` as 5a0388f9, its wording moved to 0.9.5 by 2.1.1.96's mapping; CI on 1dfa567d passed `package`, `lint`,
  `images` and `suites`.
- **Images:** published by `images` run 344 from 9d57da30, signed, and pinned as `0.9.5-rc.344` (58247111), every one of fabric's seven images.
  `source_hash.py --check` passes, with no pending marks. `previous.yaml` moved to 0.9.2's pins (`0.9.2-rc.325`, 369499f8); no upstream image moved
  since 0.9.2, so those entries stayed. Gate on each: docs, lint 5 of 5, the dev preview's 436 checks, `source_hash`.
- **apt `testing`:** `package` run 858 from 2365d94f: `0.9.5~git20261108142108.2365d94`, amd64 and arm64.
- **Every local suite** (`tests/run-all.sh`, as root, 9d57da30, 2026-11-08): 22 of 23 passed. **`kea` failed one check, the test's own fault:** "Kea's
  check works from a process with a private /tmp" mounts a private `/tmp` and then imported `fabriclib` from the checkout by its path. The rc's
  clone was under `/tmp`, so the mount hid it: `ModuleNotFoundError`. CI's checkout is elsewhere, which is why it passed there. The test now starts in
  `src/` and imports through `/proc/self/cwd`, which keeps the folder the mount hides; `.` would not, as Python makes it absolute. The `kea` suite from a
  `/tmp` clone with the fix: all passed. The product is not touched.
- **The test Pi (`temp-ub`, Ubuntu 26.04 arm64, 2365d94f, from `testing`), 2026-11-08:**
  - **Upgrades:** from 0.8.0, 17 passed, 0 failed; from 0.9.2, 17 passed, 0 failed.
  - **The host suite,** unattended on a purged Pi: 27 passed, 0 failed. **The rehearsal,** interactive, Guided in the terminal then Advanced with
    every default: 44 passed, 0 failed.
  - **As a user would:** `fabricctl doctor` exit 0; the certificate page over HTTP 200. The page by HTTPS trusting fabric's root alone and the console's
    redirect **were not read:** the run's own script quoted `curl --cacert` wrongly (`option --cacert: is badly used here`); not the product.
  - **Memory kills:** none in the kernel's journal over the run.
  - **Left for the owner:** purged, `/opt/fabric` absent, no containers, `fabric.target` inactive, with only `fabricctl` from `testing`.
- **The sandbox, kept (54e4f95d):** 296 passed, 0 failed. **The image-update test on it:** 63 passed, 0 failed, each update and rollback
  from 0.9.2's pins.
- **CI on 54e4f95d:** `suites` 43 jobs passed (run 42497568952), `lint` and `package` passed. The `.deb` on apt `testing` is now from
  54e4f95d; it differs from the 2365d94f the Pi tested only in a test (the `kea` fix above) and notes.
- **Web-guided setup on the clean VM (3.1.5.13, 54e4f95d's package): 8 passed, 13 failed. A fault in every release since 0.8.0,
  found here first.** Setup's worker runs as `fabric-setup.service` (2.1.2.26). Three minutes into the install, during `[start]`
  as Keycloak started, the fresh VM's unattended-upgrades upgraded `libpcre2-8-0` and needrestart, installed by default on Ubuntu
  Server and run by unattended-upgrades in its automatic mode, restarted every service still holding the old library:
  `systemctl restart cron.service fabric-setup.service fwupd.service ... systemd-journald.service ...`
  (`/var/log/unattended-upgrades/unattended-upgrades-dpkg.log`, 17:03:26). The worker was stopped mid-step; its `systemctl`
  lost D-Bus as systemd re-executed (`Failed to wait for response: Connection reset by peer`); the run was recorded failed;
  the unit, restarted, found it so and stopped (`setup run ... is not the one recorded as running ...: nothing to do`). The
  other 12 failures follow from the install not finishing.
  - **Proved on the same VM:** a transient unit started as `launch_worker` starts it survives `systemctl daemon-reexec`,
    with `--collect` or without, and with its transient file removed. So the re-exec is not the cause; needrestart's
    restart is.
  - **Why the 21 of 21 of 2026-11-07 passed:** no library upgrade landed during that install. A machine just booted with
    updates pending, as most first installs are, can hit it in any step.
  - **What a person sees:** the install stops part way with a message that does not say why. `sudo fabricctl setup` again
    resumes from the first unfinished step (2.1.2.26), which is the way out until it is fixed; not tried on this VM.
  - **Maybe also 2.3.1.46** (the Pi's console saying setup had ended while it ran on): not shown; that unit kept running.
  - **Not fixed in this rc, by the owner's choice (2026-11-08):** both fixes, the needrestart settings file and the worker
    carrying on after a restart, in 0.10.0 (2.1.2.28); 0.9.5 ships with it as a known issue. The VMs were put back to `clean`.
- **Not run, by the owner's choice (2026-11-08):** the two HTTPS reads the Pi's script missed, and the Pi's page-speed run
  (2.3.8.9.5); both wait for 0.10.0's release candidate.
- **0.9.5's release notes,** in the pull request and here:
  - The console is faster: a read cache sized to the host (1.8.9), on the sandbox's numbers; the test Pi's run is owed (2.3.8.9.5).
  - `sudo fabricctl setup` offers Guided in a browser on another machine (1.3.6), with a one-time code and the certificate's
    fingerprint to compare; `--terminal` keeps setup in the terminal.
  - The DNS filter's query log leaves out fabric's own lookups (1.12.3.16), and its tab's changes wait for one Apply
    (1.12.3.17).
  - Upgrades from 0.8.0 and from 0.9.2 passed on the test Pi.
  - **Known issue:** on a machine whose own automatic updates run during setup (a fresh Ubuntu Server, often), needrestart can
    restart setup's service and setup stops part way, saying the run is "not the one recorded as running". Run
    `sudo fabricctl setup` again: it carries on from the first unfinished step. Present since 0.8.0; fixed in 0.10.0 (2.1.2.28).
  - **Known issue (found 2026-11-08):** rotating the vault key (`fabricctl vault rotate`, or the console) while an
    unlock method stops answering can leave OpenBao down after the rotation's restart. Only a person starts a rotation;
    starts and reboots are not affected. Do not rotate the vault key on 0.9.5 unless every unlock method answers its
    test. To be fixed in 0.10.0 (2.3.1.56).

## 2.3.1.56 The build VM's first full run on Ubuntu 26.04: four suites, causes in the tests (2026-11-08)

Every local suite (`tests/run-all.sh`) on the build VM `g23-vm-dev-1` (2.1.1.99; Ubuntu 26.04.1, Python 3.14.4, OpenSSL
3.5.5, dig 9.20.24), as root in a fresh clone of `dev/0.10.0-devhost` at b95d3e9f, 00:39 to 01:39: 19 suites passed,
4 failed. Each passed in CI (Ubuntu 24.04) and on WSL. Each cause was shown on the VM, and each lies in a test, not in
fabric.

| Suite | What happened | Cause, shown how | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| consent (`tests/setup/site/flow_test.py`) | `CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED: CA cert does not include key usage extension` | Python 3.13+ verifies strictly by default (`VERIFY_X509_STRICT`) and refuses a CA without keyUsage. The test's own root, made by `openssl req -x509`, has none. Shown on the VM with a root and a leaf: refused without keyUsage, accepted with it. fabric's root has it (`init_pki`'s template, `certSign`, `crlSign`) | The setup site's test roots (`flow_test`, `browser_test`, `check_console_test`) carry keyUsage |
| resolver (`tests/resolver/run.py`) | DoT with `dig +tls-ca=<file>`: "TLS context cannot be created" | Ubuntu 26.04 confines `dig` with AppArmor (`/etc/apparmor.d/dig`) to `$HOME` and the system's certificates; the CA in the test's folder under /tmp was refused (`apparmor="DENIED" ... profile="dig"` in the kernel log), whatever the certificate. Not fabric's: doctor's own DoT client passed | dig is given a copy of the CA in a folder under `$HOME`, removed after |
| kea (`tests/kea/scan.py`) | Kea refused the file: "interface 'eth0' doesn't exist in the system" | The case of 2.3.1.53 in the scan's suite, which calls Kea's real check itself: the build VM's cards are `lab` and `wan` | `on_host()` moved to `tests/common/kea_on_host.py`, used by both Kea suites |
| openbao (`tests/openbao/run.py`) | The rotation's restart: "not unsealed after restart" | **Not known yet.** The test said no more than that, and the container was gone by the next run. A rerun of this suite alone stopped earlier: a KMIP server left by the first run still held the port, and the rerun talked to it (`self-signed certificate in certificate chain`). That server ignores SIGTERM and SIGINT here; only SIGKILL stopped it, so the old `terminate()` likely always left one | The restart's failure says the health, the status and the container's last log lines; a port in use is refused; the KMIP server is stopped (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL) however the run ends, and a check proves its port free |

The openbao row stays open until its rerun says why, or passes.

**Rerun of the four, be7de022, 01:51 to 01:57:** consent passed. Three still failed:
- **resolver:** my fix removed the CA copy, then doctor's DoT check still used it (`FileNotFoundError`). dig now gets its own copy; the CA itself stays.
- **kea:** `tests/kea/reload.py` reaches Kea's real check inside `deploy_kea` with `eth0`. `tests/common/kea_on_host.py`'s `check_on_host()` now puts the on-host check where fabric's code calls it, for `run.py` and `reload.py` alike.
- **openbao:** the rotation's restart failed again, and said why. The seal wanted the previous key's file (`/openbao/seal/fabric-4.key`: "no such file"), and only one key file was in RAM: `unlock_vault` wrote the new key (fabric-5) but not the previous one. The KMIP server's log shows every request of the rotation served. Why the previous key was not obtained is not known yet: the restart's failure now also lists the key files in RAM and, for each key, whether it was obtained and each method's reason if not.

**Rerun of the three, 0ae34c6d, 02:01 to 02:07:** resolver and kea passed. openbao failed at the same rotation, and
this time said why: every request to the KMIP device timed out, for both keys ("The read operation timed out").

- **The device's side, a fault of the test's server:** PyKMIP's server (python3-pykmip 0.10.0-10, the test's
  stand-in for an HSM) ends a session only on its own `ConnectionClosed`. Read in its `session.py` on the VM: any
  other exception is logged and the loop runs again. Under Python 3.14 a client that had gone away gave
  `BrokenPipeError`, so that session's thread looped without end, writing the same error into its rotating log, and
  the server, its sessions being threads, stopped answering. Not fabric's: a real KMIP device is not PyKMIP. On
  24.04 (CI, WSL) the suite passes.
- **fabric's side, a real fault, small:** when the device stopped answering during a rotation, `rotate_vault_key`
  went on. `unlock_vault` skips a key it cannot obtain without saying so (`if prev:`). The new key was written, the
  previous was not, and OpenBao was restarted with a seal that needs both, so it stayed down. A device that stops
  answering mid-rotation, on any host, would do the same. Present in 0.9.5: the vault code is the same.
- **The owner (2026-11-08): fixed in 0.10.0, not urgent; a known-issue line for 0.9.5** (2.3.1.55).
- **To do in 0.10.0:** (1) the rotation restarts OpenBao only once every key its seal names is in RAM; otherwise it
  stops before the restart, says which method failed, and leaves OpenBao running as it was, the rotation resumable;
  with a test where the device stops answering mid-rotation. (2) The test's KMIP server made to work on 26.04
  (PyKMIP's loop), so the suite passes on the build VM.
- **The owner (2026-11-08, "1. b"): both in 0.10.0.** Until then the openbao suite's KMIP step is accepted as failing on
  the build VM; for 2.1.1.99's "every suite green" before WSL goes, it is that suite's one known failure.

**The web-guided clean-VM run (3.1.5.13) on the build VM, 2026-11-10, c260b68e, 01:00 to 01:07: failed, setup cut
off part way.** The first run of it from the build VM; the package built there (`0.9.5~git20261110010019.c260b68e`),
the lab key from the forwarded agent. The browser reached the plan and the install. Then, on `g23-vm-ub24-1` fresh
from `clean`, Ubuntu's own `apt-daily-upgrade` started at 01:03:45, upgraded systemd, and re-executed it at 01:04:09
("Reexecuting requested from client ... (unit apt-daily-upgrade.service)"). Setup's terminal said "D-Bus connection
terminated", systemd could no longer open `/run/systemd/transient/fabric-setup.service`, and the run ended with "not
the one recorded as running": 0.9.5's known issue (2.3.1.55), by a second trigger. The first was needrestart restarting
the unit; this one is systemd itself re-executed, with the transient unit lost. Every later check failed from it.
2.1.2.28's part (2), the worker carrying on after any restart, is meant to cover it; its test as written upgrades a
library the worker holds, not systemd. The two VMs were put back to `clean`.
